# Decisions

Every decision still in force, one line each. Short entries are in `decisions/`: find one with `grep -n "^## D120 " decisions/*.md`. Trimmed 2026-11-07: full original text of every entry, including those marked archived here, is in [`decisions/archive/`](decisions/archive/) (D136).

- **Changing a decision:** edit its entry and note the date; don't silently contradict it elsewhere. Add a "→" note here if it changes an older one.
- **Adding one:** the short entry goes at the end of the newest file in `decisions/`, plus one line here, in the same turn. D numbers are never reused or renumbered.
- **"→ archived"** means the entry is no longer in force and its live entry was removed; the reason says why. **D155 wins over every earlier decision.**
- **Open questions** are in [`questions.md`](questions.md); answered ones in [`decisions/answered-questions.md`](decisions/answered-questions.md).

## D1–D24: foundation and the brief

[`decisions/1-foundation.md`](decisions/1-foundation.md)

- D1 · Built for the owner's own use first: company and personal projects alike
- D2 · Tickets: Jira, through Atlassian's MCP (attachments and API-token runs per D73)
- D3 · PRs on GitHub; CI defaults to GitHub Actions, behind adapters (D24)
- D4 · Target codebase shape: monorepo, many .csproj, incremental per-PR → widened by D127, D154
- D5 · Build from the ground up, contracts first; the benchmark breaks ties
- D6 · Benchmark: public repos (nopCommerce, eShop, CleanArchitecture, Kavita, bitwarden, Sonarr)
- D7 · Source of truth: files in the repo, with a SQLite index rebuilt from them
- D8 · Portability through versioned schemas and one store interface
- D9 · Each product is a CLI; the plugin is a thin layer over them
- D10 · Links live in a link file, target symbol ids, are checked in CI; FSD → reference → code → requirement hop replaced by D156 P4
- D11 · A living spec owned by BA/PM: stable ids, spec-only PRs, states, hash-checked verification → AI-written requirements replaced by D156 P4
- D12 · Specs live in the codebase; BA/PM edit them through a dashboard that writes spec-only PRs
- D13 · Spec sources and provenance labels → archived: replaced by D52
- D14 · One full audit upfront → archived: replaced by D99
- D15 · Source documents: Word, PDF, Excel, converted by code, every original kept; a person settles clashes → latest-wins reversed by D128
- D16 · Original documents live in a separate archive repo, cited by name, path and hash
- D17 · Our own code-graph extractor (C#, TypeScript, Python) with pluggable link detectors; blind spots reported
- D18 · The AI proposes, code checks; if code can't check it, a person approves → strengthened by D155
- D19 · Build order: phases 0–8; the rebuild order within 0–3 is D156's
- D20 · Every phase ends with a report; contract changes mark dependents `needs review`
- D21 · Telemetry: cost, quality, speed and failures per run, local, no content by default
- D22 · Grill each phase when it starts, not all upfront
- D23 · One `stangent` CLI is the store's only door; its core is C# (reconfirmed by D110)
- D24 · Constraints, CI and code host behind adapters, a per-repo switch that turns off every AI step

## D25–D49: the code graph and the agent-fitness pilot

[`decisions/2-code-graph.md`](decisions/2-code-graph.md)

- D25 · The code graph is rebuilt from code, never committed; CI builds it per merge to main
- D26 · What the code graph holds: method-level points, API shapes, config keys; lines labelled ✅ 🟡 📜 ❓
- D27 · Impact answers: summary first, nearest first, details on request, ❓ always listed
- D28 · How impact analysis is scored: Tests 1–4 from git history, per label, against baselines
- D29 · Phase 1 is split into 1a, 1b, 1c → archived: obsolete: an order of work that's finished (1a–1c built and reported)
- D30 · The TypeScript extractor runs in Node, inside the stangent tool
- D31 · What the frontend map holds: TS compiler, Angular templates, routes as pages, frontend → backend calls
- D32 · How frontend links are proven: frontend Test 4 and a committed openapi.json
- D33 · Test 2 is hardened before 📜 is judged: each fix once, full history, bundles as a slice
- D34 · 📜 links files, not members (members maybe later)
- D35 · 📜 evidence is a count over each file's recent commits (window 10, minShared 3)
- D36 · 📜 counts changes on main's own line
- D37 · 📜 noise filters: huge commits, bots, formatting sweeps, reverts
- D38 · 📜 follows renames and moves; not on blobless clones
- D39 · 📜 lines live in the code graph (`changes-with`, `meta.history`)
- D40 · 📜 in impact answers: its own group, last, capped, never walked
- D41 · 📜 is recomputed in full, not incrementally
- D42 · 📜 uses whatever history the clone has; CI's graph carries it
- D43 · 📜 is tuned on some repos and judged on others; a 30-fix floor
- D44 · 📜 passed its gate and ships on by default; a retune must pass all of Gate 2
- D45 · The graph's Phase 4 jobs (split, scope, check) are checked before Phase 4; context scoping still owed
- D46 · A split group "holds up" if it compiles alone, then passes the unit tests
- D47 · Split cases: recent main-line PRs from bitwarden/server and Sonarr; the rules any later split test uses
- D48 · Splits come from the after-change graph, joined within 2 steps (✅ + 🟡)
- D49 · The split check's done-when; safe failed, so Phase 4 builds parallel runs together or runs in sequence

[`decisions/3-reference-and-fsd.md`](decisions/3-reference-and-fsd.md)

- D50 · stangent is a standalone rebuild, modelled on the company's pipelines
- D51 · The spec is built from the agreed FSD and the code together
- D52 · Two documents, linked: the FSD and a reference generated from code → outside doc as truth reversed by D128; D99 layer replaced by D156 P4
- D53 · Links: code finds candidates, AI judges, code checks, a person approves → AI quoting replaced by D156 P3; AI finder fallback by D156 P2
- D54 · Reference pages: ids from the graph, every statement cited → page split replaced by D154; writer freedom by D156 P1
- D55 · The reference catches up per change: detected on each PR, regenerated on merge
- D56 · Links are scored on a Sonarr FSD with planted discrepancies → cyberbullying note reversed by D127
- D57 · The links' done-when: finds what's planted, not too noisy, grounded, repeatable
- D58 · Decisions are fields in a committed YAML file; the website later edits the same file → requirement tick boxes replaced by D156 P4
- D59 · Phase 2 is split: 2a reference from code, then 2b FSD and discrepancies → archived: obsolete: an order of work; 2a and 2b are both built and scored
- D60 · AI steps run in Claude Code; the CLI prepares their input and checks their output → writer freedom replaced by D156 P1
- D61 · Verification by a separate agent with a fresh context, capped at one check and one fix round; per layer again by D158
- D62 · Phase 2 applies D61: a fresh verifier for pages and "agree" links → one rejection reversed by D119; agree checker under review (D156 P8)
- D63 · Hubs are included, not walked into; slice limits are settings → endpoint split and endpoint pages replaced by D154
- D64 · The split check is judged on bitwarden's first run; no rerun; the cutter fix stays in the 1d harness
- D65 · The graph's blind spots and a smarter split rule are Phase 4 work → blind spots flows need moved to the graph check by D159
- D66 · Sonnet writes and verifies pages, in single-turn calls; pages are made as needed
- D67 · Phase 2's hand checks are done by a fresh agent, not the owner
- D68 · Discrepancy findings ship off by default (D57's rule, applied)

## D69–D98: tickets

[`decisions/4-tickets.md`](decisions/4-tickets.md)

- D69 · Phase 3's bundle is read by a developer's Claude Code first; Phase 4 reads the same contract
- D70 · A ticket's starting points: code's signals, and the developer's edits → AI page picks replaced by D101
- D71 · The bundle carries pages and pointers, not source code, under a size cap → cut order replaced by D101
- D72 · Attachments: code converts text and PDF, keeps images as files, lists the rest
- D73 · The stangent CLI reaches Jira as an MCP client, behind the issue-tracker adapter
- D74 · Write-back: drafts as files; a person reads and posts them → AI wording replaced by D156 P7
- D75 · A bug is proven before it's fixed, in Phase 3; "no proof yet" says why (D106); advice only (D109)
- D76 · Bug proof uses unit tests and Playwright
- D77 · Phase 3 is proven on Sonarr issues copied into the owner's Jira, read through the MCP; saved results replayed
- D78 · The frozen set: 40 bugs and 10 not-bugs (now the tuning set, D100)
- D79 · Phase 3's done-when: right place, real proof, grounded, repeatable
- D80 · "code-wrong" decisions don't become tickets in Phase 3
- D81 · The run's cost: reproduction on 30 bugs, each attempt capped ($1, 30 turns)
- D82 · Jira through Atlassian's MCP v2 (`/v2/mcp`), with Jira-only scopes and secrets in the OS keychain
- D83 · A ticket's parts can be marked "outside the code", each tagged by who said so; proof skipped only if nothing points into code
- D84 · Drafts: framing lines need no claim, writers never talk about their input, and the run drafts after proof
- D85 · A Jira read that gets no answer is tried again, the last time on a new session
- D86 · Browser proof: the app runs while the AI works; it copies a related Playwright test, or looks with a browser
- D87 · Starting points through a page index built by code; ranking orders pages, it never hides one (under review, D156 P6)
- D88 · Impact answers say what they left out; no forward walk
- D89 · Our own setup never counts against the AI
- D90 · A test runner that can't run is the setup's failure; a mock's own check is an assertion
- D91 · A setup failure gets a plain reason and advice; stangent never installs anything itself
- D92 · Must-pass fixes after the Sonarr run: drafts that stay sourced, a keychain that survives parallel runs
- D93 · A version given in the environment counts as the version
- D94 · The generality batch: nothing in stangent assumes the benchmark repos
- D95 · A proof says which ticket sentence its failure shows; a developer confirms the match
- D96 · A failing test shows the code differs from what the reporter expects; a person makes it a bug → "bug, per R-…" replaced by D156 P4/P5
- D97 · A draft leaves out a sentence no claim backs, instead of failing
- D98 · A draft is a list of parts, each one sentence with its claims → writer composing replaced by D156 P7

[`decisions/5-spec-and-finder.md`](decisions/5-spec-and-finder.md)

- D99 · The FSD gets a checked layer: word-for-word quotes, stable ids, people approve → AI requirements and ticket match replaced by D156 P4/P5 → FSD journeys by code (D160)
- D100 · Held-out bugs come from bitwarden/server; Sonarr's 40 are the tuning set
- D101 · A coding agent finds the ticket's files; code checks them; the graph is the checker and the map (under review, D156 P6)
- D102 · A ticket Jira sends as HTML reads as the same Markdown as any other
- D103 · A code quote may be one whole line, even with no space
- D104 · The proof's turn cap counts model rounds, what `--max-turns` limits
- D105 · The on-fix check says what kind of failure it was; D79's bar stays
- D106 · "Not reproduced" stops saying "don't fix" and says why
- D107 · The finder's reason is a guess: labelled for people, hidden from the proof
- D108 · `ticket post` refuses a draft whose inputs changed
- D109 · Phase 3 closed on what we have; the proof ships as advice only; its re-run is owed before Phase 4
- D110 · The core stays C#; no port to Python
- D111 · Stangent imports PDF and Excel as well as Word, by code only, listing what can't come in exactly

## D112–D166: the overhaul; new decisions go here

[`decisions/6-overhaul.md`](decisions/6-overhaul.md)

- D112 · The overhaul's order (finished; D156 sets the new one); driver tests stay xUnit with fakes on the path
- D113 · The foundation's shapes: evidence, permanent ids, the client-data check
- D114 · One module for state changes: which things are lifecycles, and where history lives
- D115 · Line citations and Playwright as evidence: the read paths, and lines kept out of the walk
- D116 · Word stops on tracked changes, and a code check compares every word
- D117 · Sections get a permanent id beside their number, and only exact signals match across versions
- D118 · What counts as the same statement, where line citations live, and a severity scale a person sets
- D119 · A second agent tries to disprove each finding (`fsd.disprove`, off); refutations wait for a person → under review (D156 P8)
- D120 · Build first, then test one component at a time → "a switch for every component" narrowed by D153 → pause ended by D158
- D121 · Switches, structured import problems and one set of import steps
- D122 · Verdicts, labels, first-import approval, `recheck` and `gone`
- D123 · Excel as a main document, files inside a document, attachments in the checks and `contentHash` → one line reversed by D126
- D124 · Table facts and the mapping file, switched off → finer verdicts, grouping and asking cut by D156 P8
- D125 · What we take from the workflow comparison, in bulk
- D126 · Batch 1 of §9: a hard cost stop, checks around a run, FSD import fixes
- D127 · Python joins the code graph (extractor, Flask routes, `root-screens`, `route-render`)
- D128 · The parsed spec is the clean source of truth; new files come in as proposals a BA or PM checks → section level built by D150
- D129 · Journeys also come from end-to-end tests, read by code (built by D149, `test-journeys` off) → FSD journeys by code (D160)
- D130 · A bundle stores only what's unique; code assembles the rest
- D131 · Batch 2 of §9: bundles store what's unique, corrections stick, pages when needed
- D132 · Security review is its own checked step in Phase 6, apart from the FSD comparison
- D133 · Every link reaches both the page and the code lines, filled in by code when read
- D134 · The file an item sits in is its status; the marks go
- D135 · Twelve rules for all work live in `CLAUDE.md`, and the full entry wins
- D136 · `decisions.md` is a short index; full entries live in `decisions/`, open questions in `questions.md` → changed by D157 (an archive now holds the full text)
- D137 · "Is it true?" is Stangent's job; "which side is right?" is the PM's
- D138 · The cut rule: a component that fails a bar gets one fix attempt, then it's removed
- D139 · No cap on new items (D153 limits switches, not items)
- D140 · A section's candidates can reach shared components through a screen's (`shared-through`, off) → archived: to be replaced by D154 if its trial wins; the switch stays built until D156's cut step
- D141 · An AI flag for findings that touch security → archived: to be cut by D156 P8 (an unproven AI switch); `security-flag` stays built until the cut step, its rules in the archive
- D142 · Readable section files go into git with D128, as a generated view; `document.json` stays the record → built by D150
- D143 · A section is cut into claims by code, with a words check; ids came in D145; switch folds into `claim-links` (D156 P3)
- D144 · Workflow files sit on top of pages, written by code → archived: pages give way to D154's flows if its trial wins; `workflow-files` stays built until D156's cut step
- D145 · Claims get permanent ids, a judge names ids and copies no words, a link's id comes from ids; on by D156 P3
- D146 · A repeated step groups despite its number, a page-cut step is one claim; `disprove-callers` under review (D156 P8)
- D147 · Generated files: a marker line, same bytes, never read back, a rebuild removes only its own files → workflow files replaced by D154
- D148 · A security flag built from the disprove step (`security-flag`, off) → archived: to be cut by D156 P8; built until the cut step, its rules in the archive
- D149 · Journeys parsed from end-to-end tests compare nothing (`test-journeys`, off); builds D129's J.1
- D150 · D128 at section level: a person approves or rejects each changed section, promote applies only what was approved (`section-approval`, off)
- D151 · A page gap means a quoted code line settles it, and waits for a person (`page-gap-strict`, off); under review (D156 P8)
- D152 · `trace findings`: the BA's queue as one readable file, strongest evidence first; no switch
- D153 · No new switches until the built ones are tested or removed
- D154 · The reference layer becomes areas → flows laid out by code; a person confirms areas once; any stack; trial on one Sonarr area first
- D155 · Deterministic first: code decides what it can, an AI only words or picks among what code laid out; wins over earlier decisions
- D156 · D155's eight changes approved (P1–P8); the rebuild goes ground up: foundation, graph, areas and flows, pages, linking, tickets, cuts
- D157 · The decision log is trimmed: short live entries for what's in force, every original word for word in `decisions/archive/`
- D158 · The fresh-check pause ends: each layer gets a narrow fresh check before the next is built on it
- D159 · The blind spots flows need (screens incl. Razor views, their handlers, what they call) are fixed in the graph check, not Phase 4
- D160 · FSD journeys are built by code from a use case's numbered steps, with no AI, and compared step by step with flows
- D161 · Every AI run's model and effort are pinned in `stangent.yaml` and recorded; a run refuses without them; a three-run repeat check compares answers by code
- D162 · Every AI step runs through the batch drivers; the plugin's in-session subagent route goes
- D163 · The code graph reads what flows need, checked on Sonarr and Kavita; `code-graph` v5 (G1–G8 fixed)
- D164 · The framework's own answer first: tables from EF's snapshot; every build cross-checks endpoints with OpenAPI and tables with the snapshot
- D165 · Razor views are read from the Razor compiler's own output (forms → endpoints, buttons, `View()` → view); an app's own access filters and plain scripts are read (nopCommerce N1–N5)
- D166 · The graph's guesses and blind spots are checked after the first flows exist, only those a flow uses; blind spots sorted by cause; runtime evidence opt-in, apart from the graph
- D167 · The fresh check's T1–T12 fixed (forms POST, button targets, partials' forms, base-class view helpers, themes, global and evidence-marked access filters, script globals and handlers)
- D168 · The T1–T12 check's V1–V5 fixed (MVC's lookup by the page's controller, cancelled submits, inherited button methods, 4xx refusals, rule modifiers)
- D169 · A submit button a script writes submits the form around it, as a guess (W1)

- D170 · Layer 3 builds flows first (a screen change stops a flow and links to that screen's load flow); areas are grouped from the flows afterwards; the first bar is on flows
- D171 · A flow starts at a click or submit (one flow per handler), a screen opening, or a system job; the graph first gains "runs on load" and "is a job" facts (layer 2, fresh-checked)
- D172 · A flow is a tree walked forward; a branch ends at the database, an outside call, a message sent (handlers are linked flows, never through the bus), another screen, a hub, a ❓ or 8 real calls
- D173 · Every out-of-bounds end is a link to another flow (handlers, jobs "runs later", screens, "continues in"), explained by a code-made sentence with its proof line; the graph gains "queues" lines
- D174 · A flow step carries the condition it sits under, quoted from the code; early exits (redirect or error returns, throws) end a branch; validation rules are a "checks" step; facts added in layer 2
- D175 · A URL match is ✅ when its text is constant and exactly one endpoint fits (✅ = proved by code); other guesses are labelled per step with their reason, listed at the top of the flow
- D176 · Layer 3 is code only (the paid run moves to layer 5): fixed rules for step order, loops, names, load effects, early exits, display-only parts; the same commit builds the same flows byte for byte
- D177 · Layer 3's bar, frozen first: 15 flows on Sonarr, Kavita, nopCommerce; key by a fresh agent from source alone, three approved by the owner; no wrong ✅, ≥95% found, every 🟡/❓ explained, byte-for-byte rebuilds, no silent skips
- D178 · Areas by a fixed folder rule (screen's home folder; shared folders never a home; jobs by who links to them; split over 40 flows; never merged by code); ties listed for a person → areas are the app's parts as its users name them; the folder rule is the fallback (D220)
- D179 · Every flow shows name, start, actor (fixed rule from guards and access), inputs, checks, steps, ends, scenarios (main path plus one per early exit), links, guesses, settings; all by code
- D180 · Flows and areas committed under `specs/` (`flows/<id>.json|.md`, `index.json`, `areas.yaml` by folders with ties); `stangent flows build|show|check`, `areas propose|check`; contracts `flows` v1, `areas` v1 → areas hold start flows only (D218); `areas.yaml` by menu items and folders (D225)
- D181 · Layer 2's rules for load (`on-load`, effect points), jobs (`is-job`, `jobInterfaces`), queues (a command made in a call's argument → its handler), conditions and exits (quoted, D176's list), and the ✅ URL rule (`http-match` reasons)
- D182 · The fresh check's Y1 (on-load for MVC pages with a runtime view), Y2 (a job requeuing itself keeps its line) and X1 (guard clauses as `unless` conditions) fixed
- D183 · The frozen key's format: one JSON per flow under `benchmark/expected/flows/<repo>/`, every item a code line (`at` + exact `code`), steps a tree by `from`; written from git-tracked source only, checked by code
- D184 · Layer 3's walk: `call-site` facts (each call's line), a hook's own functions as points, `filterType` on access filters; filters, `OnActionExecuting` and route guards walked first; a hub reading a table ends `database`; handler flows; the flows scorer's matching rules
- D185 · Ruling R1: a key end on a call Stangent walks into counts when Stangent's steps below it end the same way (outside call, message; database only on the key's table); a key end that only says it didn't follow the call leaves the count where Stangent walks on (amended); the key isn't edited
- D186 · Ruling R4: a returned call to an app's own helper that sets a 4xx/5xx status or returns only listed results is an early exit (D176's list grows by that rule)
- D187 · Ruling R5: a filter setting `context.Result` to a listed result (or a D186 helper's) is an early exit, changing D181
- D188 · Ruling R6: a C# call into a package (not `System.*`, EF Core or logging) is a `call-site` with no target; flows show it as a step ending "a package's code, not walked"
- D189 · Shared flows: each hub and depth stop a flow continues into is its own flow (kind `shared`, walked once, transitively), named by the link's `flow`; the scorer follows them; a field or property read is no step
- D190 · A frontend URL built from parameters takes each value the repo's own callers pass (🟡, with the point that gave it); a flow keeps the values its own code gave
- D191 · A view's onclick walks into the script function it calls, the route placed where the script sends to that argument; a top-level object's functions are points; jQuery's callbacks are calls; D190 applies to every HTTP call style
- D192 · Navigation by code: a returned Angular redirect, or a navigation the function returns straight after, is an exit; any other navigation is a step ending `screen`; error callbacks are `catch` conditions
- D193 · Requests made another way: Angular's `httpResource` URLs and an image's `src` are GET calls; the URL reader takes every return of a function; a component's field initialisers are walked with it
- D194 · Rulings R8 (a key item quoting no code, like `};`, leaves the count) and R9 (R1 credits `screen` ends too); the key isn't edited
- D195 · Ruling R10: a key exit's condition counts when it's on the exit or on a step on the way to it; the key isn't edited
- D196 · A hub whose own code reads or saves a table keeps its `database` end and continues into its shared flow too
- D197 · Ruling R11: a key `hub` end counts on a step that runs that hub and ends `database` (D172 names its table first); the key isn't edited
- D198 · An outside HTTP call with a runtime URL ends `outside-call` (still ❓ where); each leaving branch of an `if … else if` chain is an `unless` guard
- D199 · A query's run reads its source's table (repository `IQueryable<T>`, `DbSet<T>`); `SmtpClient` calls are outside calls; returned MVC `View`/`Redirect…` end `screen`; a method calling itself is a call site
- D200 · ASP.NET Identity's `UserManager`/`SignInManager`/`RoleManager` async calls end `database` (🟡, the Identity user store); `userManager.Users` queries read its table
- D201 · Step 4's findings fixed: a kept callback isn't walked where it's handed over (a listener's is 🟡); empty `src`; resolver files; `base.` calls; the bus stops at handler interfaces; script-global reason; endpoint names; navigation conditions; one read per table; guards in blocks; `HttpClient` runtime URLs
- D202 · The short re-check's two callback shapes, fixed generally: a function written as an object's property (`{ readFunc: (v) => … }`) is stored, not walked where it's written, unless the object goes straight to a library call that runs it (`subscribe({ next })`, `$.ajax({ success })`); a callback on a stream (`.subscribe(…)`, an operator in `.pipe(…)`) runs now only when the stream starts with a request (an `HttpClient` call, a `forkJoin`/`combineLatest` of them, or the repo's function returning one), any other stream (form changes, subjects, signals, route data) is 🟡 "runs when the stream sends a value". Cost: a stored function handed later to the repo's own code that calls it at once is missed (rare); a stream we can't trace to a request is 🟡 even when it is one
- D203 · An `if` whose branch only reports a problem and leaves is a check (a `rejects` fact in layer 2, listed for every method the flow walks), with its first string literal as the message. The branch ends in `throw new X(…)` (not `Argument…Exception`), a `return` of an error result (`BadRequest`, `NotFound`, a 4xx status, an object with `success = false`), or a `return` after `….Add…(…)` calls; before that, only `Add…` calls and local declarations. Guards that only redirect, challenge or return stay exits; an `if` that adds an error and carries on (`ModelState.AddModelError` with no return) isn't one. C# only for now. Owner: "Yes, list them" (2026-11-11). Cost: a line can show as both a condition and a check; a guard reporting any other way (a notification, a logger) isn't caught; the system reporting its own failure (a download client's error, a missing setting) is listed too: in a random 12, 10 were the input's and 2 the system's
- D204 · A redirect or navigation links to the flow of the screen it opens (`screen` link, D170). Server side, layer 2 keeps a redirect's target from constant values (the route name, the controller, action and area, the area defaulting to the redirecting controller's `[Area]`, or the URL); the route's controller, action and area, then the action serving GET in that area (the redirecting code's own project first), give the screen. Frontend side, a constant path (quoted, a template, parts joined with `+`, or an array of them) fits the pages of the navigating code's own frontend whose route matches, `:param` fitting any one segment; an Angular router call reads it from the root unless it passes `relativeTo` (the extractor's `root`), anything else needs a leading slash. One screen: ✅; several: a 🟡 link to each; none (made at runtime, relative, or no page fits): one 🟡 link to no flow, saying where it goes; a screen whose opening isn't a flow says why (the skipped list). Owner: "Yes, L1 next" (2026-11-11). Cost: a redirect inside a called method links where it's written, not at the call; a URL handed back for a page script to follow (`Url.RouteUrl` in a JSON answer) isn't a redirect, so it isn't linked; Angular `redirectTo` routes aren't followed (`/login` → `/registration/login` fits nothing)
- D205 · Work saved for a job to pick up later links `runs-later` to that job (🟡). A table is a job's work list when one of its words (split at capitals, `_`, `-`) is queue, queued, pending or outbox and the job reads it before it saves anything (walk order, shared flows taken in place); a save to it in any other flow (not the job, not shared code the job reaches; not a delete, remove, clear, purge or truncate call) links to the job, on the call that entered the method making the save or continuing into the shared code that makes it. Asked as "a write to a table a job reads": that linked nearly every save in nopCommerce (jobs read Customer, Language, Currency…); "read and written back by the job" still caught Series, Order, Customer; "the job's first read" missed RSS sync, which reads indexer settings before its pending releases. Owner: "do em all" (2026-11-11). Cost: a queue table named otherwise (a `Jobs` or `Messages` table) isn't found; a table merely named Pending… that a job reads first is taken as a queue; the Sonarr key's `RefreshSeriesCommand` link sits in `SeriesAddedHandler`'s own flow (it was already built there), not in add-series, which only links `message` to the handler
- D206 · A class an attribute registers for an event handles it (`[CheckOn(typeof(SeriesUpdatedEvent))]` on a health check): a class carrying an attribute whose argument is `typeof(E)` is a handler of E when E is an event the configured message system carries (some handler takes E, or a type E derives from or implements: Sonarr's `HealthCheckService` takes every `IEvent`); what runs is the one method it overrides from an abstract one, else its one public method (several: nothing). It's a `handle` fact like an interface handler's, so it gets a handler flow, message links and impact answers. A message is sent whether or not its class writes a constructor (an implicit one isn't on the map; the walk used to drop those sends). Owner: "do em all" (2026-11-11). Cost: a generic event made in shared code (`new ProviderUpdatedEvent<TProvider>` in Sonarr's `ProviderFactory`) links to the checks of every provider kind (81 distinct sites on Sonarr), the type-argument gap L3 waits on (the attribute's type arguments are kept in the fact for then); an attribute that names an event for another reason would make a handler
- D207 · Request filter attributes on an action or its controller that check the request before the action runs are the flow's checks, at the attribute's line: the framework's antiforgery validation (the nearest of `[ValidateAntiForgeryToken]`, `[AutoValidateAntiforgeryToken]` or `[IgnoreAntiforgeryToken]` wins; the automatic one skips GET, HEAD and OPTIONS actions), and an app's own filter whose code in the repo sets `context.Result` (it can stop the request: a honeypot) or writes `context.ActionArguments[…]` while its name says it checks (Validate, Verify, Check, Captcha: it hands the action its verdict, a captcha's; nopCommerce's `[ParameterBasedOnFormName]` only says which button was pressed). Access rules proven by code or name stay the actor's (D179). Layer 2 `request-check` facts; listed for every action the flow runs, as D203's guards are. Owner: "do em all" (2026-11-11). Cost: a filter in a package (no code in the repo) other than antiforgery isn't read; filters added site-wide (`options.Filters.Add`) aren't listed; a filter that only redirects a wrong URL (a language code) shows as a check
- D208 · FluentValidation rules the flow runs outside its request body's own validator are its checks. Layer 2 says where each rule is written (`in`: the class) and which field or property holds the validator it's added to (`on`), reads `RuleFor(c => c)` as a rule on the whole object, and records each call running a validator (`validates`: it returns FluentValidation's `ValidationResult`, or is its `ValidateAndThrow…`) with the receiver's type and member. A flow lists: for a validator run in code it walks, the rules written in the receiver's class or the classes implementing or deriving from it (`_addSeriesValidator.Validate(series)` through `IAddSeriesValidator`; `Validator.Validate(this)` in `NewznabSettings`), and those its own class or bases write on the member it runs; for an action taking a request body, the rules its controller's classes write on a validator member that the controller's `OnActionExecuting` (or `OnActionExecutionAsync`), or methods of those classes it calls, runs (Sonarr's `SharedValidator`, `PostValidator`, `IdValidator`). Owner: "do em all" (2026-11-11). Cost: a provider's settings validated through an interface the walk dispatches to every implementation lists every provider's settings rules (about 250 checks on each Sonarr provider edit flow), the type-argument gap L3 waits on; Post/Put validators are listed for both verbs
- D209 · An Angular signal form's rules are checks of a flow whose code reads the form or its model. The extractor records each rule function from `@angular/forms/signals` called in `form(model, (path) => …)`'s schema with a path into the model (`required(path.username)`, `minLength(path.password, 6)`, `validate(path.email, …)`) as a `rule` fact on the field holding the form, with the property it checks and the field holding the model; grouping functions (`apply`, `schema`, `applyWhen`…) and an app's own same-named functions aren't rules; a function argument shows as "a custom check". A flow lists them when a walked point reads the form field or the model field (`this.formModel()`). Owner: "do em all" (2026-11-11). Cost: rules in a reusable `schema(…)` applied with `apply` aren't followed; reactive (`FormBuilder`, `Validators.required`) and template-driven forms aren't read; a flow that only resets the form lists its rules too
- D210 · A call through a value taken from another function's result is 🟡, for any function, not only React hooks (G5). Layer 2 already followed `const { goToPage } = useHistory()` to the function the hook hands back when its returns say (D184); when they don't (`const predicate = getFilterTypePredicate(type)` then `predicate(…)`, `const store = createPersist(…)` then `store(…)`, `const { useOption } = createOptionsStore(…)` whose returned functions aren't points), the TypeScript extractor's `call-site` fact now carries `fromResult: "1"`, and the walker shows the step as 🟡 "calls a value taken from X's result; what it runs isn't known, so X stands for it", still walking X. It replaces the hook-only guess, which read the hook's name off the line. Item 1's code check (old 11-line read): Sonarr 69 → 39, Kavita 0, nopCommerce 8; item 2 unchanged (95.3%, 96.0%, 95.6%); Sonarr has 108 flows with such a 🟡 (`useSelect` 76 steps, `getFilterTypePredicate` 54). Found by the code check of item 1 (Sonarr's 30 real wrong ✅: `clientSideFilterAndSort.ts:77,79,82` ×9 flows, `useOptionsStore.ts:45,49`, `seriesOptionsStore.ts:314`). Cost: steps under the 🟡 are X's own code, which ran where X was called, not at this line; C# delegates held in locals aren't read
- D211 · Item 1's code check (the scorer, `FlowScore.WrongCertain`) reads the statement a ✅ step's line starts, to its end, instead of a fixed 11 lines: to a `;` outside brackets, a bracket closing one opened before the line (the step was an argument), a block's `{` after a header (the rest of that line still read: `for (…) { download(…); }`, `new() { Text = Get(…) }`), or a line end outside brackets when neither that line nor the next code line carries it on (a dot, an operator; blank and `//` lines between a chain's links skipped); strings, verbatim strings across lines and comments skipped; at most 400 lines. It also counts a module's default export called under the name the file imports it as (`import fetchJson from 'Utilities/requestAction'`). It changes the scorer, so it's recorded here openly: it's looser on long statements and stricter on short ones (a name in the next statement no longer passes). Every flag it cleared was read in the code first: `SqlBuilder…GroupBy` (`SeriesStatisticsRepository.cs:77–90`, 32), `StartNew(…).LogExceptions()` (`FetchAndParseImportListService.cs:65–91`, `122–150`), `table.WithColumn…ForeignKey` (`CustomerBuilder.cs:24–48`, 8), `fetchJson` (`appStore.ts:122`, 3), all right. A first version flagged Sonarr 316, Kavita 30, nopCommerce 10 (JSX `{translate(…)}` lines taken for blocks, one-line blocks, comment lines in chains); each was a reader fault, fixed and tested, none a wrong step. Code check now: Sonarr 39 → 2, Kavita 0, nopCommerce 8 → 0; items 2–5 unchanged. The 2 left: `goToPage(1)` at `History.tsx:95,102`, "calls useHistory.handleGoToPage" ✅, right (`useHistory` returns `goToPage: handleGoToPage`), a name the checker can't follow. Cost: the check still only asks whether the step's name is in its statement, not that the call is the one named
- D212 · Code in a function given to a timer (`setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `requestIdleCallback`, `setImmediate`) runs later, not when the code around it runs: the extractor marks its call sites and requests `deferred: timer:<name>`, and the walker shows them 🟡 "runs later: a function given to setInterval(…), after its delay", as it does a listener's (D201). Found by item 1's fresh check (Kavita: `setInterval(() => this.refreshToken()…, 600000)` at `account.service.ts:504` was ✅ at login; `setTimeout(() => this.closeReader(), 200)` at `manga-reader.component.ts:1252`). Owner: "Fix all three" (2026-11-11). Cost: a `setTimeout(…, 0)` that runs almost at once is 🟡 too; a repo's own function named like a timer is read as its code, not as a timer
- D213 · An element written inside one of the repo's components that draws nothing while a prop is unset is shown only when that component draws it. The extractor reads a component whose body begins (before its own return) with `if (<condition on its props>) return null` (or `false`, `undefined`, a bare `return`) and that draws its `children`; an element nested in it gets `closedBy: "Modal|!isOpen|isOpen=…"` on its render call site (the nearest such parent; the value passed for each prop in the guard, or "not passed"). The walker shows it 🟡 "shown only when Modal draws it: Modal draws nothing while `!isOpen` (here isOpen=…)", still walked. Found by item 1's fresh check (Sonarr: `Modal.tsx:159`; episode details, manage episodes and season search contents were ✅ on the series page opening). Owner: "Fix all three" (2026-11-11). Cost: a guard after other statements' early returns, or deeper than the body's top level, isn't read; a parent that passes `isOpen={true}` still gives 🟡; Angular's `@if`/`*ngIf` around a component and CSS-hidden content aren't this rule
- D214 · A library's mutation hook (`useMutation`, `createMutation`, `injectMutation`, `useSWRMutation`) keeps the functions it's given and runs them when the mutation is started (`mutate(…)`), not when the hook runs: the extractor marks code in them `deferred: mutation:<name>`, and a call of a configured wrapper (`useApiMutation`) whose own body sends only from such code (every `fetch` or wrapper call in it shares the deferral) gets that deferral on its request. The walker shows the request and calls 🟡 "sent when the mutation is started (its mutate(…) is called), not when useMutation sets it up"; still walked, so item 2 still finds them. Found by item 1's fresh check (Sonarr: `POST /command` at `useCommands.ts:45` was ✅ when the series page opens). Owner: "Fix all three" (2026-11-11). Cost: in a click flow that does call `mutate`, the request is 🟡 too (the step isn't moved to the `mutate` line); a hand-written mutation (a function kept in a ref or state) isn't read
- D215 · Code in a function given to a cache's get-or-create call runs only when nothing is stored for the key: it carries a `cache-miss` condition quoting the call (`_shortTermCacheManager.GetAsync`), innermost first like an `if`, and the flow's Markdown reads "only if `X` finds nothing stored for the key". Recognised by the method's name and a function argument, not by the receiver's type, because the types differ in every repo and library (`IMemoryCache`, `HybridCache`, Sonarr's `ICached<T>`, nopCommerce's `IStaticCacheManager` and `ShortTermCacheManager`, Kavita's own `FileCacheService`, `ConcurrentDictionary`) and no fixed list of them would carry to the next repo: a `GetOr` + capital name (`GetOrAdd`, `GetOrCreateAsync`, `GetOrFetchAsync`; `GetOrder` isn't one) or `AddOrGetExisting` says it whatever the receiver; a plain `Get`/`GetAsync` says nothing alone, so it counts only when the method's type or the receiver's names a cache (contains "cach"). Layer 2 in C# (`ConditionsDetector.Around`) and the TypeScript extractor (`conditionsAround`, `getOr…`/`get`), which none of the three repos' frontends use. The walker no longer hands a call with no condition fact of its own the conditions of another call on its line (the cache call itself would have taken its function's `cache-miss`); a step not tied to a call still takes the line's. Found by item 1's second fresh check (nopCommerce `CustomerService.cs:1546`, the read of `CustomerPassword` under it). Conditions don't change labels: ✅ counts unchanged; Sonarr 381 steps carry one (`_cache.Get` 153), nopCommerce 1,498 (`_shortTermCacheManager.GetAsync` 866, `_staticCacheManager.GetAsync` 602), Kavita 1 (its `HybridCache` and `IMemoryCache` functions are handed to a package, so no flow walks them, D201). Bar unchanged: code check Sonarr 2 (the known `goToPage`), Kavita 0, nopCommerce 0; item 2 95.3%, 96.0%, 95.6%; items 3–5 pass. Cost: a cache method named otherwise (`Fetch`, `Remember`, nopCommerce's repository `GetAllAsync(func, getCacheKey)`) isn't read; a function handed over by name (a method group) isn't either; a repo's own `GetOr…` method that runs its function every time would be read as a cache; a quoted call split across lines keeps its line break
- D216 · A call after `?.` runs only when what's before it is set, and so do its arguments and the functions given to it: it carries a `set` condition quoting the chain up to the nearest `?.` (`this.doubleRenderer()` in `this.doubleRenderer()?.renderPage(page)`, `a?.Next` in `a?.Next?.Draw()`), and the flow's Markdown reads "only if `X` is set". C# and TypeScript alike: layer 2's `ConditionsDetector.Around` reads a call inside a conditional access's `WhenNotNull`; the extractor's `conditionsAround` reads the call's own optional chain, and a call or element access it's an argument of. What's before the `?.` itself runs either way and gets nothing. Found by item 1's second fresh check (Kavita `manga-reader.component.ts:1669`: step 272 of the swipe-end flow, "calls DoubleRendererComponent.renderPage", now only if `this.doubleRenderer()` is set). Conditions don't change labels: ✅ counts unchanged; Sonarr 915 steps carry one (`fieldValue?.ToString()` 246, `analysis.AudioStreams?…` 120), Kavita 59, nopCommerce 356 (`(… as IEntityBuilder)?.MapEntity` 292); three read in the code, all right. Bar unchanged: code check Sonarr 2 (the known `goToPage`), Kavita 0, nopCommerce 0; item 2 95.3%, 96.0%, 95.6%; items 3–5 pass. Cost: a `System` call after `?.` (`handler?.Invoke()`) is no step, so it shows nothing; a null check written otherwise (`x && x.b()`, `if (x != null)` is already an `if`) isn't this rule; `?.` in an Angular template isn't read
- D217 · Bar item 1 (no wrong ✅) counts as passed for layer 3 on two fresh checks: the first found 6 wrong ✅ of 36, all from three general causes, fixed (D212–D214); the second found 0 of 36 new steps once its two flags were read in the code; the code check finds none but the known `goToPage` checker limit (Sonarr 2, Kavita 0, nopCommerce 0). No third check for D215/D216: they add conditions and change no label (✅ counts identical), so a check would retest what the second one did, for ~100–120K tokens. Owner: "ur call" (2026-11-11), the assistant's call. Cost: a sample can't prove zero; 0 of 36 still allows roughly 1 wrong ✅ in 12 (95% bound), so "passed" means every cause found is fixed and none is known, not that none exists. Left as a small roadmap item: RxJS `debounceTime`/`delay` (and the like) run what follows later, yet it's ✅ as running now, the same kind as D212's timers
- D218 · Areas hold only start flows (clicks and submits, screen openings, jobs, handlers); a shared flow (D189) belongs to no area. `areas check` checks every start flow sits in exactly one area; each area lists the shared flows its starts reach, by code, for information, not for a person to confirm. Changes D180's "every flow in exactly one area". Found by laying D178 over the real flows: shared flows are most flows (Sonarr 1,287 of 1,679, Kavita 248 of 530, nopCommerce 434 of 1,378) and most are reached from several areas (Sonarr 821 from 2+, nopCommerce 342 from 2+, 256 from 5+; `shared-idatabase-openconnection` from all 8 of Sonarr's), so one area each would mean hundreds of ties settled arbitrarily. Owner: "Yes, starts only" (2026-11-11). Cost: a shared flow only one area uses (Sonarr 407) isn't filed under it, only listed as used by it
- D219 · A screen's home folder comes from the graph, not from the flow index's `home` (the route file's or `Controllers` folder): the component a route opens (React, Angular) or the view an MVC action shows, for every screen, display-only ones included (Kavita's `/settings`, 104 clicks under it). Frontend: the deepest folder holding all one frontend's screens is its root (Sonarr `frontend/src`, Kavita `UI/Web/src/app`), the home the next folder down (Sonarr 9, Kavita 21); a frontend with one screen takes that screen's folder. Razor: the folder after `Views/`, named with its project and the folders between, `Areas/` dropped (`Nop.Web/Areas/Admin/Views/Product` → Nop.Web › Admin › Product; `Nop.Plugin.Misc.Forums/Public/Views/…` → Nop.Plugin.Misc.Forums › Public). A view path known only at runtime (nopCommerce's 91 plugin screens, `~/Plugins/…`) takes MVC's own convention: the controller's name in its project. A folder holding a screen is a home; one holding none is shared (Sonarr's `InteractiveImport`, `Episode`; Kavita's `cards`), and a click in it takes the home of the screen showing it, several → a tie. A route matching any URL (`*`, `**`, the not-found screen) makes no home (Sonarr's `/*` → `Components/NotFound.tsx`). A dialog is shown by the screen whose code opens it (the graph's `uses` line to its component). Clause replaced 2026-11-11 (owner: "Yes, replace it"): the first one ("shown on screens of two or more other homes") left Sonarr 4 homes, since every home lends a few pieces (Series 5 of 83); with this one, folders alone place Sonarr 89 clicks (19 ties, 8 on no screen), Kavita 210 (10, 13), nopCommerce 320 (2, 3). Owner: "Yes, that rule" (2026-11-11). Cost: stiff names to rename once; the project name shows even for the main web project → the fallback under D220
- D220 · An area is a part of the app as its users would name it (Sonarr's Series, Calendar, Activity, Settings › Indexers; nopCommerce's Checkout, Admin › Catalog › Products), not the code's folder layout. Areas serve two readers: the spec pages people read (layer 4, one page per area) and the requirement matching that puts a document's section in one area before comparing it with that area's flows (D154), and both speak the users' words. Code drafts them from where the app itself names its parts for users (the navigation menu, the rule to be settled next); D178/D219's folder rule is the fallback for screens no menu reaches. Still no AI, and a person confirms once. Owner: "A, user-facing parts" (2026-11-11), after saying the folder rule felt too simple. Cost: the graph doesn't read menus yet (layer 2 work and its fresh check); an app with no menu, or one built at runtime, gets folder areas
- D221 · How code reads the menu (D220). A menu item is a name plus a constant address leading to a screen, written in markup (`link=`, `routerLink`, `to=`, `href`, `asp-controller`/`asp-action`) or as data (an object with a name-like and an address-like property, children nested under it), the address through the repo's own URL helper when its arguments are constant (D190's rule: nopCommerce's `GetMenuItemUrl("Product", "List")`); its name from the translation files (D176). A list of three or more items is a menu. Each top-level item is an area (Sonarr's Activity, Settings; nopCommerce's Admin › Catalog); its children are the split when the area is over 40 flows (Settings › Indexers), in place of the next folder level. A screen no menu reaches joins the menu area holding a screen in its home folder (D219: Sonarr's series details, in `Series/` beside the series list), else its folder is an area (Kavita's readers). Clicks, dialogs, jobs and ties as settled for areas. Layer 2 gains `menu-item` facts in the TypeScript extractor and the C#/Razor detectors, fixtures on React, Angular and Razor, then a narrow fresh check (D158, estimated 60–100K tokens, asked first). Seen in the code: Sonarr's `PageSidebar.tsx` (data with children), Kavita's `side-nav.component.html` (flat markup; its libraries come at runtime), nopCommerce's `AdminMenu.cs` (109 items built in C#). Rough, unmeasured: Sonarr about 7 areas, Kavita about 18, nopCommerce's admin about 10 top-level (57 by folders). Owner: "Yes, that rule" (2026-11-11). Cost: items added at runtime (Kavita's libraries) aren't read; a menu written in a way the rule doesn't know (a nav built by a loop over server data) gives folder areas; any list of three links counts as a menu, so a footer or a settings index can too
- D222 · A click on no screen (D219's walk found none): the walk up goes on through code that isn't a component, a table holding the component or a function opening it, to whatever reads or calls it, until a component (Sonarr's settings inputs picked from `componentMap` by field type land where the forms are; Kavita's match-series, edit-collection and edit-smart-filter dialogs, opened by shared service code, land in the areas whose screens call it, several → a tie). One that reaches only the app's frame, the layout every screen sits in (Sonarr's header menu with Restart and Shutdown, Kavita's top bar, nopCommerce's `_Root.cshtml` footer forms), goes to a code-made area, App frame, one per frontend, for the person to rename or share out. One that reaches nothing is a tie with every area to choose, saying code can't see which screen shows it; nothing is dropped. Found on the three repos: 24 clicks on no screen by folders alone (Sonarr 8, Kavita 13, nopCommerce 3). Owner: "Yes, those rules" (2026-11-11). Cost: walking up through a widely used service can reach many screens (a tie with many choices); App frame is a made-up name; numbers unmeasured until built
- D223 · Jobs and handlers (changes D178's "the area that links to them most"): count per area the start flows leading to the job or handler, directly or through shared flows; one already placed counts for those it leads to, repeated until nothing changes, in a fixed order (Refresh series follows Add series into Series). An area with more than half of them takes it; an even split between exactly two areas is a tie for the person; spread wider, it serves the whole app and goes to its backend folder, as one with no links does. The backend folder is the first folder below the class's project (`NzbDrone.Core/Tv` → NzbDrone.Core › Tv, `Nop.Services/Orders` → Nop.Services › Orders), a class at a project's root taking the project's name; a job registered on an interface takes its one implementing class's file (Kavita's Hangfire jobs on `IBackupService` and the like, in `Kavita.API/Services`). Measured roughly on folder areas: Sonarr 91 by majority before chains, 4 ties, 5 spread; Kavita 0, all 23 to backend folders (reached from 11 areas through shared code); nopCommerce 24, 2 ties, 110 spread (`AclEventConsumer` handles every entity insert; "most" gave 97 even ties and the rest to whichever page had one link more). Owner: "Yes, that rule" (2026-11-11). Cost: nopCommerce's spread is partly the generic-event gap (L3), so after L3 more find a majority; backend folder names are developers' words until renamed
- D224 · An area over 40 flows with nothing left to split by (no menu children, no deeper folder) stays whole and is flagged in `areas.yaml` ("over 40, nothing to split by") for the person to split or leave; 40 is a reading-size guide, and code invents no split. Splitting by screen was turned down: it brings back many tiny areas (Product list, Product edit…). By folder count: nopCommerce's Admin › Product (64), Customer (49), Admin › Order (44); the menu count is unmeasured. Owner: "Yes, flag it" (2026-11-11). Cost: a few large areas left for a person
- D225 · `areas.yaml` describes each area by where its flows come from, not by a list of flows: `name` (the person's), `menu` (the menu items' addresses it holds), `folders` (screens' homes and backend folders); the deepest match wins, so a person merges by listing two sources under one name and splits by giving a menu item or deeper folder its own area; new flows land by themselves. `large` lists D224's flags; `ties` lists each flow code couldn't place with `choose` and the person's `answer`. `areas propose` writes a fresh `specs/areas.proposed.yaml`, never touching `areas.yaml` (D180); `areas check` reads `areas.yaml` and reports every start flow in exactly one area, menu items and folders no area covers, ties unanswered and answers whose flow is gone, and writes the code-made `specs/areas.json` (every start flow's area, and per area the shared flows its starts reach, D218), so the person's file stays short. Changes D180's "by folders" to "by menu items and folders". Owner: "Yes, that shape" (2026-11-11), after asking what `answer` is. Cost: a tie is keyed by flow id, so a renamed flow's answer shows as gone and is asked again
- D226 · Which lists of links are the menu (D221 left it open; any list of three counts, footers and settings indexes included). A menu needs three or more items that lead to a screen; menus are taken by how many screens they reach, most first, and one more than half of whose screens a menu already taken reaches adds nothing (Sonarr's settings page index of the sidebar's settings children). Each taken menu's top-level items are areas, two with the same name one area; a menu whose addresses lead into an MVC area prefixes it (nopCommerce's Admin › Catalog). A menu area over 40 splits by its children only; a folder area by its next folder; anything else over 40 is flagged (D224); a person still splits a menu area by a deeper folder in `areas.yaml`, since a folder deeper than any its menu area lists wins. Found on the three repos: Sonarr's settings index (13 items, all in the sidebar) and its outside-links list (11, no screens) aren't taken, nor Kavita's (7); nopCommerce takes AdminMenu.cs and its header links. The assistant's call while building; owner: "ok sure" (2026-11-11). Cost: an in-page tab list reaching screens no bigger menu reaches becomes top-level areas of its own; a menu split into parts can leave the parent with what no part holds (Sonarr's Settings 77 after its parts took 56: jobs serving all settings)
- D227 · Parts of a split area count for it (follows D223 and D226). A job's or handler's leaders are counted up the split: the deepest area holding more than half takes it (a handler led by Settings › Indexers, › Download Clients and › Import Lists alike goes to Settings, not its backend folder); a click two parts of one area could hold goes to that area, not a tie; when an area splits, a home folder most of whose menu screens sit in one part moves to it (Sonarr's Series/ to Media › Series); a part left with no flows folds back, and an area no flow sits in isn't proposed (a heading whose parts took all, Kavita's display-only `announcements`). An area's parts are the areas named `<it> › …`, so `areas check` reads the same from `areas.yaml`. Found on Sonarr: splitting Settings and Media by their children first sent 44 jobs that served the whole of Settings to backend folders and tied 4 Series flows between Media and Media › Series. The assistant's call while building; owner: "ok sure" (2026-11-11). Cost: renaming a part so it no longer starts with its parent's name makes it an area of its own for this counting
- D228 · Area names read translations further than flow names do: a translation that is only another key's reference (transloco's `"customize": "{{settings.customize}}"`) reads that key, and after the JSON files .NET-style XML resources are read, a neutral or English `.resx` or an `.xml` file named for resources, each element with a name and a value child (nopCommerce's `defaultResources.nopres.xml`: "Account.MyAccount" → "My account"). Flow names (D176) are unchanged, so flows rebuild byte for byte. The assistant's call while building; owner: "ok sure" (2026-11-11). An area's folder is its name made into a slug the way a flow id is (D176: ASCII letters and digits lowercased, every other run one dash; `Settings › Indexers` → `settings-indexers`, a name with nothing left → `area`); two names giving one slug: the first in ordinal order keeps it, the others take `-2`, `-3`, so reordering `areas.yaml` moves nothing; `shared` and `unplaced` are never an area's. A start flow tied and unanswered, or in no area, sits in `specs/areas/unplaced/`, listed by `flows build` and failing `areas check` as before. A shared flow's users are the folders of the start flows reaching it, an `unplaced/` start counting as its folder: one folder → there, else `shared/`. `index.json` gains each flow's `file` (its `.json` path under the repo; `flows show` and the scorer find flows through it). `flows build` removes flow files no longer where the layout puts them and folders left empty, never a `page.md`; without `areas.yaml` everything goes back to `specs/flows/`. A flow's "Links" are relative Markdown links to the target's `.md`. Flow `.json` bytes are unchanged by the move; contract `flows` v1 keeps its version, since only r3 reads it (roadmap). The assistant's call while building; owner: "Yes, confirm them" (2026-11-11). Cost: a slug collision suffix depends on the other names; non-Latin area names all slug to `area-…`

The repo's own telemetry replaces the estimate after the first run, and a full repo gets its own cap set from the pilot's real numbers. Opus 5.5 (~2× the cost) only as D138's one fix attempt if Sonnet fails bar item 3; Haiku 5.5 (~20× cheaper) turned down as the likeliest to drop conditions and hedges, so retries and fallbacks against bar item 1's 5%. The fresh agent for bar item 3 (~80–120K tokens) is apart from the cap and asked for first. Owner: "Yes, Sonnet, $10 cap" (2026-11-12), on the recommendation. Cost: a model change later re-runs every page (the hash includes the model)
- D257 · Layer 4 pilots on 7 areas across the three repos, each chosen for what it tests: Sonarr's Series (78 start flows, the biggest: screen sections and 14 message groups), Series › Add New (5, the smallest, the mock page, its flow in the approved key), Settings › Indexers (8, "one of N" 🟡 and run-time outside calls: hedges); Kavita's Reading Lists (12, Angular) and registration (8, "anyone, signed in or not"); nopCommerce's Nop.Web › Checkout (20, Razor and the public store's access filters) and Admin › Catalog (61, admin permissions with long rule names, over 40). Turned down: Sonarr only (no ties to answer, but one React app, "who" always "signed-in user", no Razor or Angular: the single-repo shaping R.2 and R.9 warn of). Owner: "Yes, all three repos" (2026-11-12), on the recommendation. Cost: the owner's two sittings on ties before the pilot; an area name may change when its ties are answered, and the pilot takes the confirmed name
- D258 · The old `specs/reference/` pages retire one reader at a time (D230's "retired once these replace it", applied per consumer): when layer 4 passes its bar, the old page writer stops being run (no new paid reference runs), people are pointed to `specs/areas/<area>/page.md`, and the old files stay frozen on disk, marked on the roadmap "kept only for old linking and tickets"; old linking (FSD candidates, link judge, disprove, requirements) stops reading them when layer 5 passes; the ticket bundle (page index, starting points) when layer 6 passes; the files, the writer and the `reference-page` contract go in the cuts (layer 7, P8), on the owner's OK, with the other unproven switches (D138). `spec ask` and test journeys, already marked for cutting, go with them. Turned down: all at once when layer 4 passes (old linking and tickets break before their replacements exist, and their numbers, 24 of 30 found and Phase 3's proof, can't be re-run to compare); keeping both for good (two page sets disagreeing about one screen, the old one going stale). Owner: "Yes, per consumer" (2026-11-12), on the recommendation. Cost: frozen old pages sit beside the new ones until layer 7
- D259 · Where layer 4 keeps what it makes, settled while building (steps 2–3): the summary isn't committed: `stangent pages summary <area>` rebuilds it from the flow files and `areas.yaml` byte for byte, so git holds only what was paid for or decided (D7, D25). Each section's checked words, its hash (its items, the writing rules and schema, the model, the effort, D254, D256), the model and effort, its cost and whether it was retried go in one committed file, `specs/pages-runs.json`, keyed `<area>#<section>`; the words between D233's two lines are rebuilt from it on every `pages check` (a person's edit there is lost on the next check). Not in `specs/areas/<area>/`, since `flows build` removes stray `.json` files there. Section ids are stable: a screen's opening flow id, `clicks`, `on-<message>`, `jobs`, `in-short`. The assistant's call while building (2026-11-12); owner: "Yes, confirm all" (2026-11-13). Cost: renaming an area re-keys its records, so its sections are written again
- D260 · How code cuts a flow into items, settled while building (D251's items made exact): an item's label is its own step's (✅/🟡), not its parents' (else every item under a guessed first call is 🟡, against D251's worked example); a message is 🟡 when its handlers were found by the message's type; a ❓ with a step marks that step's item (the outside call with a URL built at run time) and only a ❓ with no item becomes its own; who is one item per wording; a screen's flow reaching a click's item again under all the click's conditions plus the screen's own is one item with both flows (D251's "saves Series … only while the dialog is open, only when Add is pressed"), while a copy on another path stays apart; a save inside shared code is listed once per start flow, at its line, with only the conditions on every path to it (each kept condition is needed on every path; none is made up), reads inside shared code aren't followed; shown texts (D252) are calls named `translate`, `t`, `T`, `instant`, `GetResource`, `GetResourceAsync` or `Localize` with a quoted key; a guard check is named by its member (`AddSeriesService.SetPropertiesAndValidate`); a click on several screens sits with the first, and clicks on no screen of the area get their own section. Why the shared-save rule: without it nopCommerce's Checkout gave 113,134 items (778 MB), the same saves along thousands of paths; with it 1,443. The assistant's call while building (2026-11-12); owner: "Yes, confirm all" (2026-11-13). Cost: a condition true on only some paths to a shared save isn't on the page (the shared flow's own file still has it)
- D261 · Layer 4's sections, settled while building: "Can't tell from the code" is code's list of every ❓ item, with no AI call; who shared by the whole area is an item of "In short"; "In short" is prepared only once every other section is current, and cites their items as `§n:ID` (n the section's place on the page), from their checked sentences (or a fallen-back section's must-cite items), none of them required. A section not yet written for its current items, or one that fell back, shows the code's list and says so. The assistant's call while building (2026-11-12); owner: "Yes, confirm all" (2026-11-13). Cost: any section's rewrite re-runs "In short"
- D262 · Layer 4's checks, settled while building (D255 item 1 made exact): each sentence marks every condition of every item it cites, `said` or `always` (per sentence, not per page, as D251's "one dropping a condition"); the hedge list is 15 words and phrases (probably, likely, may, might, appears, seems, could, perhaps, possibly, not known, unknown, isn't known, unclear, can't tell, at run time), matched as whole words; a sentence or lead with a line break, HTML or Markdown is rejected (code adds the formatting and the hidden item note); must-cite items are who, saves, messages, outside calls, early exits, check groups and any 🟡/❓. The assistant's call while building (2026-11-12); owner: "Yes, confirm all" (2026-11-13). Cost: long sections need many marks, which is most of the writer's output
- D263 · `stangent pages summary <area> --proposed` places flows by `specs/areas.proposed.yaml` for a look before `areas.yaml` is confirmed (open ties leave their flows out); `pages sections`, `prepare` and `check` take only the confirmed file. Used to count Kavita's and nopCommerce's pilot areas before the owner confirms their ties (D257). The assistant's call while building (2026-11-12); owner: "Yes, confirm all" (2026-11-13). Cost: none beyond a flag
- D264 · Layer 4's sections shrunk before the paid pilot (changes D251's exits and D253's one section per screen): an early exit item carries only its own conditions (the path to it stays in the flow's file), and exits are one item per what they throw and their own conditions' code, in a section, with the other places counted (`alsoAt`); a section whose item lines pass 40,000 characters (about 10K tokens) splits by flow, in order, each part as full as the limit allows, a flow over it alone, as `<id>-part-<n>` "<title> (n of k)". Measured on the 7 pilot areas: Sonarr's Series 1,858K → 853K characters (with D265), its biggest section 935K → 100K; nopCommerce's Checkout 2,093K → 1,629K, biggest 1,388K → 388K (one flow). Owner: "Yes, shrink first" (2026-11-12), on the recommendation. Cost: a split section repeats the items its flows share in every part (Settings › Indexers 510 → 1,543 items); part ids shift when a flow is added, so later parts are written again; one flow over the limit still makes one big call
- D265 · Reads and shown texts are one item per table or wording in a section, keeping only the conditions every place shares, with the other places counted (the rule D260 uses for saves in shared code, applied to items a sentence may leave out). Needed after D264: in Checkout's one-page form 164 reads carried 11 path conditions each. Reads and shown texts aren't must-cite, so no check gets weaker. The assistant's call while building (2026-11-12); owner: "Yes, confirm all" (2026-11-13). Cost: a condition true for only some reads of a table isn't on the page
- D266 · Layer 4's pilot runs under `--max-cost 25` (changes D256's $10): after D264/D265 the 7 pilot areas measure 125 sections, ~5.2M characters of items, roughly $7 a run and ~$20 for bar item 2's three runs; all 7 areas still run three times as D255 says. The model stays Sonnet 5.5 at medium effort; the first run's telemetry replaces the estimate, and a full repo's cap is set from it. Owner: "Raise cap to $25" (2026-11-12), on the first option offered. Cost: up to $25 for the pilot, against $10 planned
- D267 · Kavita's and nopCommerce's `areas.yaml` confirmed by the owner (2026-11-13), kept in `benchmark/expected/areas/`, answered by group as D231 did for Sonarr, every group on the recommendation. - D268 · Layer 4's pilot runs all 7 areas once and repeats only the four small ones (changes D255 item 2's scope and D266's cap): the smoke run on Series › Add New (3 sections checked, none fell back, $0.21) priced a full run of the 7 areas at ~$17–20, so three full runs (~$55–60) don't fit. - D269 · Layer 4's bar item 2 is measured on three areas, under the pilot's $30 cap (changes D268's four): run 1 cost $19.96 for 116 of 124 sections (none fell back), so finishing it (~$3.50) and repeating the four small areas (~$10.40) would pass $30. Settings › Indexers leaves the repeats; Series › Add New, Kavita's registration and Reading Lists (16 sections) are repeated twice. A repeat is D161's `spec-run.sh --repeat 2 --call <id>` on each section's first writer call (the same input and settings, nothing recorded in the project), and code compares the item ids each of the three first answers cites; a section counts as matching when all three cite the same must-cite items, and bar item 2 passes at 90% of the 16.
