- D270 · Layer 4's pilot stops and the page is redesigned (changes D253's page shape and D255's bar; D268/D269's runs end here): the owner read the pilot's Series › Add New page (2026-11-13) and rejected it: "i just dont like it at all", "it just doesnt make sense for anyone to read this bloat", no flowchart to see how it works at a glance, no scenario steps, nothing on what stops or continues them, no edge cases a PM can read. That is bar item 4 failing, so layer 4 fails as designed, though items 1, 2 and 5 passed: the bar measured whether sentences are true four ways and whether a person would read them once. Item 3 isn't run (the sampler and its bundle are kept, nothing spent). The new shape, proposed by the assistant and agreed in outline (owner: "Yes, redesign"), is per task on a screen instead of per code section: a flowchart drawn by code from the flow's steps, conditions and exits; a scenario table, one row per path; edge cases as the conditions that change the path; plumbing (developer-only exits, default setups, the ❓ list) folded away; and a "differs from the FSD" slot that layer 5 fills later. The details are being grilled and get their own entries; nothing is built until then. Cost: $24.82 of AI spend went to a shape that's dropped; what survives of `pages summary`, the checker and the driver depends on the new shape; a later paid wording run is about $20 again.
- D271 · The new page's unit is the click that changes something (D270's "per task", settled): a click gets its own part of the page, with a flowchart and scenario table, when its flow saves, deletes, sends a message, queues work or calls an outside service; clicks that only change what's on screen (sorting, filtering, opening a dialog) are one line each with no chart; a screen's opening flow gets a chart only if it does more than read. Example: Series › Add New has two charts, "Search for a show" and "Add a show". Chosen over one chart per screen, which turns into a wall of boxes on anything larger than Add New. Owner: "Yes, per changing click" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: a big screen gets many charts (nopCommerce's Admin › Catalog could pass 50), so the page needs a contents list, and a click whose only effect is one the graph can't see (a ❓) would be listed as display-only unless the rule counts ❓ steps as changes, which it does.
- D272 · A click's refusals sorted into three buckets, shared technical guards folded away from a PM → archived: replaced by D278: a developer reads the page and wants the guards visible; the three kinds stay as headings in the lists, nothing is folded for being technical
- D273 · What a click's flowchart draws, by fixed rules, in Mermaid inside `page.md`; the size cap is set from a count, not guessed. Drawn: the click as the start box; one box per thing that changes the world (a save, a delete, a message sent, queued work, an outside call), the same change in several places drawn once; a diamond per condition that decides whether one of those happens; all field checks as one diamond ("All checks pass?") leading to "Refused", the single checks in the table underneath; an outside-service failure as one dashed line from the call's box to "Refused"; what runs afterwards on its own as one box linking to its part of the page, not expanded. Not drawn: reads; D272's folded technical guards; setup steps, a save whose only condition is that nothing is saved yet ("saves a default naming setup if none exists"), which go to the folded technical part. Mermaid because it is text in the page, GitHub and most Markdown viewers draw it, and code can produce it byte for byte. The cap and the over-the-cap rule (for instance, main path in the chart and the rest in the table) are decided after the chart maker is built and its boxes per click are counted on the 7 pilot areas, so the number isn't tuned to Add New, the smallest page. Owner: "Yes, measure first" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: the setup-step rule will sometimes fold a real business step out of the chart (it stays in the folded part); a viewer that doesn't draw Mermaid shows the chart as text; building the chart maker before the cap is known may mean reworking how it splits.
- D274 · The AI words a chart's labels and nothing else; code owns the shape → archived: replaced by D278: a developer wants code's own names and lines, so there is no wording step
- D275 · The redesigned page's five-gate bar, the owner's read first → archived: replaced by D278: gates 1 and 5 rested on a PM's read and gate 4 on AI labels; a developer's bar is to be written before anything else is built
- D276 · The redesigned page has no paragraphs, and the built layer 4 code is kept or retired by that: kept, the item cutter (`pages summary`, contract `pages` v1), which the chart maker builds on and layer 5's judge still reads; kept and pointed at labels, the run machinery (`pages prepare`/`check`, `spec-run.sh --pages`, `--max-cost`, the run records, one retry then code's own text), now one call per chart instead of per section, a chart whose labels fail twice keeping code's raw labels; retired, the paragraph writer (the `stangent-area-writer` agent, `area-section-output.v1.json`, and the sentence checks for citations, hedges, conditions and coverage) and "In short". The page is, per changing click, a chart, a scenario table, refusals and edge cases. Chosen over keeping a one- or two-sentence AI-worded "In short" per screen: it's the part of the old page most prone to bloat and a chart's title already says what the click is; it comes back only if gate 5 (D275) shows the page lacks a lead-in. Owner: "Yes, drop paragraphs" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: roughly a third of the layer 4 code built so far is retired, removed when the label step is built and not before; the pilot's $24.82 of results carries nothing over, since it measured sentences that no longer exist; `pages` stays v1 until the new page's output is settled, then bumps with its consumers marked `needs review` (D20).
- D277 · Two charts per click (a business-logic overview and the detailed one) with a scenario table between them → archived: replaced by D278: the business-logic overview and the business/technical sorting were for a PM; its counts and the fault it found in the shared-save fold are kept in `phases/r4-pages.md`
- D278 · Layer 4's page is for a developer, not a PM (changes D251's readers and D270's redesign; archives D272, D274, D275, D277; keeps D270, D271, D273, D276). The owner had taken the page to be written knowing the FSD; told it is made from the code alone and meets the FSD only in layer 5, the owner said "then i guess i have the wrong assumptions, the page should be dev, not PM" (2026-11-13) and agreed to this split ("yup okay"). So: the page is made by code only, with no AI step, no wording run and no paragraphs; labels are code's own names with `file:line`; nothing is hidden for being technical; per click that changes something (D271) it shows D273's flowchart as the main chart and a scenario table, one row per path with what decides it and where. The PM's product is the FSD comparison: layer 5 compares the FSD's requirements to the code's items and words the differences for a PM, and the page is what a developer opens to check a finding. Kept from the archived entries as facts, not rules: the first and second counts of chart sizes, the five sample clicks, and the fault that folding a save passed by 10 or more flows hides real business saves (Sonarr's "Delete series"). Still open, to be settled before more is built: what the page gives a developer that layer 3's flow files don't, and the bar that tests it (a developer's question such as "can I tell what this click touches and where it stops, faster than reading the code?"); whether framework saves made on most requests (nopCommerce's customer and log saves, in 85% of its flows) are still folded, and at what share; the size cap on the chart; what becomes of the built overview-chart and business-decision code; whether `pages prepare`/`check` and the paragraph writer are all retired now that no AI step is left (D276 kept the run machinery for labels). Cost: about 365K tokens of building and $24.82 of pilot runs went to a PM page that is dropped, though the chart maker, the scenario rows and the counts carry over; a PM has nothing to read from layer 4 and waits for layer 5; a developer page has to earn its place beside the flow files, which isn't shown yet.
- D279 · The developer page's shape, and what it has to give beyond the flow file (settles two of D278's open points). Shape, per click that changes something, in this order, nothing folded: the flowchart (D273's, code's own labels); the scenario table, one row per path with what decides it, `file:line` and how it ends, holding the field checks so they aren't listed twice; outside services failing; what runs afterwards on its own; guards shared with other flows, each with how many flows pass it; changes made in shared code. The owner read a hand-assembled sample for Add New's Add click (2026-11-13) and accepted it ("okay", then "sure"). What it gives: a developer already has the click's flow file, the same facts as a step list read top to bottom; the page has to let them see the click's whole behaviour at a glance (the chart) and every way it can end in one table (the scenarios). The bar tests exactly that, by comparison: a fresh agent answers the same questions about a click from the page and another from the flow file, and the page must be at least as accurate and need less reading; its numbers are frozen in their own entry before anything more is built. Owner: "sure" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: if the page doesn't beat the flow file, layer 4 has no product and the flow files stand alone; chart labels are cut at 80 characters today, which hides the end of outside-call labels, to be fixed in the layout round.
- D280 · The developer page's bar, frozen before anything more is built (takes the place of the archived D275): three gates in order, each run only if the one before passes. (1) Nothing lost: by code, every change and every stop in a click's flow file is on its page, and rebuilds are byte for byte the same; 100% on all 7 pilot areas; free. (2) The page beats the flow file: 9 clicks, 3 per repo (small, middle, large, picked by code with a fixed seed), 4 set questions each (what does this click save, send or queue; which outside services does it call; every way it can stop, with the condition; what runs afterwards on its own); one fresh agent (D61, Sonnet) answers from the pages alone, another from the flow files alone, a third grades both against the source code; the page scores at least as well as the flow file, gets at least 34 of 36 right, and is the shorter read by characters; about 120–200K tokens. (3) The owner reads three pages as a developer, "would I open this before reading the code?", yes on all three; free. Owner: "Yes, freeze it" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation, over gates 1 and 3 alone. Cost: 36 answers show the page is roughly as good as the flow file, not that it's better on every kind of click; the grader is an AI reading source, so a wrong grade is possible and the owner rules on any answer the two readers and the grader split on; the size cap and the framework-save rule are settled before gate 1 and aren't gates.
- D281 · Framework saves stay on the developer page but out of the chart, by share of the app's flows (settles D278's open point; replaces the 10-flow threshold tried under the archived D277): a change (a save, delete, message, queued job or outside call) counts as a framework change when more than 10% of the app's flows pass through it, and at least 10 flows; it is listed under "Changes made in shared code" with its `file:line` and flow count, isn't drawn, and doesn't make a click "changing" (D271), so a click whose only changes are framework ones is one line, like a display-only click. The share is a setting in `stangent.yaml`, 10% by default. Measured on the three repos: nopCommerce's customer and log saves are in 85% of its 948 flows, Sonarr's default naming setup in 27% of 392, its `ModelEvent` broadcast in 16%, Kavita's user-record update in 15% of 282; the business saves seen are at 7% or less (Kavita reading progress 7%, Sonarr series and episode files 3–5%, nopCommerce orders 1–3%). The fixed threshold of 10 flows had made Sonarr's "Delete series" display-only (its save is in 11 flows, 3%). Chosen over drawing every save, which never misfiles but opens every nopCommerce chart with the same framework boxes. Owner: "Yes, 10% rule" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: the line is fitted to three repos and could be wrong either way on a fourth, hence the setting; a save between 7% and 15% hasn't been seen, so where the line should really sit isn't known; shared guards (stops) keep their own section and aren't touched by this rule.
- D282 · A click's chart is drawn up to 40 nodes; above that the page shows no chart for it, only a line saying how many nodes it would have had, with the scenario table and lists as usual (settles D273's cap). The cap is a setting in `stangent.yaml`, 40 by default, and the number is checked against the recount under D281 before D280's bar runs. From the second count (taken under the old fold rule, so it shifts a little): charts over 40 nodes are 4 of 44 in Sonarr's Series, 3 of 26 in nopCommerce's Admin › Catalog, 3 of 7 in Checkout, 3 of 5 in Kavita's Reading Lists, none in Add New, Indexers or registration. Nothing is lost above the cap, since every path is a row in the table. Chosen over always drawing (a 152-node chart is unreadable in any viewer) and over splitting a big chart into several, which takes real work to find cut points and helps a handful of clicks. Owner: "yes" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: the clicks that most need a picture, the biggest, are the ones that get none; Kavita's Reading Lists loses the chart on three of five clicks because one click dispatches eight actions, and splitting by action is left undone; 40 is a judgement of what fits a screen, not a measured limit.
- D283 · Every layer 4 page opens with a note made by code, and layer 5's combined FSD-and-code output gets a similar one when it is built. The owner asked (2026-11-13) whether the page lists its confidence and related information "like a note --- --- at the top"; it didn't: the header held the area's name and two flow counts, and only single flow files said what they were built from and listed their guesses. The page's note is a YAML block between `---` lines: the area; that it is made by code with no AI; the commit it was built from; the settings that change what it shows (hub callers, call depth, D281's framework share, D282's chart cap); clicks charted, listed in one line, and too large to draw; steps certain, guessed (🟡) and can't tell (❓). Each click also carries one line with its own guessed and can't-tell counts. Counts, not one percentage: "92% confident" would read as measured accuracy when it is only the share of steps found without guessing, and the measured number is the bar's (D280). No date, so rebuilds stay byte for byte; the commit says what the page was built from. The full list of what code couldn't see stays at the foot of the page. For the combined output the owner said "add something similar also"; what it holds (the FSD imported and its version, the commit, the judge's model and effort (D161), requirements matched, differing and not found, the run's cost) is settled when layer 5 is grilled. Owner: "add something similar also i mean" (2026-11-13), taken as yes to the page's note on the recommendation and a request for the same on the combined output. Cost: a block of YAML sits above every page's title in viewers that don't hide front matter; the counts say how much was guessed, not how much is right.
- D284 · Every box and every table row on a layer 4 page carries the step number of its flow file, so a developer goes from the page straight to the detail: "saves Series (step 133)", "calls SkyHookProxy, outside (step 37)", and a scenario row names the steps it passes and the step where it stops. The owner opened the Add click's flow file (Sonarr, Series › Add New) and asked whether the meaningful steps are shown: on the page they are the chart, but in the flow file they sit unmarked among 137 steps (32 shared helpers, 9 library calls, about 87 of URL building, mapping and validation plumbing; the five that change something are steps 37, 58, 70, 133 and 135), and the page gave only `file:line` into the code, not a way into the flow file. Step numbers are already in the flow file's JSON, so this is code only and free; gate 1 of D280 can match a flow file's changes and stops to the page by step number instead of by label. Chosen over also marking the meaningful steps inside the flow file ("⚡ 133. saves Series"), which changes layer 3's finished output and bumps its contract with every consumer to review (D20) for a job the page already does. Owner: "okay yeah" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: one more token per box and row, so labels get a little longer; a step number is only good for the build it came from, which the page's note pins by commit (D283).
- D285 · Layer 4's AI machinery is removed in the build round, now that the page has no AI step (changes D276, which kept it and pointed it at chart labels; D278 took the labels away from the AI). Kept: `pages summary`, which layer 5's judge reads, and `pages chart`, which becomes the code that writes each area's `page.md`. The `components.pages` switch stays but loses its AI commands and its "costs money" reason; it stays off by default, like other code-made files no one has read yet (workflow files, test journeys), until gate 3 of D280 passes. - D286 · The chart cap's default is 60 nodes, not 40 (changes D282's number; the rule and the setting `pages.chartCap` stay). The owner saw Kavita's login click drawn at 53 nodes: it reads as one long top-to-bottom line (refusals, the user save, then 12 queued background jobs each with its follow-on box, then the outside calls), and the 12 jobs, which the table alone buries in rows, are what a developer would want to see. At 60, those 11 charts are drawn and 12 (about 10%) stay table-only. Chosen over keeping 40. Owner: "Yes, 60" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: a 50-node chart is tall and needs scrolling; repeated runs such as the 12 queued jobs take half a chart, and nothing shortens them, since D279 folds nothing; the biggest clicks still get no picture.
- D287 · A one-line flow's own stops are listed under its line on the page, each with its step number, condition and `file:line`; its shared guards (stops at a line as many start flows as `hubCallers` have, as for charted clicks) are one count, "+ N shared guards" (settles the clash between D271's one-liner and D280's gate 1, which asks for every stop on the page). Counted on the three clones after the build: one-line flows have 424 stops in Sonarr (382 shared guards, 42 their own), 551 in Kavita (374, 177) and 4,104 in nopCommerce (3,671, 433); the own ones are mostly redirects and refusals, such as nopCommerce's `RedirectToAction("List")` (97) and Kavita's return to `/home` (29), which are what a developer opens the page to explain. Gate 1 then checks one-line flows' own stops like any other, and stops counting them apart. Chosen over leaving them out and narrowing D280 to changing clicks. Owner: "Yes, list them" (2026-11-13), on the recommendation. Cost: pages grow by about three lines per area on nopCommerce and more on Kavita; a one-liner's shared guards are only counted, so which guards they are needs the flow file.
- D288 · Fixes to what a layer 4 page says, from the fresh check of the build (2026-11-13; 4 defects, 9 risks, 5 nits, every traced `file:line` and step number right). (1) A loop is drawn once, with everything inside it nested under it; Sonarr's scene-mapping handler drew its `foreach` twice, so the saves at steps 24–26 looked like they ran after the loop and the "no items" row said it passed them. (2) A field check run in the middle of the flow says "checked at step N" (the step that calls its validator); only checks bound to the request say "before the steps" (Sonarr K8, K9 run at step 131, after the outside call at step 37). (3) Scenario rows use the chart's own diamond ids, so "D2" means the same thing in both (Kavita login's row D1 was diamond D8). (4) A stop with no step takes the step whose `at` is the same line (nopCommerce billing address, `CheckoutController.cs:633` is step 107). (5) Saves with the same words on different lines stay separate boxes. (6) Conditions print outer first, as the code nests them. (7) The main path says "steps that can run", since some run only on one branch. (8) Nits: no empty "()" after "runs later, in the background"; a negated simple condition prints plainly, "not: !hasLicense" becomes "hasLicense" (corrected 2026-11-14: the first wording, "unless x", kept the `!` and printed "unless !hasLicense"), while a combined one stays "not: (…)". Left as notes in `docs/pages.md`: a screen's changes are excused when a click on it has a change at the same line (R2), and a stop inside a `catch` carries the step of the call it catches (R9). Owner: "Yes, that list" (2026-11-14), on the recommendation. Cost: one fix round, about 100–150K tokens; pages on all three clones change and are rewritten.
- D289 · Gate 1 of D280 is tightened, from the same check: a start flow with no readable file fails the gate instead of passing quietly; a change with neither a step nor a code line is reported as unmatched; a change is matched against the change boxes (or, for an undrawn chart, the list of changes), not against any "step N" on the chart, so "skips step 133" no longer counts; saves inside shared code reached from a flow are checked too; and one test covers `areas check` writing `page.md` and the switch turning it off. A hand edit already could not pass (the page must equal a fresh rebuild); these close the generator bugs the gate could not see. Owner: "Yes, that list" (2026-11-14). Its counts name each change once, however many flows reach it through shared code (after the fix round nopCommerce counted 126,325 changes, the same shared saves once per flow). - D290 · Gate 2 of D280 runs on the clicks code picked, however big their flow files, and the flow reader may search a big flow file instead of reading it whole, as a developer would. The picks (3 per repo from the pilot areas, sorted by flow-file size, one drawn from each third with seed 280; `pick.py` and `picked.json` in the session notes, `layer4-build/gate2/`) have flow files of 1.22M characters against 168K for their page parts; Sonarr's series search job alone is 812K, and 15 of Sonarr's 23 large-third flows are over 200K. The page reader reads only each click's part of its page; the flow reader reads the click's `<flow>.md` and the shared flow files it links, not the source; the grader reads the source. Chosen over capping flow files at 200K and redrawing, which would drop most of the big clicks the page is meant for. Owner: "Yes, run it" (2026-11-14), on the recommendation. - D300 · Layer 3 marks a thrown error as handled, not a stop, when a caller higher on the path has a `try` whose `catch` takes it: a bare `catch` or `catch (Exception)` takes everything, a typed catch takes that type and the types built on it (by the compiler's type information), and a catch that throws again (`throw;`, `throw ex`) still lets it stop. The page lists handled errors in a short list of their own ("handled: IndexerException at `BroadcastheNetParser.cs:26`, caught by `HttpIndexerBase.cs:252`, carries on") instead of in the stop rows. Found by gate 2 (2026-11-14, split 1 of D299): Sonarr's series search job lists all 110 exits as throws, many of them inside one indexer's parser, caught per indexer in `HttpIndexerBase.cs` (catches at lines 224–271), so the job carries on while the page said "Stops". Chosen over leaving them as stops. Owner: "Yes, handled" (2026-11-14), on the recommendation. Cost: real work in the walk (each caller's try/catch ranges along the path); part of the same flows contract bump; a catch whose type can't be worked out leaves the error a stop, marked ❓; how many of the 110 are handled is known only once built.
- D301 · Layer 3 makes an ASP.NET Core access, action or resource filter that sets `context.Result` a stop, with the conditions on the way to it ("answers 400 BadRequest when `user.IsInRole("Read Only")`, `DisallowRoleAttribute.cs:28`"); the page shows it as a stop row and gate 1 checks it like any other; a filter on many endpoints becomes a shared guard, counted. Found by gate 2 (2026-11-14, split 3 of D299): Kavita's reading-list save carries `[DisallowRole("Read Only")]`, which refuses by setting `context.Result` (no throw, no early return), so the flow file had no stop and the page named it only on its Who line. Owner: "yes do it" (2026-11-14), on the recommendation. Cost: part of the same flows contract bump; nopCommerce's pages gain stop rows.
- D302 · Layer 3 marks a call into a known outside-service client library as an outside call instead of a package call: a default list of namespaces (Azure SDKs `Azure.*`, AWS `Amazon.*`, Stripe, Twilio, SendGrid, Brevo `brevo_csharp`/`sib_api_v3_sdk`, Avalara `Avalara.AvaTax`, MailKit and `System.Net.Mail.SmtpClient` for email) that a project can extend in its config. Found by gate 2 (2026-11-14, splits 5 and 7 of D299): nopCommerce's Azure Blob calls (`BlobContainerClient.CreateIfNotExistsAsync` and others) were in the flow file only as package calls; Avalara and Brevo, plugins reached through interfaces chosen at runtime, are not in the flow files at all, and following plugins is left out: they stay unseen, so those two answers stay wrong in the re-test. - D303 · The build round of D291–D297 and D300–D302 (2026-11-14) made these calls the decisions left open, on the owner's standing "keep going on my calls": (1) layer 2 gains facts in place, with no `code-graph` bump (`try`, `file-change`, a throw's `exceptionTypes`, a package call's `ns`, D301's wider filter exit); graphs need `graph build --full`, since detector code isn't in a project's fingerprint; (2) a filter setting any `context.Result` is a stop, an `EmptyResult` too (kind `Result`, named by its type), not only a refusal; (3) a `catch … when (…)` doesn't count as taking an error (it stays a stop), and a typed catch never takes an error whose own type is unknown (a bare `throw;`), with no ❓ (❓ only when the catch's type can't be worked out); (4) the climb to a catching caller stops at an HTTP hop, a filter, an on-open hook or the flow's start, and an error thrown inside a shared flow is checked only against that shared flow's own code; (5) a handled error whose catch itself ends the request reads "caught by X, which then answers BadRequest (…)" instead of "carries on" (Kavita's reading-list save: `catch (KavitaException) { return BadRequest(…); }`); (6) a push through an app's wrapper is 🟡, one through a wrapper called with a name made at runtime is ❓ "pushes a message named at runtime"; (7) "changes in shared code" widen from saves to pushes, file writes and may-saves (D260's list), so pages and gate 1 see them; (8) receivers are listed for the flow file's own `message` and `runs-later` links, also when the send is a framework change or has no drawn change; messages sent inside shared flows a click continues into aren't followed for receivers; (9) a timer link points at the function the timer runs; (10) D302 matches a package call's namespace and type, so `System.Net.Mail.SmtpClient` stays covered by D199's "outside SMTP" ❓ (.NET's own calls aren't package facts), and every call into a listed library counts, local builders like `GetBlobClient` too; (11) a scenario row's positive wording reads a negated simple condition plainly ("When `refreshConnections` is false"). Cost: (3) and (4) leave some handled errors as stops; (10) over-counts outside calls a little; charts over the 60-node cap rose from 14/11/29 to 54/27/45 (Sonarr/Kavita/nopCommerce), mostly from D296's carried changes and D292's pushes.
- D304 · Fixes from fresh check 2 of the gate 2 build (2026-11-14, `layer4-build/check2-findings.md` in the session notes; 6 defects, 4 risks): (1) a catch that throws anything, a new wrapped error included, lets the error go on ("turned into RecycleBinException at `RecycleBinProvider.cs:125`"), never "carries on" (Sonarr 425 entries); (2) .NET file writes and deletes carry their conditions like any step (Kavita had 0 of 164); (3) `SignInManager.CheckPasswordSignInAsync` and other `Check…SignIn` calls are "may save", since lockout writes the user store; (4) gate 1 doesn't count "as in step N" as mentioning step N, and matches a change by its step and its kind word; (5) a filter stop names the endpoint's attribute value ("answers 400 for the Read Only role"); (6) receivers of a message sent in a shared flow the click continues into are listed (Sonarr 69 flows, nopCommerce 271), and gate 1 checks each receiver per send; (7) a handled error says only what is known: "caught by X (file:line)", then "and answers/returns …" when the catch or the code right after it ends the request with an error, never "carries on" by default (nopCommerce place-order catches 28 entries, Sonarr `DownloadClientCheck.cs:46` 127); (8) filters that only redirect (https, www, language code, sign-in) word their stop "sends the user to …"; (9) D302 skips local builders (`Get…Client`, `Create…Client`) and a step on a line D199 already has; (10) a typed catch facing an error of unknown type marks the stop ❓. - D305 · After the gate 2 re-test (33/36, one short): (1) an interface call whose implementations sit in plugin or add-on projects lists, as an outside call marked 🟡, each outside service those implementations call ("may call Avalara through the tax plugin `AvalaraTaxProvider`"), without walking the plugin's code into the flow (changes D302's "plugins stay unseen"); (2) what an exit passes is worked out from where it sits in the code (the path to it), not from step numbers, so an ending after the saves, such as nopCommerce edit product's redirect to List when `ModelState.IsValid` and `!continueEditing` (`ProductController.cs:1419`), reads "ends by sending the user to List, after the saves (steps …)" and not "Sent elsewhere … passes no change"; then the D298 re-test runs again on the same nine page parts. Cost: one Opus build (~100–150K) and another re-test (~250K); plugin implementations are found by the interface, so a plugin wired up some other way stays unseen. Built (2026-11-14) with these calls: a plugin or add-on project is one whose project file sits under a folder named `Plugins`, `Plugin`, `AddOns`, `Add-ons` or `Extensions` (any case), other than the caller's; its outside services are found by searching that project's own calls from the implementation (calls back into the app's core aren't searched), one 🟡 step per service at the calling line, and the existing walk into the implementation stays (its saves, such as Avalara's pickup Address, were already right in gate 2); pages carry these plugin outside calls from shared code like saves; an exit's passed changes are the steps written above it in its method's code (with all under them) under its own conditions too, and a redirect that passes changes reads "Ends after the work: …"; a redirect names its action (`RedirectToAction("List")` → List).
- D306 · After gate 3 (Kavita Reading Lists no, Sonarr Series partly): (1) layer 3 stops a click's walk where code only waits for a later user action, so a selection click doesn't take in the bulk-action menu that runs on another click; the builder finds why Kavita's `selectItem`, `handleSelection` and `onTouchEnd` reach every bulk action and cuts it there, generically; (2) on the page, heavy shared work, a shared flow reached by 3 or more of the area's start flows or longer than 150 steps, is shown once in a part of its own ("Shared work: RefreshSeries"), and each click that reaches it gets one box "runs RefreshSeries (see its part): N changes, M stops"; gate 1 checks the shared part's changes and stops there and the link from each click (changes D279's "folds nothing": nothing is dropped, it moves to one place); (3) start flows with the same title in one area add what tells them apart (component or handler class, then the code line); (4) identical change lines within one click's shared-code list appear once with all their steps. - D308 · A scenario row says only how it differs from the main path, so long flows' tables stop repeating every step (changes D284's and D297's row wording, not what they name). Found checking D306's build before re-running gate 3 (2026-11-14): Sonarr's Series page is still 2.4M characters, and 1.85M of it is scenario rows; RefreshSeries' table alone is 606K, 145 rows of ~4K each, every row listing every step it passes, every step it skips and every skipped change in full words ("skips SkyHookProxy calls outside the app … (step 23), saves Series (step 78), …" for 87 changes). Rule: the main row lists its steps once, as now; another row lists whichever is shorter, the steps it passes or the main-path steps it skips ("passes steps 2, 917–923, skips the rest"), with runs of consecutive steps as ranges; its ending names the skipped changes in words when there are 5 or fewer (D297's "skips saves X, which run only when it is true" stays for small rows), otherwise names the changes it does make ("makes only: sends SeriesRefreshStartingEvent (step 2); skips the main path's other 86 changes") or, when it makes more than it skips, lists the skipped ones by step number only ("skips 12 changes: steps 78, 80, …"). Stops and their conditions are untouched; gate 1 checks changes against boxes and change lists, not rows (D289), so it is unchanged. Chosen over moving giant jobs to files of their own, which hides the size instead of removing the repetition. Cost: one Opus build (~150–200K tokens); a big row's skipped changes are named by step only, so a reader looks them up in the Changes list above the table.
- D418 · **D414–D417 built (2026-11-19, not committed); the build's own calls** (owner, 2026-11-19: "Do this task here", the build card; the details my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) **D414:** `onDemand` is set by code on each `fsd rulings` from the review (every finding of the entry a control, `item` with a dot); `rulings` v2 and `truth-map` v2 gain it as optional, no bump (additive, as D371, D410). A listed entry keeps a number, after every question, so `fsd rule` can still take an answer (D414 (4)); the view shows the number beside it and never asks. (2) **D417:** "a new bare keep" means one whose `ruling` line isn't committed (git blame), so a file holding old bare keeps is never refused; `needsWords` in the report and the map. A Jira reply "the code is right" on undocumented code counts as a bare keep. (3) **D415:** an area folder is `NN-<slug of the chapter heading>` in FSD order (`07-7-reading-lists`), a module file `<section number>-<slug>.md` (`m<k>` for a sheet or unnumbered section), code no section covers `zz-not-in-the-spec/screen-<slug>.md`; the headline of a question is the review's first note, cut at a word (code can't rank or summarise, so the index lists counts, not "the biggest differences"); `truth-map.md` is deleted at `rulings-check` so no stale copy is read; `TruthView.ReadAll` gives the view as one text for Claude. (4) **D416:** the picker reads the flow file directly by D273's categories rather than layer 4's chart builder, which needs the area files a client repo may not have; caps 8 changes and 8 stops (Kavita's 475 flows: p95 and p90); the writer runs with no tools; the chart is the journey in order with each edge case a dashed branch from the start (code can't order a stop among the changes); a code name is a dotted name, a call, an inner capital or snake_case not in the step's own text. (5) **Free check on a copy of the Kavita clone (no AI):** 118 entries, 64 listed (53 on screens no chapter covers), **41 questions up front** (8 open, 33 answered) instead of 117 numbered; 11 areas, 64 module files, the view 195 KB against `truth-map.md`'s 493 KB. **Found:** the planter's committed rulings hold **70 bare keeps**, which D417 now counts as blank (7 of the 8 open questions); none of the nine tickets' cited entries is a bare keep (their `bug-proof.json`), so the 9 of 9 should stand, not re-run. (6) **Fresh check (D158, Sonnet, ~135K tokens, no AI calls): no crash or numbering fault; 9 fixed:** a keep's sentence never reached the FSD (`fsd write` now places it as restated words, `writtenFrom.ruling` may be `keep`); the words' hash held each pick's line, so an edit above a flow would have paid for it again (lines left out); the index read another document's older map and linked to views not written (only this version's maps, links only to a written view); the view wasn't rewritten before a retry the driver might never reach; the code-name check let SCREAMING_SNAKE and paths through and refused "e.g."; stray `**` in the index's kinds; a backtick in code or a bracket in a title broke the Markdown; the index's anchors (now explicit, by doc id); a note with a line break escaped its quote. **Left:** `flow-words.json` keeps entries for flows no longer linked (never used, since their hash differs); the run's `failed` stage is never set, as `fsd write`'s. Tests: 2,003 pass (1,994 before, 9 new). **Why:** the decisions as made, built. **Cost:** free; D416's wording run and its measurement not run (paid, asked first).
- D417 · **"Keep" comes with the PM's sentence: a bare keep is refused** (owner, 2026-11-19: "Keep needs a sentence", over a bare keep allowed or a bare keep meaning unclear, on the recommendation; the details my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) A `keep` ruling on code the spec doesn't mention needs `statement`, one sentence in the PM's words of what the feature should do, as `restated` already does; `rulings-check` refuses a new bare keep. (2) `fsd rule <doc> <n> keep` without `--words` is refused and asks for the sentence; the PM view asks `keep "…"` (D415). (3) A `Ruling: keep` reply from Jira with no sentence is listed and asked back, never drafted (D374). (4) A bare keep already committed is listed by `rulings-check` as needing words and counts as blank (unclear for `ticket prove`) until it has them; the file isn't refused, so nothing already ruled is lost. (5) The sentence judges tickets as D411 says (backed when the ticket quotes at least four of its words and the code doesn't do what they say) and goes into the spec as restated words do (`fsd write`, D379). **Why:** a bare keep tells `ticket prove` the code is right as it is, so a real bug ticket on a kept feature ("Remove Read also removes items other users read") is turned away with no test. **Cost:** each keep costs the PM a sentence; with D414 only the reviewer's own left-out findings, whole screens and controls a ticket reached are asked, so few; not built.
- D416 · **The PM view's actor, journey and edge cases: code picks the steps, an AI words each in one line, code checks it; the words are trusted by one frozen measurement, then traced** (owner, 2026-11-19: "Code picks, AI words" and "Measure once, then trace", each over the alternatives, on the recommendation; answers D415's open point; the details my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) **Code picks** from each flow linked to a numbered question (never the listed controls, D414) by D273's fixed rules: who may start it and who is refused, each thing that changes the world (a save, a delete, a message sent, work queued, an outside call), and each way it stops early with its condition; the flowchart is drawn by code from the same items, as D273. (2) **An AI words them (D60):** the CLI prepares each flow's picked items with ids, `stangent-view-writer` (read-only, capped) writes one plain line per item, at most one sentence, and the CLI checks: every line names one picked item, every picked item has a line, no line is extra, no line holds a code name the item didn't (a class, a method, a table), and the journey and edge cases stay within a cap set from counts when built (the mock-up's Remove Read: 4 steps and 3 edge cases from 85 steps and 8 scenarios). One retry; an item still failing shows the code's own label, marked, never dropped (D111). (3) **Paid, with a choice (D126):** `--max-cost` or `none`; reused while a flow's items are unchanged, so a refresh pays only for changed flows; my estimate about $1–2 per document the first time, not measured. Without a run the view shows the questions with the code labels folded, and says how to run it. (4) **Trust (D18, D61):** before the view is called done, one measurement on Kavita and Sonarr: code samples 100 worded lines per app (fixed seed), a fresh agent judges each true, false or can't tell against its item's code, and the owner spot-checks 10 of them; the bar, frozen now: at least 95% of judged lines true on each app, and the owner's 10 agree with the judge. Cost asked before it runs (two wording runs and two judge runs, my estimate $4–6). After it, no per-run checker: each line keeps its item's `file:line` in the folded developer section, so a developer can trace a line the PM doubts. **Why:** a PM can't rule on 85 code steps; D270's rejected pages were paragraphs, here every line is pinned to one step and nothing else is written. **Cost:** a paid step in front of the PM, and a wrong line the sample didn't catch can mislead a ruling until someone traces it; not built.
- D415 · **The PM's view of the truth map is files: one index at the root of the mapping, one summary per area, one file per module** (owner, 2026-11-19: "i want index of the mapping at the root, and i dont need summary at the top of each module, just create one summarize module per area", after reading a hand-made mock-up of Kavita's chapter 7; the details my calls on the owner's standing word). For the PM it replaces the single `truth-map.md` (D366, D371); `truth-map.json`, for agents, is unchanged (contract `truth-map` v2). (1) **`specs/fsd/index.md`**, the root: every document with when it was compared (app commit, spec version), its open and answered questions and how many controls are listed (D414); per document the counts by kind in plain words (promised, not built · built differently · built, not in the spec · looks right), the biggest differences one line each, and its areas with their counts; the actors across the app, each with the areas it appears in. (2) **One folder per area** under the document (`specs/fsd/<doc>/view/<NN-area>/`): an area is an FSD top-level chapter, since the PM thinks in the spec's terms, and the code no section covers is one more area, by screen. Its `summary.md` holds the area's modules with their question numbers, its actors, screens, journeys, how sure, the differences in it, and the controls listed but not asked. (3) **One file per module** (a section with text of its own): the section's text, then only its questions, no summary block: each with a headline that says the difference ("25 items in the spec, 10 in the app"), "Spec says" and "App does", how sure (every run or one; a match's confidence), the actor, journey and edge cases from the linked flow, a flowchart where a flow is linked, the answers with what each causes ("`code`: 10 is right; the spec changes to say 10, and a ticket asking for 25 is turned away"), and the code paths folded at the bottom for developers. (4) Question numbers stay per document, as `fsd rule <doc> <n>` reads them (D371), so a module shows e.g. 21–24. (5) Written by code at `fsd rulings-check` from the same inputs, the same bytes each time; the single `truth-map.md` goes, and its readers (`/stangent:rule`, the docs) move to the new files. **Settled by D416 (2026-11-19):** whether actor, journey and edge cases are put in plain words by an AI step checked by code (the mock-up's "Read Only users are refused"), or show code's own labels as today ("user who passes DisallowRole(roles = ["Read Only"])") (`questions.md`). Mock-up: `pm-view-mock/` in this group's session notes, made by hand from the 2026-11-18 review. **Why:** the owner's call; today's one file is long, shows link ids and 85 code steps per flow, and has no summary a PM can start from. **Cost:** free, code only; not built.
- D414 · **Left-out controls are listed, not asked: the PM is asked about one only when a ticket lands on it** (owner, 2026-11-19: "the on demand thing lets record it", after the worked example on Kavita's chapter 7; answers D404 (5), and for left-out controls the 2026-11-05 question "Ask for a PM's ruling only when a ticket reaches the section?"). (1) **Asked up front, numbered:** gaps, conflicts, each section's matches, the reviewer's own left-out findings, and a whole screen the checklist left out. Kavita: about 38 questions instead of 105 (today all 71 undocumented entries are numbered). (2) **Listed, not asked:** a control the checklist left out (D403, D404) keeps its entry in `rulings.yaml`, grouped by link as now, marked as asked on demand; the PM's view lists it under its screen with no number (D415), `rulings-check` counts it apart ("listed, not asked: N"), and `done` doesn't wait for it. (3) **When a ticket lands on one,** the bundle carries its blank entry, `ticket prove` says unclear as for any blank entry, and the questions draft asks the PM in Jira about that control; the `Ruling:` reply fills the entry (D364, D374), which counts from then on like any other. (4) An answer the PM gives to a listed entry before any ticket is kept as usual. **Why:** 360 (Kavita) and about 500 (Sonarr) controls are too many to rule, nearly every answer would be "keep", and a bare "keep" tells `ticket prove` the code is right as it is, so a bulk "keep" would turn away a real bug ticket on that control (contradicted, no test). **Cost:** a control the spec should cover and nobody tickets stays unruled, so the whole-spec audit covers rules and screens, not every button; not built.
- D413 · **Roslyn 5.9.0 → 5.12.0** (owner, 2026-11-19: "Yes, bump it", after D412). The .NET 11 SDK's Razor compiler is built for compiler 5.12; on 5.9 a .NET 11 project's views never compiled (D412 now says so in `loadErrors`). Checked: all tests pass on 5.12; the shop fixture built in a container with only SDK 11.0.100 and no `global.json` gives 26 view points and no Razor load error. **Why:** client repos move to .NET 11; the SDK's Razor compiler only runs on a compiler at least as new as the one it was built for, so stangent should track the newest SDK's Roslyn. **Cost:** free.
- D412 · **CI's Razor failures: the shop fixture pins the repo's SDK, and a Razor compiler too new for stangent is named** (owner, 2026-11-19: "Yes, fix them", then Auto-fix on PR #4). (1) **Cause:** the tests copy `tests/fixtures/shop` to a temp folder, outside the repo's `global.json`, so it builds with the machine's newest SDK; CI's runner (ubuntu-26.04) has .NET 11.0.100, whose Razor compiler needs compiler 5.12, and stangent ships 5.9: the generator never loads and all 27 views vanish (10 tests). Macs and the local Linux container had no SDK newer than 10.0.x. (2) **Fix:** `ShopCopy` copies the repo's `global.json` into the copy, as a client repo pins its SDK. (3) **Product:** a project with `.cshtml` views that gets no Razor output now gets a load error saying why (the compiler didn't load, or needs compiler X while stangent has Y; pin an older SDK), never silently no views. (4) Also fixed: `FsdSourceTests`' cache test depended on test order (the process-wide read cache); it forgets that cache first. **Left:** a client repo on .NET 11 lost its Razor views on Roslyn 5.9 (→ D413). **Why:** CI must match what runs locally; a lost view is a blind spot and must be said (D111). **Cost:** free.
- D411 · **A "keep" ruling with the PM's words keeps the feature working as the words say** (owner, 2026-11-19: "Yes, fix it", after D410's re-run left KAVR-8 unclear). (1) The reproducer's input says: an entry ruled "keep this code" with the PM's words keeps the feature, working as the words say; judge the ticket against them, and if the code doesn't do what they say, the words back the ticket (quote at least four). `bundle.md` labels them "the PM's words (how the kept code should work)". (2) Code: the check that refuses a backed verdict on a code-right or keep entry is skipped only for backed on **keep** whose quote is at least four words of that entry's PM words. Every other pairing keeps the check: the other rulings' names already say which way the PM meant, and code can't tell which way words point (a fresh check, Sonnet ~75K tokens, found the first, wider version let contradicted on "remove" through when quoting the words, and a one-word statement switch the check off; narrowed). Tests: backed on keep quoting the words stands; quoting the note, or under four words, or contradicted on remove quoting its words, gets the retry. Left: a statement that itself says "the code is right" would still let backed on keep through if quoted. (3) **KAVR-8 re-proved ($0.25):** backed on entry 128, reproduced by a failing test in `GenreRepositoryTests` (the first answer's test failed on another assertion; the retry fixed it). **Score: 9 of 9 verdicts right, 3 of 3 backed reproduced, 0 wrong tickets tested, replies 2 of 2** (`score-d411.json`, result.md's fourth section). **Caution:** D410 and D411 were both built after seeing KAVR-8 fail, aimed at it. **The other eight re-proved under them (owner, 2026-11-19, $0.62): every verdict unchanged, still 9 of 9, 0 wrong tickets tested** (`score-reprove-all.json`); the three backed ones reproduced on tests earlier runs left in the clone (byte-identical), so only the verdicts are fresh. **Why:** a PM who says "keep it" and then says how it should behave has said what's right; the label alone threw that away. **Cost:** $0.25 paid; a narrow fresh check.
- D410 · **A truth-map entry reaches a ticket through the flow its code is on, or the screen that flow opens, not only by file** (owner, 2026-11-19: "fix the kavr-8 also", after D409 (3); the details my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) **The ticket's own code** is the method a found file's quoted line sits in (the smallest graph node holding it) and each symbol point's span; not the whole file: by file, `SeriesRepository.cs` sits in 53 of Kavita's flows and KAVR-1 and KAVR-3 would have taken in 17 unrelated entries each, KAVR-8 seven. (2) **Its flows:** every flow with a step on a line inside that code (`specs/flows/index.json`), plus the flows that continue into a shared one among them (one hop). An entry whose link is one of those flows, or the screen a screen-opens flow among them opens, applies, with `via` `flow:<id>` or `screen:<id>` (file matches `via: file`); the bundle's `truth.reach` lists the flows. (3) **Guard:** more than 5 flows beside shared ones and only file matches count (`reach.tooMany`, said in `bundle.md`). (4) `ticket-bundle` v3 gains optional `via` and `reach`, no bump (additive, as D371). (4b) **After a fresh check (Sonnet, ~78K tokens; 3 defects, 2 minor, all fixed):** a flow file or index line that doesn't read is skipped, never a failed bundle; a quoted line in no method (a field, a `using`) or reading the same in two methods gives no span; a path the JSON writer escapes is looked for both ways (not tested: no fixture path needs escaping); `bundle.md`'s too-many line no longer counts shared flows. Left as risks: `node` links never match through flows; two screen-opens flows on one point name the first in `via`; every flow file that mentions a ticket file is read per bundle. (5) **Free check on Kavita's nine tickets:** the key's entry is in the bundle for 9 of 9 (was 8); KAVR-8 gets entry 128 by its screen and two route entries (45, 127) by the screen's flow; KAVR-4 gains one entry by flow beside its file matches; the other seven unchanged. **KAVR-8 re-proved (owner: "Yes, re-run it", $0.38):** its entry now reaches the verdict, which is still unclear: the planter ruled entry 128 `keep` with PM words saying what the ticket expects, and the reproducer read `keep` as "the code is right as it is", against the words; so 8 of 9 stands, finder misses 0 (`score-d410.json`, result.md's third section). **Why:** on Kavita 115 of 118 entries cite only frontend files (the left-out list is by screen), so a ticket whose code is backend missed nearly all of them; the flows already join the two. **Cost:** free to build; flow files read once per bundle (those that mention a ticket file); three tests.

- D408 · **The truth-map verdict comes before the test check** (owner, 2026-11-18: "yes go ahead with fix 1", after D407). With no test target (no unit-test project covers the code, no `tickets.app`) but a truth map entry that can back the ticket, `ticket prove` still asks the reproducer, for a verdict only: the input says there's no test target and to return `stopped`; code refuses any other outcome (one retry).
