- D405 · **Matches come back from the checklist, and for testing Claude stands in for the PM on them** (owner, 2026-11-18: "for now us testing, i want u to do the confirmation, just confirm most and pick out some scenarios that says it didn't match"). (1) **Matches from the checklist (code):** a checklist item a run answered covered with a checked quote, and no run left out, becomes a `match` finding at merge (section, the quote, the item's `file:line`, `item`), joined across runs by item; a screen item has no line, so it gives none. Code checked the quote and the line only; whether the code does what the sentence says is the PM's (D402 (2)). Rulings group them by section and quote, a difference on the same sentence wins (D366). No contract change: `match` was already in `fsd-review` v3 and `rulings` v2. (2) **Kavita, re-merged free from D404's saved runs:** 51 matches, 26 match entries in 13 sections; with the 366 left-out findings grouped by link into 71 entries, `rulings.yaml` holds 117 entries in all. (3) **Claude's test rulings (blind: chosen without reading the FSD sentences or the key):** each section's matches confirmed (13 answers), and the first match in three sections where the review also found a conflict (3.1, 9.1, 10.3; entries 9, 54, 73) answered no: 23 confirmed, 3 not a match, gaps, conflicts and left out left blank (the D375 planter's). Left uncommitted in the clone until the owner says. They are a test fixture, not a stakeholder's judgement: they count toward no bar. Why: the owner's call, to test the truth map and `ticket prove` with matches present. Cost: the three "no"s may be wrong about the code on purpose; `ticket prove` must follow the ruling, not its own reading. **Fresh check (Sonnet, ~93K tokens): no bugs;** fixed: two runs covering one control with different sentences now stay two matches (Kavita 51 → 52 findings, entries still 26, rulings carried over); left as noted: a reviewer's own match (not asked for since D401) wouldn't join a checklist match on the same sentence, and a ruling entry grouping many controls takes the union of their runs for its confidence.
- D404 · **The checklist is its own AI call beside the review, which goes back to D401's prompt unchanged** (owner, 2026-11-18: "Yes, split it", after D403's live runs: undocumented 2 → 8 of 10, conflicts 8–9 → 1–3, the reviewer stopping at 25–39 turns). Design (details my calls on the owner's standing word): (1) **Each review run has two parts, each one AI call with its own one retry:** the review (`fsd-reviewer.md` exactly as D401, the checklist parts taken out) and the list (a new prompt `fsd-lister.md` and agent `stangent-fsd-lister`: the FSD file, code's checklist, the same read-only limits; it answers each item covered with a checked quote or left out, as D403 (3)). (2) `fsd review --run N` prepares both (`run-N/` and `run-N/list/`); `fsd review-check --run N` checks whichever part is waiting, and after the review is recorded it names the lister as the next step, so the driver's loop runs both without a new command; the run is checked when both are. With no inventory there is no list part, as before. (3) The lister runs under the reviewer's model and effort (`ai.steps.stangent-fsd-reviewer`, one setting for the review) and gets `fsd.reviewBudget` of its own. (4) The merge, `review.json` (v3) and `review.md` are unchanged: a run's findings are its review's and its list's left-out items. (5) **Not decided here (answered by D414, 2026-11-19):** how about 370 left-out items reach the PM (one ruling each, or by screen); raised with the owner after the split is measured. Why: inside one call the list crowded out the conflict hunt (D403's live runs); apart, each does what it measured best at. Cost: two calls per run, about $0.5–0.8 more per run on Kavita; measured live on Kavita (asked first), then Sonarr. **Built 2026-11-18, not committed** (my calls): the list part's answer has its own schema `fsd-list-output.v1.json` (the review's output back to v2; D403's `fsd-review-output.v3` removed before any commit); the list part's state moves on the state module as `<doc>/review/run-N/list`; `review-status` shows each run's list stage; a lister with no answer after its retry leaves every item not checked and the run recorded; `AiSettings` lists `stangent-fsd-lister`, resolved from the reviewer's step; the driver names the step's own agent in its system-prompt line. Free check: the driver with a fake `claude` on Kavita, 4 calls (reviewer, then lister, per run), recorded and merged, $0. **Fresh check (D158, Sonnet, ~100K tokens, no AI calls): no stuck state or lost usage; 4 fixed:** a run whose review was recorded but whose lister hadn't run (cost guard stop, failed call) was prepared over on the next `fsd review`, paying for the review again (now it carries on with the lister, its retry prompt kept; `--again` still starts over); the driver's estimate counted one call per run (`review-status` gives `callsPerRun`, 2 with an inventory); the lister's usage was stamped as the reviewer's; the lister was chosen by a `checklist.md` in the folder (now by the folder being the list part). Left: `checked.json` is written before the list state moves, so a crash between the two would add the left-out findings twice on the next check (not hit; a narrow window). Tests: 1,980 + 1 new. **Live (2026-11-18, $2.94, Kavita, method.md I): 27 and 25 of 30 per run, merged 27 (gap 10, conflict 9, undocumented 8)**, against 17–18 for D401 alone and 15–18 for D403 in one call; the review worked 86–103 turns, the lister 8–9; no retries. About 360 left-out findings (8 planted) still to decide how the PM sees (5). Kavita's key is worn: to confirm once on Sonarr. **Confirmed on Sonarr (2026-11-18, $1.91, method.md J): 29 and 29 of 30 (gap 10, conflict 9, undocumented 10).** The review's findings alone score 23 (Sonarr) and 19–22 (Kavita); the list adds 5–6 left-out items per run, and about 500 (Sonarr) / 340–360 (Kavita) unplanted left-out findings. Next: how those reach the PM (5).
- D403 · **"Left out" comes from a list code makes: every screen and control in scope, each answered covered (with the FSD's words) or left out** (owner, 2026-11-18: "i feel like we should list the things that was left out during the comparison of fsd and code no?", then "okay sure" to the worked example). Re-challenges D322's Kavita result ("not confirmed", 26 against 28 alone): then the agent alone found 9 of 10 left-out items, so a checklist had nothing to add; live, alone finds 2 of 10 (D401), so the trade has changed. Design (the details my calls on the owner's standing word, to be planned and fresh-checked when built): (1) **Code lists** the screens in scope (the inventory's routes under `fsd.scope`, as D400's `ScreensInScope`, kept in `d400.patch`) and every control on them (`specs/inventory.json` lines: buttons, fields, menu entries, with `file:line` and label; Kavita: 460 in all), each with an id. (2) **Code ticks off for free** a control whose label appears word for word in the FSD's text (normalised as the quote check), marked covered by code with the section. (3) **The agent answers the rest, item by item:** covered, quoting the FSD sentence that describes it (word for word in that section, checked by code as D341's quotes), or left out, with the code line (checked). A whole screen is answered the same way. (4) A covered answer that fails its quote goes to the one retry, then counts as left out; an item the agent skips is listed as "not checked", never dropped. (5) `review.md` gets a "Left out" list by screen and a coverage line (listed, covered by code, covered by the agent, left out, not checked); each left-out item is a finding the PM rules keep or remove (D361). Gaps and conflicts stay as D401 asks for them. Why: left-out is the kind a PM can't find by reading the FSD, and an agent working from the FSD's sentences has no list to walk (D401's runs: 2 of 10); code already knows every screen and control (D155). Cost: a long answer (a few hundred items on Kavita before code's ticks), about $0.50–1 per run; Kavita's key is worn by today's runs, so a good score there is confirmed once on Sonarr before it's trusted; D322 showed an agent marks items covered loosely unless the quote is checked, hence (3). **Built 2026-11-18, not committed** (`src/Stangent.Spec/Fsd/FsdReview.Checklist.cs`, `docs/spec.md` "The checklist"; my calls on the owner's standing word): (a) **Items:** a screen's own `lines` only (Kavita 465 = 443 in scope's lines + 22 screens; dialogs' and menus' lines aren't in D403's count), a control listed twice on one screen once, unnamed controls kept with their code name; ids `S03` / `S03.012`, made again each prepare, not permanent. (b) **Code's tick needs two words or more** (or a route, or a two-word screen title), whole words, case folded: one-word labels (`Save`, `All`, `Edit`, `Library`) are in almost any FSD and would tick off controls the FSD never describes (Kavita: 72 one-word-or-more ticks case-sensitive, 39 with this rule); the first section whose own text names it is recorded. (c) **The checklist is a file** beside `fsd.md` (`checklist.md`, `{{CHECKLIST_FILE}}`), as D401's FSD, not inline; its prompt parts sit between markers and drop out with no inventory, so D323's wording is unchanged otherwise. (d) **A left-out answer needs no evidence for a control** (code attaches the item's own line, which it made); a whole screen gives the line that defines its route. Code writes the finding's note naming the item, the reviewer's note after it, so the finding says what it is whatever the agent writes. (e) **Missing answers go to the one retry** with the other errors; after it nothing is sent back (covered-but-failed → left out with `why`; unanswered → not checked). (f) **Merge:** an item is left out if any run left it out (findings are unioned, so the same rule), with each run's result kept; left-out findings join by item only. `fsd-review` v2 → v3 (`checklist`, findings' `item`), `fsd-review-output` v2 → v3 (`checklist` optional); the truth map's row marked needs review (D20). The state-module test reserves `outcome`, so an item's field is `result`. (g) `AiCompare.IsPlainString` no longer throws on a nullable type (`["string","null"]`), which the v3 output schema's `note` has. **Fresh check (D158, Sonnet, ~109K tokens, no AI calls): 4 fixed:** runs where only some had a checklist made their left-out findings vanish from `review.md` (merge now refuses, `runs_differ`); two apps' screens on one route kept only the first's controls (now both); the root route `/` was ticked off by any stray slash (never ticked by code now); a checklist part that broke the schema (`evidence: null`, more than 6 lines, over 3,000 answers) threw away the run's real findings (the checklist's schema errors go to the retry apart from the findings, odd answers are read one by one as not checked, and the limits are loosened). Not changed: a left-out screen with no route line after the retry keeps `evidence: []` (allowed by `fsd-review` v3; nothing is dropped); a covered quote isn't tied to the item's label (D403's design: the quote is checked, whether it describes the item is the PM's to see); odd value types in `inventory.json` (stangent's own contract). Tests: 7 new in `FsdReviewTests`. Free check: the driver with a fake `claude` on Kavita (the checklist file reaches the reviewer, no unfilled paths, no `--json-schema`, 426 answers recorded and merged, $0). **Live (2026-11-18, $1.91, Kavita, `benchmark/results/2026-11-18-fsd-review-live/` H): 15 and 18 of 30 per run, merged 18; undocumented 8 of 10 in each run (2 before), but conflicts 1–3 (8–9 before), so no gain in total.** The reviewer stopped after 25–39 turns (74–77 before), its effort going into 426 answers; it left out 351–366 items, about 370 left-out findings for the PM, 360 of all findings unplanted. The checklist works; inside the same call it crowds out the conflict hunt.
- D402 · **Matches stay off; when they return, the PM confirms each one; the end-to-end run waits; one app is enough for now** (owner, 2026-11-18, on what can't be trusted yet: "lets review later" for the end-to-end run, "its okay for now" for testing on one planted FSD, and on matches: "we can stay off for this, but the PM still has to mark if its true or false for the things right"). (1) Matches (D366) stay out of the review (D401). (2) When they come back, an AI's "the code does what this sentence says" is a claim like any finding, so each goes to the PM to mark true or false, the same as a gap or conflict, before the truth map counts the sentence as backed (D18: a person checks what code can't). (3) The full live run through the fixed hook (D401) waits for the owner's word. (4) Breadth (a second app, a real client FSD) isn't needed before the next step. Why: the owner's calls. Cost: until matches return, the truth map shows no sentence as backed by code.
- D401 · **The FSD review goes back to D323's measured prompt: three kinds, the FSD as a file, the answer written to a file; D400's coverage and the ask for matches come out of it** (owner, 2026-11-18: "sure", after D399's diagnostics: D323's prompt scored 18 of 30 on the same headless harness where the built command's scored 9, with or without structured output; and to "take them out" explained as the two extra jobs). (1) `fsd-reviewer.md` is D323's `alone/prompt.md` word for word but for no app name and three parts filled in: the scope (by code), the FSD's path and the answer's path (by the driver, `{{FSD_FILE}}`, `{{OUTPUT_FILE}}`). (2) `fsd review` writes `fsd.md` beside `input.md` (the input hash covers both); the driver copies it into a throwaway folder outside the repo, allows Read there and writes only there (`Edit(//folder/**)`: Claude Code's rule for Write), drops `--json-schema`, and copies the answer to `output.json` when it exists, also after the reviewer hit its cap. `review-check` and merge are unchanged; `fsd-review-output` stays v2 (it still accepts a match). (3) **D400 removed** before commit (its diff kept as `benchmark/results/2026-11-18-fsd-review-live/D-coverage/d400.patch`): code's screen list comes back later as its own measured step. (4) **Matches (D366) are no longer asked for**: the truth map gets none from new reviews until they return as a measured step, likely a pass of their own. Free check: the driver with a fake `claude` on Kavita (the FSD file with its 30 sections, no unfilled paths, no `--json-schema`, writes only to the folder, recorded and merged, $0). Why: the command's own prompt is what stopped the reviewer early (D399 (7), (8)). **Live (2026-11-18, $1.39): 17 and 18 of 30 per run, 0 and 2 unplanted; both runs together 20 (gap 8, conflict 10, undocumented 2)**, against 2–17 before (`benchmark/results/2026-11-18-fsd-review-live/` G). Both runs failed in the command because the driver's read-only hook (T.8) refused the answer file; their answers were taken from the refused calls and scored. Fixed: the hook allows the one folder `STANGENT_WRITE_DIR` names, set by the driver to the throwaway answer folder for the reviewer's call only (`ReadOnlyHookTests`; checked live with a cent-sized Haiku call each way). Not yet seen end to end live: a run that records and merges through the fixed hook. **Fresh check (D158, Sonnet, ~83K tokens): nothing lets a step write into the app; 6 fixed:** the hook takes `STANGENT_WRITE_DIR` only as an absolute path that is neither the working folder nor a folder holding it (a home folder left in a shell opens nothing); the reviewer may write only `output.json`, not the folder; an interrupt removes the answer folder (and the free re-check caught my first try deleting it before the copy); a run prepared before D401 is refused (`prepared_before`) and the driver stops if the FSD file is missing; the agent file lists Write and says it runs only through the driver; two stale comments. 1,972 tests; the fake-`claude` driver check records and merges. **Left: undocumented, 2 of 10 live against 9 measured.** Cost: matches gone for now; tuning stays on D323's wording, written before Kavita's key existed, so it is not fitted to Kavita.
- D400 · **The FSD reviewer must account for every section and every in-scope screen, and code checks it** (owner, 2026-11-18: "Code-checked coverage", over one call per chapter, after D399's runs stopped on their own choice at 2–17 of 30; the details my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) `fsd-review-output` v2 → v3: a required `coverage` beside `findings`: `sections` (each `{section, evidence}`) and `screens` (each `{screen, section}`). (2) **Every section with text of its own** must be in `coverage.sections` with at least one `path:line` that passes the findings' line check (a path alone doesn't count): the code the reviewer read for it. (3) **Code lists the screens in scope**, from `specs/inventory.json` (its screens' routes, kept when a route equals an `fsd.scope` prefix or starts with it plus `/`; every screen when the scope is empty), under "Screens in scope" in the input; each must be in `coverage.screens` with the section that describes it, or `null` and an undocumented finding with that screen. No inventory: no list, and `review.md` says the screens weren't checked by code. (4) A gap in coverage goes into the same one retry as the findings' errors. Still missing after it: the run is recorded with its findings and an `uncovered` list (sections, screens) in `checked.json`, and `review.md` names them per run, so nothing is silent. `fsd-review` (the merged file) stays v2. Why: D399 showed the live reviewer stops after a few findings with most of its turns unused; making it cite a real line for every section and answer for every screen is a floor code can check (D18), and the screen list is something code already knows (D155), so the agent no longer has to find the screens itself. **Fresh check (D158, Sonnet, ~83K tokens):** 7 defects, all fixed: an undocumented finding dropped by its own checks no longer accounts for a screen; a coverage that alone breaks the schema (an empty section id, empty evidence) no longer fails the run and loses its findings (it goes to the retry, then counts as covering nothing; evidence may be empty, 50 at most); a section listed twice or an unknown id no longer forces a retry; a screen listed twice holds when either entry does; a scope of `/` is the whole app; a screen's route is everything after the first `|`; a run prepared before D400 (no `askedCoverage` in its state) is checked without coverage. Tests: 22 in `FsdReviewTests`. **Live rerun (2026-11-18, $0.60): 6 of 30 merged (4 and 5 per run), worse than D399's A (13).** The reviewer met the floor by citing a line per section without checking it, so `review.md`'s "every section checked against code" sat beside a 5-of-30 run: the section half misleads (`benchmark/results/2026-11-18-fsd-review-live/` D-coverage). **Removed before commit (D401)**; its diff is `benchmark/results/2026-11-18-fsd-review-live/D-coverage/d400.patch`. Cost: a longer answer (about 30 more entries on Kavita); a cited line shows the reviewer opened code for the section, not that it checked every sentence; screens depend on the inventory being built.
- D399 · **Kavita's clone migrated for the live FSD review; the reviewer runs on Sonnet 5.5, medium; the old review stays out of git** (2026-11-18; owner: "okay sure lets do it" to the live review and the migration D398 (1) asked for; the rest my calls). (1) `~/stangent-bench/kavita` gets branch `bench/fsd-migrated`: `b0878e1` the planted FSD as imported, `f4abc65` `fsd migrate` (11 chapter files; `fsd export --whole` equals the old `document.md` but for its header; `fsd check` unchanged). (2) **The saved `review.json`, `rulings.yaml` and `truth-map.*` are not committed there**, moved to `~/stangent-bench/kavita-fsd-before-live-review/moved-from-clone/` (copies, plus the old `stangent.yaml`, beside it): the reviewer's worktree is the commit, its guard on `specs/` is a Read deny rule, and whether Grep and Glob obey it is still open (D341), so an earlier review in the commit could leak answers. D375's planter takes them back from there. (3) `ai.steps.stangent-fsd-reviewer` is Sonnet 5.5 at medium effort in the clone's `stangent.yaml`: D321–D324 measured Sonnet 5.5, and D161 allows no default. (4) First attempt (`--max-cost 8`): both runs refused by Claude Code's seven-day limit, $0 spent; the owner signed the CLI in to another account and it ran. (5) **Result: 11 of 30** (gap 6/10, conflict 5/10, undocumented 0/10) against the free check's 29, for $0.40; both runs stopped on their own after 21 and 38 turns (cap 60), and 9 of the 20 findings are matches (`benchmark/results/2026-11-18-fsd-review-live/`). It differs from the measured runs in four ways: no `fsd.scope` in the clone (the measured prompt named nine route prefixes), the `match` kind D366 added, a generic prompt, and headless medium effort. **Layer 5's "built" stands only on the free check; the live command is not yet as good as D321–D324 measured.** (6) **Reruns, one change at a time** (owner: "Yes, up to $3"; $2.42 spent): the scope set: 13 and 4 per run; plus high effort: 17 in one run, about 5× the cost, mostly matches; the scope without `match`: 2 and 4. **The reviewer stops on its own choice**, far under its caps ("Two conflicts confirmed so far. I'll write up the findings."); neither the match kind nor effort is the cause. What differs from every measured run is the harness (headless `claude -p`, structured output) and that nothing makes the agent cover each section and each screen in scope. The clone keeps the nine scope prefixes (a setup fix); its per-run cap is back to the default. How to make the review cover the FSD is the owner's call (asked). (7) **Diagnostic ($0.84): D323's prompt word for word through the same headless `claude -p`, writing a file instead of structured output: 18 of 30** (gap 8, conflict 8, undocumented 2; 2 of 20 unplanted; 104 turns, 268 s). The harness isn't the cause; the command's prompt or answer format is, most likely the `StructuredOutput` tool `--json-schema` gives, which ends the run when called (`benchmark/results/2026-11-18-fsd-review-live/` E-measured-prompt). (8) **Diagnostic 2 ($0.27): the command's own prompt (A's input) answered by writing a file: 9 of 30**, 29 turns. So the answer format isn't the main cause; the command's prompt is (one run each; A ranged 4–13). Today's paid spend $4.53. Why: the live run needs a migrated, committed FSD (D383), and its score means nothing if the agent could read the last review. Cost: the clone carries a bench branch; the D375 files live outside it until the planter runs.
- D398 · **Phase fs closed: three calls made writing its end-of-phase report** (2026-11-18; my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) **The Kavita and Sonarr benchmark clones are not migrated, and are left as they are:** both still hold `document.md` and their sections in `document.json`, and neither has its `specs/fsd/` committed (Kavita's is untracked; Sonarr's `specs/` is in `.git/info/exclude` so `fix start` sees a clean base, D351), so every step that reads an FSD there now stops with `fsd_not_migrated` or `fsd_not_committed`. Each is migrated, and its `specs/fsd/<doc>/` committed on a branch of its own in that clone, when the next step on it needs its FSD (Kavita: D375's measurement and the live review), asked first, since it is a commit in a benchmark clone. Changes D389 call 3 ("migrated free" during the phase → migrated when needed). (2) **The missed ripple:** layer 4 bumped `rulings` and `truth-map` to v2 (the schemas, D394) but not the `provides` of the phase that owns them, so `phases check` couldn't see it; tm now provides `rulings` v2 and `truth-map` v2, and fs consumes them. tm was already `needs review` for them in `roadmap.md`; nothing else consumes them. (3) **Built items leave the roadmap (D134):** the "Decided, not built" rows for the FSD as source and for no switches go, and the "Off until its test says so" list is retitled, since D381 is built and what it lists is on. Why: the owner said not to change the clones without asking; a front matter that hides a bump defeats D20.
- D397 · **The Word export is cut: phase fs ends at layer 5 and D396** (owner, 2026-11-18: "honestly i think this export feature can just scrap, we dont need to cater the other cleints fo rnow", asked how a Word export should carry a section's attached files). Layer 6 (`fsd export <doc> --whole --format docx`, D378's "or Word", D389) is not built. The whole-document view stays Markdown only: `fsd export --whole`, built and unchanged, and the per-chapter Markdown export with it (the owner's words were about the Word export; removing the Markdown export would need their yes, D381). Why: no client needs a Word copy now, and doing it properly also meant carrying attachments inside the Word file (Office files embedded, others left out) so a file sent back compares cleanly: work for no current user. Cost: a reader who wants Word gets the Markdown view or the original client file from `fsd.archive`; it comes back only by a new decision when a client needs it. Changes D378 ("Markdown or Word" is now Markdown) and D389 (six layers built, not seven). Next in phase fs: "After the layers" (docs, `OVERVIEW.md`, the end-of-phase report).
- D396 · **`fsd take` brings a section's attachments, and `fsd check --removed` marks a removed section's trace links** (owner, 2026-11-18: "yup add this and the just now that one", on my recommendation, closing two gaps D395 left). (1) Taking a section also brings its attachments from the new file: the files under `attachments/` and their records in `document.json`, the id kept for the same file name in the same section and a new one from the ledger otherwise, one no longer there marked removed; a section whose words are the same and whose attachments changed becomes offered (`file-changed` with `attachmentsChanged`), and a PM's change to a section's attachments counts as a repo change, so it is never taken over. (2) `fsd check --removed <id>` (and a section removed with its marker in the same commit) moves each `specs/trace.yaml` entry citing it to `(removed <id>) <number>`, decision and `decided_by` kept, listed in the result, as promote did (`FsdCarry`). **Built (2026-11-18) with these calls:** (a) an attachment keeps its id by the same bytes in the section, then the same file name there (as import's `Assign`, within the section only): one that moved to another section is marked removed where it left and gets a new id where it lands, when each is taken; (b) `fileSections` entries gain optional `attachments` (file name and hash), no version bump (as D395 call 1); an entry written before D396 has none, so a section holding attachments reads as changed here (never offered) until taken by hand; (c) a PM's change is the record in `document.json` or a file under `attachments/<id>/` edited and committed (HEAD's bytes against the record's `files[].hash`, `attachmentFilesEditedHere`); an uncommitted one (records, or a file missing, edited or added there) makes take refuse the section; (d) the comparison keeps the file's attachment files in `compare/attachments/pending-NNNN/`, hash-checked before take copies them; a section whose only change is its attachments keeps its words byte for byte; the record's `sectionNumber` is the section's number here; (e) files attached to the whole document (no section) aren't taken, still listed under `attachmentsWithNoSection`; (f) `fsd check` finds a removed section's entries by `sectionId` first, then by number; one whose number names it while its `sectionId` names another is left and listed in `sectionIdsDisagree`, one marked by `sectionId` with another number is listed there too (`markedBy`); run again it marks nothing twice; a removal undone before the commit puts its entries back on the section's current number (`trace.restored`); `specs/trace.yaml` joins `uncommittedFiles`. `attachmentsNotTaken` and the "take doesn't bring attachments" texts removed. Tests: `EmbeddedFilesTests` (4: an attachment changed with the same words offered, taken, id kept, `rates.csv` the file's; added gets a new id, gone marked removed, a removed id never comes back; a PM's committed edit both-changed and not taken; an uncommitted edit kept), `FsdCheckTests` (1: marked with decision kept, a refusal writes nothing, disagreeing ids left, idempotent, undone and renumbered put back). 1970 tests passed before the fresh check's fixes and 1970 after them. **Fresh-checked** (2026-11-18; Sonnet, about 107K tokens measured against 60–100K said; `fs-d396-check.md` in the session notes): no path found for take to bring in, overwrite or lose an attachment of a section nobody took. **2 defects, fixed:** a restored entry got HEAD's number, not the section's number now; a taken record kept the file's `sectionNumber`. Also listed now: an entry marked by `sectionId` whose number was another. Not re-checked after the fixes. Risks noted, not changed: take replaces a kept record with the file's whole, so a field added to a record by hand goes; a PM who edits a file and rewrites its hash in `document.json` to match isn't seen; a throw midway through take (after the chapters, before `document.json`) leaves folders and records out of step.
- D395 · **Layer 5 of phase fs (a new file is compared three ways, never imported over; `fsd take`) built; ten calls made while building** (2026-11-17; my calls on the owner's standing word). Once a document's FSD is committed, `fsd import` of any file for it (and `fsd reread`) changes no chapter: it writes `.stangent/fsd/<doc>/compare/` (`comparison.json`, `comparison.md`, the file's `import-report.md` and pictures), status `compared`, each file section matched to a permanent id (the last file's heading path and text hash first, then the repo's text, as D117's rules; doubtful ones settled by `fsd resolve`, `fsd-section-match` on the state module) and given a case: `file-changed` (offered), `new` (offered), `both-changed` (side by side, the repo's kept), `repo-changed` (kept), `gone` (listed, never deleted), `removed-here`, `doubtful`, `same`; `converterChanged` names a converter or version that differs from the last import's, or one not recorded. `stangent fsd take <doc> <section>… | --all-updates` copies the file's heading and text into the chapters, uncommitted, and moves the taken sections' `fileSections` entries, `original`, `converter` and `importCommit`; the commit approves (D380), and the next file compares against it. Removed (D388): `fsd approve`, `fsd reject`, `fsd promote`, `fsd discard`, `--partial`, `candidate/`, `SECTION_DIFF.md`, `promotions.jsonl`, `sections/`, `FsdSectionApproval`, `FsdSectionFiles`, the `fsd-candidate` and `fsd-section-answer` lifecycles (old history lines stay, unread). **Calls:** (1) **`source-document` stays v4, with two optional fields** (`fileSections`: section, number, title, path, text hash; `converter`: format, version): v4 came in this unfinished phase on an unmerged branch, and nothing outside reads them, so no bump and no new `needs review`; (2) **a document written before layer 5** (no `fileSections`) is matched by the chapters of the commit that approved its import (the first commit after `importCommit` that touched them, named in `base.from`), but **nothing is offered from that base**: that commit can hold the PM's edits made before it, so each difference is `both-changed` with the repo's kept, and `baseNote` says why (new sections can still be taken); take records only what it took; `fsd migrate` writes the list from `document.json`'s sections; (3) **a converter version per format** (`IImportFormat.Version`, 1 for docx, pdf and xlsx), raised when a converter's output changes; (4) **take never takes `both-changed`**, not even when named: the PM copies the client's words by hand from `comparison.md` if they want them, so no command can put a client's words over a PM's; (5) **a section whose working-tree text or heading differs from HEAD's is never taken** (an uncommitted edit stays), and a comparison made before HEAD's chapters or facts moved is refused (`comparison_out_of_date`); (6) **the heading counts**: a section Word renumbered is `file-changed` with `headingChanged` and no `textChanged`, so `--all-updates` brings the client's numbering; (7) **a repeated number is warned, not refused** (`numbersRepeated`, as `fsd check` warns, D384): a section gone from the file usually still holds it here, and only a person removes it; (8) **take doesn't move a section** to another level or parent (listed; done by hand) and **doesn't bring attachments** (listed in `comparison.md` and `attachmentsNotTaken`); it copies pictures the text names, gives tables their ids again, and puts the file's problems on the taken sections in place of the last file's; (9) **a new section is placed after the section it follows in the file**, at the file's level, with the next id from `ids.json`; with none before it in the repo it's listed for a person; before anything is written every other section must read as before (title, level, text, parent) and each taken one as the file has it, or nothing is written; (10) **any import of a committed document compares**, the same file again too (a re-import no longer says `unchanged`, since a partly taken file still has offers); before the first commit the same file is `unchanged` and another is `fsd_not_committed`. Trace entries follow a section whose number moved (`FsdCarry`). Tests: `FsdCompareTests` (11: the four cases plus new and gone on a `.docx` pair, take never over a PM edit, an uncommitted edit kept, a new section, take then commit moves the base so a third file compares against the second, a doubtful match, a converter change named, a stale comparison refused, a document from before layer 5, a first import not committed, a reread keeping an uncommitted take's record), the matcher's tests kept in `FsdVersionTests`, `WholeFlowTests` stage 8 through compare, resolve, take, `fsd check` and a commit. **Left, listed:** a section deleted by hand and committed with `fsd check --removed` doesn't mark its trace entries removed (promote did; trace.yaml is frozen since D342); attachments can't be taken; a moved section is moved by hand; a document from before layer 5 gets no update offers until its sections are taken by hand. 1966 tests passed before the fresh check's fixes; the affected classes (489) pass after them. **Fresh-checked** (2026-11-17; Sonnet, about 143K tokens against 120–160K said; `fs-layer5-check.md` in the session notes): no way found for a client's file to overwrite a PM's edit without a person's `take`. **3 defects, fixed:** the fallback base could label a PM's pre-commit edit `file-changed` and offer its revert (now never offered, call 2); `fsd reread`'s same-text branch saved HEAD's `document.json` over an uncommitted take's (now only `problems` move, onto the working file; test added); `take` checked mixed line endings while writing, so a second file could stop it halfway (now every file is checked first). Not re-checked after the fixes.
- D394 · **Layer 4 of phase fs (`stangent fsd write <doc>`, rulings become the FSD's words; `rulings` v2, `truth-map` v2) built; eight calls made while building** (2026-11-17; my calls on the owner's standing word). `fsd write <doc> [--max-cost N|none] [--section <id> [--item <n> --map <id>]]` writes, uncommitted, every ruling that becomes words: the PM's `restated` words replace the quoted sentence (found word for word in its section or one under it) or, for code no section covers, go at the end of the `--section` named (listed without it); a `code-right` ruling goes to `stangent-fsd-writer` (D60) through `plugin/scripts/fsd-write-run.sh --max-cost N <doc>`, and `fsd write-check` checks each draft by code (`replaces` the ruled quote word for word, one line of words, at least one cite, each a real `path:line` at the run's commit inside the entry's linked code), one retry for the failed ones, then listed. Each written entry becomes a confirmed match on its new sentence with `writtenFrom: { state, section, quote | link, ruling, statement, cites, by, on }` and its section's `textHash` moves to the new text (D386); `inFsd` is gone; `rulings-check` lists `written`, `truth-map.md` says "Written into the FSD from … by …; it replaced: “…”"; `/stangent:rule` runs `fsd write`, asks for a section or a cost cap when one is needed, then `fsd check`, and reads the chapter diff back. **Calls:** (1) **only a section that reads as the text its rulings were recorded on takes an edit** (its block's `textHash`, else HEAD's text): an uncommitted hand edit or a newer FSD is listed, so the moved hash never hides another change; after each edit every other section must read byte for byte as before, no heading gained or lost; (2) **listed, never guessed:** a quote found twice or not at all, bold or code marks or lines running past it (marks right around it go with it), words that would change the layout (a line break, a heading, a marker, a list/quote/table/rule start, a fence, a mark left open, an HTML tag, a `|` in a table row), a quote inside words the same run wrote, a chapter file with mixed line endings; (3) `--section` with more than one waiting needs `--item <n>` (the truth-map number, `--map` checked as `fsd rule` does); (4) without `--max-cost` the code-right rulings are listed with the command (rule 9); `none` is the drafter's own $2; (5) "inside the linked code" is within one of the entry's evidence ranges, or every cited line linking (D362's linker) to the entry's own flow, screen, node or file; (6) a Jira-drafted ruling is written only once committed, checked again at `write-check`; (7) a refresh drops the review finding a written entry replaced while its old sentence is gone (`supersededByWritten`), never marks a written entry not found, and a `restated` statement found in a changed section's text becomes a written match (`writtenByHand`: D386's "simply a match"); (8) the rulings to be written must pass the reader's checks, schema included, or nothing is written. A committed ruling not yet written still judges tickets. Tests: `FsdWriteTests` (20). **Fresh-checked** (Sonnet, about 131K tokens against 120–170K said; `fs-layer4-check.md` in the session notes): nothing found that writes an unasked sentence or lets an uncited one through; 2 defects fixed (a `--section` target found before an edit moved its lines: a crash or a false "edited since"; a huge line number in a cite crashed `write-check`) and risks closed by calls 2 and 6 and the superseding condition in 7; a test it asked for found an 8th: `writtenFrom.cites` wasn't in the v2 schema (fixed, call 8). **Left, listed:** the linker reads today's graph while line counts come from the run's commit, so a graph rebuilt between `write` and `write-check` judges "inside the link" by the newer graph; a restated statement found anywhere in a changed section's text (table bars and list marks aside) becomes a match even if it sits elsewhere; a quote may be a short fragment the whole-word check allows; the person reading the chapter diff is the last check on the words' meaning. Not re-checked after the fixes. Contract ripple (D20): Phase 3 and tm marked `needs review`.

- D393 · **Layer 3 of phase fs (`stangent fsd check <doc>`, the check before a commit) built; six calls made while building** (2026-11-17; my calls on the owner's standing word). It reads the working tree's chapters, compares them with HEAD's by permanent id, gives each unmarked heading the next id from `ids.json` (the only write, and none when anything is refused), and lists every section added, removed, renamed, moved or changed (what the commit approves), with the document's other changed files, new chapter files included. Refused (`fsd_check_refused`, each under `result.refused`): a damaged marker or one off its heading, an id on two headings, one the ledger never gave, marks removed, or gave before HEAD with no committed heading holding it (`old-id`), a ledger that went back, a section gone while a heading has no marker (`marker-removed`), an unmarked heading like a committed section whose marker moved (`marker-moved`), a new level skip, and a section removed while `rulings.yaml` (working tree's or HEAD's) holds a person's work on it (listed). Warned: repeated or out-of-order numbers (D384), a level skip HEAD or a first import already has. **Calls:** (1) **a missing marker is never guessed**: a section gone while any heading is unmarked is refused, naming a heading that looks like it and the marker to put back, or `--removed S-0007` if it goes on purpose; (2) **`--removed` is also the only way past rulings on a removed section**: its entries stay in `rulings.yaml`, listed as `rulingsLeft`; (3) **a damaged or stray marker is refused by the step reader too** (`invalid_fsd`), closing D392's leftover; a damaged marker committed earlier may be repaired only on the heading that held it; (4) **an id on another section's words is refused** (`marker-on-other-section`): by words, or by title where neither has the same words, and only when that other section no longer holds them, so two sections sharing "TBD." stay apart; (5) **a ruled section whose heading and words both changed is refused unless `--same S-0002`** says it's still that section; (6) chapter files match `.md` in any case, as HEAD's reader does. `/stangent:rule` and `docs/spec.md` say: edit, `fsd check`, read the list, commit; the uncommitted-changes warning names `fsd check`. Tests: `FsdCheckTests` (30). **Fresh-checked** (Sonnet, about 105K tokens against 120–160K said; `fs-layer3-check.md` in the session notes): 3 defects, all fixed by calls 4 and 5: a marker typed over another heading whose section was deleted, two same-title headings swapping markers, and a new heading taking a ruled section's marker passed with a warning or none; risks fixed: new chapter files unlisted, `.md` case, an empty block id. **Left, listed:** ids are taken from the ledger before the chapter files are written, so a failure between skips ids (never reused); `--removed` doesn't mark the id removed in `ids.json` (`old-id` guards it); a commit that skips `fsd check` is still read with D389 call 1's warnings, so a marker deleted and committed that way leaves its rulings on no section until the next review.
- D392 · **Layer 2 of phase fs (sections read from the committed chapters, `source-document` v4) built; four calls made while building** (2026-11-17; my calls on the owner's standing word). One reader, `FsdDocument.Load`, behind `FsdOutline.Load` and `FsdImport.Documents`: HEAD's `document.json` (the facts), HEAD's `ids.json` and the chapter files as of HEAD, read through git and cached in `.stangent/fsd/<doc>/chapters-cache.json` by the chapters' tree hash; a section's attachments, `attachmentLines` and `contentHash` are worked out from the facts' `attachments` by permanent id, and attachment files a step reads (quotes, sheets) are HEAD's too. `document.json` is v4: no `sections`, no `markdown`, `importCommit`; every writer goes through `FsdDocument.Save`. Refused: `fsd_not_migrated`, `not_git`, `fsd_not_committed`, and `invalid_fsd` for an id on two headings, one the ledger never gave, one it marks removed (rule 11), or an emptied ledger ("restore it from git"); an unmarked heading reads with no id and a warning naming `fsd check` (D389 call 1). Also: `fsd tables` lists a table a committed edit took away (`tablesNotFound`) instead of crashing, and matches a sheet to its section by permanent id. - D391 · **Layer 1 of phase fs (chapter files and ids) built; four calls made while building** (2026-11-17; my calls on the owner's standing word). `fsd import` writes `specs/fsd/<doc>/chapters/` and no `document.md`; `stangent fsd migrate <doc>` converts a document imported before (refused outside git, with uncommitted changes, or on a hand-edited `document.md`); `fsd export --whole` writes one file, markers off; `FsdChapters.Read` parses chapters to sections (number as written, title, level, parent, text, hash, id). **Calls:** (1) **a `no-number` flag in the marker**, `## 2023 Roadmap <!-- S-0012 no-number -->`, only where the import read no number on words that start with one: without it the reader would make "2023 Roadmap" section 2023, and the import's judgement would change on the first read (the PM can delete the flag to make it a number, D384); (2) **a backslash on a line that would read as a heading and isn't** (`\# of retries`), taken off when read; code fences aren't read, since no converter writes one and a stray ``` paragraph would otherwise hide every heading after it, so the text and its hash read back the same and the line renders as text; (3) **the writer checks its own round trip** and writes nothing if the files wouldn't read back as `document.json`'s sections (`chapters_out_of_step`); a converter heading inside an unclosed code fence is refused this way, named; (4) **until layer 2**, `document.json` keeps `sections` and `markdown: "document.md"` (removed with the v4 bump, so `source-document` bumps once, D389 (4)), promote writes chapters (migrating that document), the hand-edit warning compares the chapters, and the candidate folder keeps its `document.md` until layer 5 removes it. Kavita's and Sonarr's benchmark FSDs migrate to the same text byte for byte (Sonarr gets 21 ids it never had). **Fresh-checked** (Sonnet, about 120K tokens, `fs-layer1-check.md` in the session notes): 2 defects, both fixed: the attached-files section (`AttachmentHome`) has a number its heading doesn't show, so any numbered document with an unplaced attachment was refused, now its marker carries it (`<!-- S-0012 number=7 -->`); and a stray fence paragraph refused the import (fences dropped, above). Of 5 risks, fixed: a `\r\n` in the text is caught by the round-trip check as the disk would read it; import, approve and promote check the chapters before anything moves, so a refusal leaves the current version as it was. Left: promote rewrites the chapters from the new file, as it rewrote `document.md` (layer 5 replaces promote); dead section-approval code still reads `document.md` (layer 5 deletes it); `markdown: "document.md"` until layer 2.
- D390 · **Layer 0 of phase fs (no switches) built; three of my D388 calls corrected while building** (2026-11-17; my calls on the owner's standing word, found reading the code). (2) **An old key is ignored with a warning, not refused:** every `stangent.yaml` that `stangent init` wrote holds `detectors: []` and `history.enabled`, so refusing them would break every repo set up so far, the benchmark configs included; `components`, `detectors`, `history.enabled`, `fsd.findings`, `fsd.attachmentHome`, `fsd.compareTables` and `tickets.aiPoints` are read, dropped and named on stderr ("no longer do anything: every feature is on (D381)"), and `init` writes none of them. (3) **The ticket bundle keeps `findings: false`** until `ticket-bundle`'s next bump, so this layer causes no unplanned contract change. Also: the history fingerprint keeps the old `true` in its text, so saved history isn't recomputed; first-import approval's branch is gone now (nothing could turn it on), the rest of it and section approval go in layer 5. `StangentComponents`, `stangent components`, the envelope's `components` and the two telemetry attributes are removed (`cli-conventions` v4, `telemetry-event` v3); 1a and tm `needs review`. Tests: `NoSwitchesTests` (7), an `ai.enabled: false` bug-proof test, the finder's before-and-after points; the switch tests and A.9's and D150's approval tests removed. **Code's signals stand until the finder answers** (my call, building): a ticket with a stack trace starts from `OrderService.Place` before the paid finder runs, instead of from nothing; once it answers, only its files (D101's rule after that, toward D156 P6). **Fresh-checked** (2026-11-17; Sonnet, ~146K tokens; `fs-layer0-check.md` in the session notes; 2 defects, 7 risks, no wrong result). **Fixed:** help texts, an unreachable `component_off`, docs and the bundle schema's descriptions still spoke of switches; unused switch-name constants; `graph build` now warns (`graph_enums_not_ignored`) when git doesn't ignore `.stangent/graph-enums.json`, which every build writes now (a repo set up before needs `stangent init` again). **Left, listed:** section approval's code stays behind a hard `false` until layer 5 removes it; `PageChart.BodyMarker` still says "while components.pages is on" (it is written into every page.md, so changing it rewrites every page); everything that came on now runs on every build or write (enums, plain React apps through Node, Python, follow-moves, statement updates, schema-once, attachment homes, workbook text), and frontend graphs re-extract once. Cost: a repo can't skip a detector or git history any more.
- D389 · **Phase fs planned: the FSD as source, in seven layers** (2026-11-17; my calls on the owner's standing word, `phases/fs-fsd-source.md`). Layers: 0 no switches (D381, D388), 1 chapter files and ids, 2 sections read from the committed chapters, 3 `fsd check`, 4 `fsd write`, 5 a new file compared, 6 Word export; each with tests and a narrow fresh check (D158). **My calls:** (1) a heading committed with no marker reads with no id and a warning, and the steps still run; a marker on two headings or an id the ledger never gave makes the reader refuse that document; (2) no git hook (hooks aren't committed; the reader's refusals are the guarantee); (3) `stangent fsd migrate <doc>` moves an existing document once, refused on uncommitted changes there; (4) `source-document` bumps to v4 once, at layer 2, and layer 5 extends v4 before the phase ends; (5) Word export last. Contract ripples (D20): `cli-conventions` 3 → 4 and `telemetry-event` 2 → 3 (layer 0, phase 0), `source-document` 3 → 4 (layer 2, phase 2b), `rulings` and `truth-map` 1 → 2 (layer 4, tm); consumers go `needs review` as each lands. Cost: seven fresh checks, about 650K tokens in all.
- D388 · **How D381 lands: two components removed, the rest on** (owner, 2026-11-17: removed "Section + first-import approval" and "findings", on my recommendation, after asking why those two). **Removed:** `section-approval` and `first-import-approval` with `fsd approve`, `fsd reject`, `candidate/` and `fsd promote` (D385 replaces them: the comparison, `fsd take`, the commit), and `findings` (the frozen old-route findings, D342, in bundles and drafts; the truth map replaced them, and on they would sit beside a ruling and disagree). **Turned on, listed to the owner first as D381 asks:** never on a real repo: `truth-verdict` (fresh-checked; D375's paid run still owed), `ticket-excel-text`, `follow-moves`, `schema-once`, `attachment-home`, `statement-update`, `table-facts`; only on the retired pilot: `root-screens`, `route-render`; on Sonarr, Kavita and nopCommerce: `pages`. **My calls within it:** the on-by-default switches go too (`ai`, `git-history`, `python`, `file-finder`, `bug-proof`, `fix`, `drafts`, `page-verifier`, `line-citations`, `page-corrections`, `page-exclusions` and each `detector-*`, with `stangent.yaml`'s `detectors:` list and `ai.enabled`): a paid step is stopped by rule 9's cost choice, not a switch, and a detector that finds nothing adds nothing; `stangent components` goes, and a `stangent.yaml` that still has `components:` or a removed key is refused, naming D381, as cut ones are today (D342); `NotConnectedYet` moves to the roadmap. Cost: a repo can't skip a detector or git history; a benchmark compares commits, not switch settings. Not built.
- D387 · **A `Ruling:` reply from Jira is drafted by pull and written by `fsd write`** (owner, 2026-11-17, on my recommendation). `ticket pull` stays free and drafts the ruling into `rulings.yaml` as D374 does. New **`stangent fsd write <doc> [--max-cost N|none]`** turns each ruling not yet written into a chapter edit, uncommitted: words the PM gave (`restated`) are placed by code, replacing the quoted sentence for a gap or a conflict; for code no section covers (including a ticket's own `ticket:<KEY>` entry) they are added at the end of a section the PM names (`--section`), and without one it is listed, not guessed; `code-right` gets an AI-drafted sentence checked by code (D379: the replaced sentence is the ruled quote, the cited `path:line` is real and in the entry's linked code), under the cost cap (rule 9). Each entry becomes a match (D386). The developer or PM reads the chapter diff and commits once (D380). A committed ruling not yet written is a decision on file and still judges tickets meanwhile (D376: `rulings.yaml` holds what isn't yet FSD words). `/stangent:rule` calls `fsd write` after the PM answers. Rejected: pull writing chapters (it would edit the FSD as a side effect of reading Jira). Cost: one more command between a reply and a judged ticket. Not built.
- D386 · **A ruling written into the FSD becomes a confirmed match; the rest stay rulings** (owner, 2026-11-17, on my recommendation; settles how `rulings.yaml` shrinks, D376). When a `code-right` or `restated` ruling is written into its chapter (D379, D387), its entry turns, in the same change, into a confirmed match: key = its section and the new sentence, the same link and fingerprint (D362), `ruling: confirmed`, and `writtenFrom: { state, quote, ruling }` naming what it replaced; the section's recorded text hash moves to the new text, so a refresh doesn't flag the section for the edit it made itself. Tickets on that code still find the rule with no new review. `fsd-right` (the code has the bug), `keep`, `remove`, `not-a-match` and unanswered entries stay as rulings: they are decisions, not FSD words. D363's `inFsd: true` goes (a restated statement found in the text is simply a match). `rulings` contract bumps (D20). Rejected: deleting written entries (the link to code is lost until the next paid review). Cost: a written ruling's entry is a match a person didn't confirm as a match, marked by `writtenFrom`. Not built.
- D385 · **A new file is compared three ways, section by section; the commit approves** (owner, 2026-11-17, on my recommendation; settles D376's proposal). `fsd import` of a file for a document that already exists writes a comparison and changes no chapter: each section of the new file is matched to the last imported file's sections as import matches today (number, title, then text; a doubtful match is settled by a person), and so to a permanent id. Per section: only the file changed it, offered as an update; only the repo changed it, the repo's text kept (the file is behind); both changed, shown side by side with the repo's text kept; new in the file, offered as a new section; gone from the file, listed, never deleted from the repo. `fsd take <doc> <section>` copies the file's text into the chapter, uncommitted, and the commit is the approval (D380). **My call within it (challenging D378):** the base is not the repo's chapters at the import commit, since after a second import those already mix in the PM's edits; `document.json` (D383) keeps each section's text hash as the last imported file gave it, which says who changed what, and the side-by-side shows the repo's text and the file's. Replaces D150's section approval, D122's first-import approval (a first import is approved by committing its chapters) and the `candidate/` and `fsd promote` step; each is listed to the owner before it is removed (D381). Rejected: a two-way comparison (every section the PM changed since the import would show as a difference). Cost: a converter change between two imports can make unchanged sections look changed by the client (the comparison says when the converter version differs); approving a whole file's updates is one `fsd take` per section, or `--all-updates`. Not built.
- D384 · **Section numbers are the PM's text; stangent never renumbers** (owner, 2026-11-17, on my recommendation). A heading's number ("2.2") is read from the heading as written in the chapter file (D377), and no step rewrites a heading; a number repeated in one document, or out of order, is a warning from the pre-commit check (D380), not a refusal; a heading with no number has none. Everything stangent keeps about a section refers to its permanent id (D382), so numbers can change by hand without breaking a ruling or a link; whatever still keys on a number (attachments' `sectionNumber`, the frozen link route's entry ids, D342) is listed in the plan and moved to the id or left frozen. Rejected: stangent renumbering after an insert (edits the PM's text, a long diff for one insert); no numbers in headings (no longer matches the client's document). Cost: a number can be wrong or repeated for as long as nobody fixes it. Not built.
- D383 · **`document.json` keeps only the import's facts; sections are read from the committed chapters** (owner, 2026-11-17: "A: drop the copy", on my recommendation, after asking what the question meant). The committed `document.json` holds what only an import knows: the file, its hash and archive place, the import problems, tables, pictures and attachments with their ids, and the commit that recorded the import (D378's base for comparing a new file). Its `sections` go. Every step gets the sections from one reader that parses the chapter files as of `HEAD` (number, title, level, parent, text, text hash and permanent id from D382's marker), so an uncommitted edit is never read (D380) and nothing can be stale; the 11 files that read `document.json` today go through that reader. `source-document` contract bumps (D20). Rejected: keeping the section copy, refreshed by the pre-commit check (the text twice in the repo, and a forgotten check blocks every step). Cost: the reader runs git on each step (cached by `HEAD`'s tree hash in `.stangent/`); a repo that isn't a git checkout can't read its FSD. Not built.
- D382 · **Each section's permanent id sits on its heading, hidden** (owner, 2026-11-17: "Hidden", on my recommendation, after asking why hidden): in the chapter files (D377) every heading line ends with its id in an HTML comment, `### 3.1 Series page <!-- S-0012 -->`, which GitHub and the whole-document export (D378) don't show; the id moves with its heading, so a rename keeps the section's rulings and links. Before a commit (D380) stangent's check gives a heading typed with no marker the next id from the ledger (`ids.json`, never reused, rule 11), and refuses a marker that is missing from a section that had one, appears on two headings, or names an id the ledger never gave. Rejected: a visible `{#S-0012}` (clutters every heading for readers); matching by title and text after each edit (a guess on every edit). Cost: a visual Markdown editor that hides comments can drop a marker unseen; the check catches it and refuses, so the person puts it back. Not built.
- D381 · **No component switches; rule 8 changes** (owner, 2026-11-17: "2nd one, and also dont auto off, just tell me if the feature added is totally useless then verify with me first", choosing to remove switches entirely over my pick, which kept the switches and dropped only "starts off"). Replaces D120's "every component gets a switch; anything that changes a prompt starts off" (and D121's switch list, D373's defaults): a feature is on once built and fresh-checked; when a measurement or check shows a feature is useless, I tell the owner and it is removed or kept only on their yes, never turned off quietly. Rule 9 (no paid step without a cost choice) stays. Why (owner): the pipeline is deterministic where it can be and uses AI where it should, so a switch per piece adds little. Cost: removing `StangentComponents` touches 19 source files, `stangent components`, the `components` envelope and telemetry fields (`cli-conventions` and `telemetry-event` contracts, D20 ripple); a benchmark can no longer compare with and without a piece in one checkout (it compares commits instead); the pieces now off (truth-verdict, ticket-excel-text, D372's and the roadmap's "Off until its test says so" list) come on, and any of those never tested on a real repo is listed to the owner first. Not built.
- D380 · **A commit is the approval of an FSD change** (2026-11-17; my call on the owner's standing word, after the owner moved on without answering): an edit to a chapter file (D377), drafted by stangent, made by Claude for the PM or typed, counts only once committed; `document.json` is rebuilt from the committed chapters, so tickets are judged only on the committed FSD; before the commit stangent checks the edit (every section keeps its permanent id, nothing lost by accident, headings nest); who approved and when come from git (a developer committing for the PM is shown, not refused), as for `rulings.yaml` (D364). Rejected: a separate per-section approve step on top of the commit. Not built.
- D379 · **A ruling becomes the FSD's words this way** (owner, 2026-11-17: "yup", on my recommendation): the PM's own words (restated, or a ticket nothing covered) go into the chapter as written; for "the code is right", an AI drafts the replacing sentence and code checks that the sentence it replaces is the ruled one and that the draft cites a real code line it describes (Sonarr: "Deleting a series always deletes its folder" → the delete dialog's checkbox); the PM approves the chapter change, read back as `/stangent:rule` does, before it counts (rules 1, 6). - D378 · **`document.md` is removed from the repo** (owner, 2026-11-17: "i see then we can remove it from repo", after hearing that a regenerated copy is no archive: the original file is in `fsd.archive` and every version of the text is in git). The chapter files (D377) are the only copy of the FSD in the repo; a whole-document view is made on demand outside git (`fsd export --whole`, Markdown or Word, to build). The version a new file is compared with (D376) comes from git: the chapters as of the commit that recorded the last import, which `document.json` names; no frozen copy is kept. Why: no second copy for someone to edit or to read as current; rule 4. Cost: browsing the repo shows a folder of chapters, not one document. Not built.
- D377 · **The FSD you edit is split per chapter, not one `document.md`** (owner, 2026-11-17: "okay then", on my recommendation after "if i have a big fsd, it will be just one document.md?"). Under D376, the editable FSD is `fsd export`'s layout made the source: one Markdown file per top-level chapter, cut at its sub-headings when over `fsd.exportMaxLines` (1,500), each file named with its section's permanent id (`01-02-s0003-series-details.md`) so a renamed heading keeps its id; edits, diffs, approvals and a new file's comparison go chapter by chapter; Claude opens only the chapter a ruling touches; `document.json` stays the generated index every step reads. Why: a 200-page FSD is 10,000+ lines in one file, past what Claude reads in one go, and one file makes per-section approval and parallel edits hard. What becomes of `document.md` is open (questions.md). Not built.
- D376 · **After import, the FSD in the repo is the source of truth; the original file never comes back** (owner, 2026-11-17: "assume the word file or excel or pdf or png or whatever wont come back anymore, fsd will be source of truth"). Changes D131 (`document.md` was a generated copy, never read back, changed only by importing a new file) and how D363/D371/D374 rulings end: a PM decision is not kept beside the FSD for good but becomes an edit to the FSD itself ("code right": the sentence changes to what the code does; restated or a ticket nothing covered: the sentence is added in its section; "FSD right": no edit, the code has the bug), and `rulings.yaml` holds only decisions not yet written into the FSD. Not built; how an edit is made, approved and recorded is grilled next (questions.md). **If a new file does arrive** (owner, same day: "we should verify against our fsd and see what's differ and see which one is latest"): it is compared with the repo's FSD and never replaces it ("but fsd alwas source of truth"): the repo's FSD stays the truth, and the file's differences are proposed changes a person approves section by section. Proposed (not decided): a three-way comparison per section against the version last imported, to say who changed what: a section only the file changed is offered as an update; one only the repo changed keeps the repo's text (the file is just behind); one both changed is shown side by side, the repo's text kept unless a person takes the file's. File dates aren't trusted to say which is newer. Cost: stangent now keeps the FSD's history (git), and an import after the first is a comparison, not a replacement.
- D375 · **D358's measurement redone for the truth map; `benchmark/tickets/kavita-fsd-rules/plan.md` revised and frozen** (2026-11-17, before the planter runs; my calls on the owner's standing word, from `phases/tm-truth-map.md` "After the layers"). A fresh Opus planter, blind for this session, answers Kavita's 42 truth-map entries as a PM through `fsd rule` (leaving 2–3 blank on purpose), commits them as `Planter PM`, and writes 9 replay tickets (3 backed on an `fsd-right` or restated ruling, with nothing planted in the code: the ruling says the code is wrong; 3 contradicted against `code-right`/`keep`; 3 unclear, at least one blank and one not covered) plus 2 `Ruling:` replies, with a sealed key. Scored by code: the verdict per ticket (over all 9, and over those whose bundle holds the entry, so a file-finder miss is told apart), no test run for any contradicted or unclear ticket (`fix start` refuses it, free), the backed ones reproduce, and both replies lead (pull, commit, `rulings-check`, prove again) to the right verdict. Bar: 0 of 6 wrong tickets with a test, verdict right on 8 of 9 or on every ticket whose entry reached the bundle, 2 of 3 backed reproduce, both replies right; fails on any wrong ticket with a test or 6 or fewer right with the entry in the bundle; between is talked through. No fix runs, no FSD-text arm (the old question is gone: D372 prints section text and the judge is the truth map). Replay can't post, so `reply.py` records the post and adds the question and the reply to the replay ticket; `reply.py` and `score.py` are written before the planter runs. Cost: planter ~250–400K tokens (~$4–8), tickets ~$5–12, at most $20 under `--max-cost 20`; asked before spending.
- D374 · **Layer 5 of the truth map (`Ruling:` replies, D364) built** (2026-11-17), behind `truth-verdict` (off by default); code only. `ticket pull` (`RulingReplies.cs`) reads the comments after the posted questions draft that holds the PM's question, for lines starting `Ruling:`; `the code is right` / `the FSD is right` / `keep` / `remove` (and `yes` / `no` on a match) give that ruling, any other text is `restated` with it as the statement; it drafts the ruling into `specs/fsd/<doc>/rulings.yaml` as `fsd rule` writes (`JiraRulings.cs`; only that entry's lines change) with `jira: { key, comment, author, created }`, uncommitted, and says a person confirms it by committing; `rulings-check` lists `fromJira` with the Jira author, the git committer and `differ`, and `truth-map.md` shows both ("in Jira by Pat PM (SHOP-1, comment 101), confirmed by Dana Lead on …"). No contract bump: `rulings` v1 and `truth-map` v1 already had `jira`. **My calls (the owner's standing word):** (1) the entry asked about is the latest proof attempt's `verdict.entry`, trusted only while the posted draft still ends with the question that verdict writes (code recomputes it), so a reply never lands on an entry the question wasn't about; (2) **a drafted ruling backs no ticket until committed** (the bundle lists it `usable: false`): a Jira comment can come from anyone, and D364 makes the commit the confirmation; (3) **"not covered"** (no entry), and the rule in words about a match (a match can't be restated, D371), become an undocumented entry of the ticket's own, keyed `{ section, link: "ticket:<KEY>" }`, on the ticket's file and symbol points, so it judges later tickets on that code too; a review refresh keeps it and never marks it not found (it was never a review finding), its link isn't re-checked; with several FSDs a "not covered" reply is listed, not put in one; (4) one person's later reply replaces their earlier one; replies from different people that disagree are listed and nothing is drafted; (5) `the code is right` on undocumented code means keep; a fixed word that doesn't fit the question (`keep` on a conflict, `the FSD is right` with nothing in the FSD) is listed, never turned into a restated rule; (6) a reply overwrites the entry's ruling (a contradicted ticket's question asks whether the rule should change), and the output names the ruling it replaced; (7) pull doesn't run `ticket prove` (paid): its output says to commit, run `rulings-check`, then prove again. Cost: a ruling on an entry the last review didn't find again stays unusable until found or dropped (as `fsd rule`); pull writes into the working tree, beside whatever else is uncommitted there. **Fresh-checked** (2026-11-17; Sonnet, ~104K tokens; `tm-layer5-check.md` in the session notes; 5 defects, 7 risks). **Fixed:** on a contradicted ticket ("Should the rule change?") `Ruling: no` was drafted as the opposite of what the PM meant (now `yes`/`no` there is listed: the reply must give the rule); "yes, it does that" or "keep it, thanks" became a restated rule (a fixed word with more after it is now listed and asked back); two people who disagreed could never settle it (each person's latest reply now counts); a new statement under an already committed `ruling: restated` line counted as committed (a Jira ruling is committed only when its `ruling`, `statement` and `jira` lines all are); `fsd rule` kept the `jira:` line of the reply it overrode, so the truth map credited the Jira author (now dropped), and a ticket's own entry couldn't be dropped (now `drop` takes it); anything that threw while reading replies failed the whole pull (now `state: failed`, the pull stands); a draft a person took out was drafted again on the next pull (now recorded in `rulings-drafted.json` and listed); only the later of two posted tones was read (both count); backticks, `-`/`1.` bullets and Jira's `+0800` offsets weren't read. **Left, listed:** the author is Jira's display name, so two accounts with one name count as one person; free text that isn't a rule ("not sure, ask Bob") still becomes a restated draft, caught only by the person committing; a `ticket:` entry's link isn't re-checked on a refresh. 4 tests, each fix covered in them.
- D373 · **`page-corrections` and `page-exclusions` are on by default** (owner, 2026-11-17: "Yes, turn them on", on my call after listing the 15 switches off by default). Both act only on what a person decided (an approved correction to a page, a page excluded with a reason), which is rule 1 working as meant; with none on file they change nothing. Off, an approved correction was undone the next time its page was written, and an excluded page kept showing in bundles. Why they were off (D131): a corrected statement changes what link judges read, and that route is frozen (D342). The other unmeasured AI-input switches stay off, each with a call: `ticket-excel-text` until a real ticket carries a workbook (try it on and off there); `schema-once` measured within the next paid page run (half the batch each way); `statement-update` waits on whether Phase 2a's reference pages outlive the rebuild (D156), dropped if not. Cost: a repo with corrections or exclusions on file sees them take effect on its next page write and bundle.
- D372 · **Layer 4 of the truth map (the bundle's truth list, the verdict, the PM's question; D361, D364, D366 (4)) built** (2026-11-17), behind a new component **`truth-verdict`, off by default** (it changes what the reproducer and the draft writer read, rule 8). (1) **The bundle** (`ticket-bundle` v2 → v3): `truth` lists, from every `specs/fsd/<doc>/truth-map.json`, the entries one of whose evidence lines is in a ticket file (a file point, a symbol point's file, a file a page point's statements cite; not the impact answer's: an entry two lines away doesn't judge the ticket), as `T1`, `T2`, … with ruling, the PM's words, standing, confidence, notes, lines and `usable` (can it back a ticket: ruled, a confirmed match, an unconfirmed high one; never blank, flagged, not found again, not a match, an unconfirmed medium or low match), and each section's full text; `state` `off`, `none` (no truth map in the repo: nothing about the FSD is judged, today's behaviour) or `built`. (2) **No usable entry, no AI call:** nothing covers the ticket's files, or nothing that does can back it, so the attempt is recorded at once as `not-provable`, `noProof: unclear`, with the entry it stopped on (none: not covered). (3) **The verdict in the reproducer's same call** (`bug-reproducer-output` v1 → v2, read only with the component on; off, the reproducer reads v1 as before): `verdict` backed / contradicted / unclear with the entry and words from it; code checks the entry is in the input and the quote word for word in its FSD sentence, the PM's words, its section's text or the review's note (one retry), that backed comes with the test and the others with outcome `stopped`, and makes it unclear (`forced`, `said` kept) when the entry can't back a ticket. Contradicted and unclear write and run no test: `not-provable` with `noProof: contradicted | unclear` and the verdict on the attempt (`bug-proof` v1 → v2; a v1 record is saved as v2 unchanged). Backed goes on to the test, its verdict kept, `pmConfirmed: false` on an unconfirmed high match. (4) **The PM's question is written by code**, appended to the checked questions draft: what the rule says or why none settles it, quoted ("**For the PM:** the FSD's §1 Orders says “…”, and nobody has ruled whether the code or the FSD is right (the code: `Core/OrderService.cs:5`). Which is right?"; no code names in the client tone), then "Reply with a line starting `Ruling:` …"; the writer is told code adds it. **My calls (the owner's standing word):** the switch, off; code writes the question rather than the AI (deterministic, and `ticket-update` stays v1: no new link kind); the entry the question is about stays in `bug-proof.json`'s verdict for layer 5, not in the draft; a section cited with no entry is unclear (D366 rejected the reproducer leaning on FSD text no PM checked); screens (an entry linked by screen to a ticket's page) don't make an entry apply yet, only its lines in ticket files; `fsd.pm` (mentioning the PM in Jira) isn't built: the question says "For the PM". D20: Phase 3 provides `ticket-bundle` 3 and `bug-proof` 2; Phase 4 and 2c (consumers, already `needs review`) are marked for it in `roadmap.md`; the truth map consumes 3 and 2. Cost: 7 tests; a ticket with several entries asks about the first only; the truth list isn't cut by the bundle's size cap. **Fresh-checked** (2026-11-17; Sonnet, ~129K tokens; `tm-layer4-check.md` in the session notes; 4 defects, 8 risks; with the switch off nothing the AI reads changed, and v1 proofs flow through confirm, verify and the fix loop). **Fixed:** a bundle built before the switch was turned on (no `truth`, or `off`) let a test be written with no truth check (now refused: build the bundle again); a verdict against its entry's ruling passed (backed on code-right or keep, contradicted on fsd-right, remove or not-a-match: now the retry); a reproduced, perhaps confirmed, proof was replaced by the no-AI stop when its rule later needed the PM (now kept, with a warning to ask); a truth-map.json that doesn't read counted as a built map with nothing in it, stopping every ticket (now an error); evidence lines with spaces, backslashes, `./` or a column weren't read back to their file; the question read wrongly for undocumented code and said "needs another look" twice, and the client tone kept backticks from the FSD's or the PM's words. **Left, listed:** a test the reproducer wrote before code forced its verdict to unclear stays on disk, unrun; a bug in code no FSD section covers stops as unclear once any truth map exists (D364's "not covered", as decided); the bundle JSON is v3 with `truth: off` even when off (nothing hashes it).
- D371 · **The PM rules by reading `truth-map.md` and telling Claude; no person edits `rulings.yaml`** (owner, 2026-11-17: "PM dont know syntaces, we shoudl make it easier for them to read, which i initally thought is the markdown file", then: the Phase 8 dashboard (D12) will do it properly, "so its not jira, or they can do it while reading the file and ask claude to do it, so for now we can just go for the claude"). Changes D363's "the PM fills in a committed file": the file stays, as the record git blames for who ruled and when, but code writes it. (1) **`truth-map.md` asks plain questions:** every open item (a blank ruling, a flagged or not-found one, a section's unconfirmed matches, low and medium first) gets a number and a question in plain words with plain answers, no YAML (example: "12. §4.2 Deleting a library. The FSD says: \"Admins can delete a library\". The code: `LibraryService.cs:212` (medium). Does the code do what the FSD says? Yes / No"; for a difference: "The code is right (the FSD needs updating) / The FSD is right (the code has a bug) / Neither, in my words: …"; for undocumented code: "Keep it / Remove it / In my words: …"). (2) **`stangent fsd rule <doc> <n> <answer>`** writes the answer into `rulings.yaml`: `yes`/`no` for a match or a section's matches, `code`/`fsd` for a difference, `keep`/`remove` for undocumented code, `--words "…"` for a restated rule, `--note` for a PM note; it keeps every other line as it is, prints the item back in plain words, and never commits; answering clears the item's `flagged` and `new` marks; `clear` undoes an answer, `drop` removes an item the review didn't find again. The numbers belong to one build of the map: `truth-map.json` records them, the command turns a number back into its entry's key through that file and looks the key up in today's `rulings.yaml`, refusing an item a refresh removed or changed in kind, so a number never lands on the wrong item; the map isn't rebuilt per answer, so the numbers the PM is reading hold until `fsd rulings-check` runs (built: the hash check planned first would have refused every answer after the first). (3) **A plugin command `/stangent:rule`**: Claude reads the items the PM names, turns the PM's words ("12 yes, 13 the FSD is right") into `fsd rule` calls, asks back when a reply could mean two things instead of guessing, reads each recorded ruling back, and offers to commit; the commit's author is who ruled (D363), so the PM runs it on their own machine. AI proposes, code checks (D18): the command refuses an answer that doesn't fit the item. (4) The Phase 8 dashboard later calls the same command. Hand edits of `rulings.yaml` are still read, and its header says to ask Claude or run `fsd rule` instead. Unchanged: a ticket's open point is still asked and answered in Jira, a `Ruling:` reply drafted into `rulings.yaml` (D364, layer 5). **My calls (the owner's standing word):** numbered items over the section-and-quote keys (a PM can say "12"); a command Claude calls, not Claude editing YAML (code checks the answer and keeps the file valid); built next, as layer 2b, before layer 4, since it changes what the PM sees at setup and layer 4 reads only `truth-map.json`. Rejected: one Jira issue for the setup rulings (owner: not Jira; the dashboard comes later); ticking boxes in the markdown (still syntax, still a hand edit in git).
