Cost: one command and one plugin file; the PM needs Claude Code in the repo until the dashboard; Claude can map words to the wrong answer, so each is read back; when a developer commits for the PM, git names the developer. **Built and fresh-checked** (2026-11-17; Sonnet, ~132K tokens; `tm-layer2b-check.md` in the session notes; `PmAnswers.cs`, `plugin/commands/rule.md`; Kavita: items 1–42, answers wrote only their own lines). **Fixed from the check** (5 defects, 7 risks): `restated` was accepted on a match, leaving a file `rulings-check` refuses (now refused, and every answer is checked against the file's checks before it is written, a file with problems first); a number from a map rebuilt since could land on another item of the same kind (`truth-map.md` now opens "Questions numbered for map `<id>`", Claude passes `--map <id>` and a rebuilt map is refused); the PM's words went through the shell in double quotes (`$50`, backticks: now single quotes); `clear` with words ignored them (refused); a replaced note was silent (the read-back says so); the markdown kept file words (ruled, blank, the flag reasons: now plain, "the code it points at changed since it was answered"). **Left, listed:** answering again with the answer already there changes no line, so blame keeps the first person; on a hand-edited file `statement` and `pmNote` come back quoted and comments go (the `ruling`/`matches` lines are kept as typed, which is what blame reads); a section's `yes` also clears the flags of its unanswered matches (answering is looking); a note can't be emptied. → the PM's view becomes an index, area summaries and module files (D415)
- D370 · **Layer 3 of the truth map (email templates in the graph, D362 (5); `code-graph` v6 → v7) built and fresh-checked** (2026-11-17; Sonnet, ~112K tokens; `tm-layer3-check.md` in the session notes). A `template-files` detector (`TemplateFilesDetector.cs`, a component on by default like every detector): a class's string constant that is a file-name pattern (`"{0}.html"`, `"Templates/{0}.liquid"`) and its other string constants whose value is a base name give a 🟡 `sends-template` line from each constant to a new `template-file` point per repo file the filled pattern names; walked by impact and page slices (a template's change reaches the constant and the code using it). Kavita: `EmailService`'s 14 constants plus `GetEmailBody("base")`, 22 lines to 22 templates in `Kavita.Server/EmailTemplates/` and `config/templates/`, one blind spot (`config/templates/EmailMigration.html`, which nothing names); Sonarr and nopCommerce 0; Kavita's flows byte for byte the same with the detector off or on. D20: 1a provides `code-graph` v7, the truth map consumes v7, r2, r3 and 4 (on v6) marked `needs review`. **My calls (the owner's standing word):** (1) a whole string literal in one of the class's members names a template too, from that member (Kavita's frame `base.html` is sent that way); (2) **a template folder** (after the check): a match counts only in a folder where two or more of the class's names find a file, or the folder the pattern names, never the repo's root otherwise; rejected: scoping to the class's own project, since Kavita's templates sit in `Kavita.Server` and the sender in `Kavita.Services`; (3) on by default: it adds points and walked lines only, and a ticket bundle's impact walks back from its starting points, so a bundle changes only for a ticket starting at a template. **Fixed from the check:** a lone name matched every same-named file in the repo (`"{0}.json"` with `"package"`), now the template-folder rule; attribute literals named templates; one stray match pulled its folder's other files in as blind spots, and two patterns gave each blind spot twice; the match scanned every repo file per name (now by file name); a template point was added when its sender's point was missing. Left, listed: names held in another class (a shared `EmailTemplates` class) or built at runtime, patterns held in a property, and a class with a single template name in a folder the pattern doesn't name are not linked; matching is case for case; the working tree is read, so an untracked template counts (the graph's dirty files list it). Cost: one build and one fix round; the graph's points grow by the templates found.
- D369 · **Layer 2 of the truth map (rulings and the truth-map files, D363, D366) built and fresh-checked** (2026-11-17; Sonnet, ~140K tokens; `tm-layer2-check.md` in the session notes). `stangent fsd rulings <doc>` drafts or refreshes `specs/fsd/<doc>/rulings.yaml` (contract `rulings` v1); `stangent fsd rulings-check <doc>` checks it, takes who and when from `git blame`, and writes `truth-map.json` (contract `truth-map` v1) and `truth-map.md` only when the check passes (`Rulings.cs`, `TruthMap.cs`). **My calls (the owner's standing word):** (1) a match's confidence counts "on a flow step or a screen's control" as linked by flow or screen, exact or not (a fallback link is the "else a node or file" of D366 (4)); (2) a section's `matches: confirmed` confirms its matches, and a match can also be ruled `confirmed` or `not-a-match` one by one (layer 5's `Ruling:` reply needs that); a match added to a confirmed section gets `new: true` until the PM deletes the line; (3) blank entries no run finds again are dropped and listed; one holding a ruling, statement, note, Jira source or a confirmed match is kept as `notFound` until a person deletes it; (4) a changed section's rulings go blank with a `flagged` reason ("FSD section changed since ruled (was code-right)"), not silently, and a ruling of the wrong kind after the review changed an entry's state (a conflict now a match) is blanked and flagged the same way; (5) a one-entry statement a run called a match and another a difference is the difference, marked `disagrees`; the entry's `notes` are a list (grouped undocumented findings); (6) the PM's `ruling` and `matches` lines are written back as typed, so a refresh never takes blame from the PM; who ruled is the blamed commit's author, an uncommitted ruling shows `committed: false` with a warning. **Fixed from the check:** (a) `inFsd` stayed set after a section changed again (a blank, flagged entry read "the FSD says it now"); (b) a refresh rewrote a CRLF file as LF, so blame named whoever committed the refresh: line endings are kept; (c) a section's change was judged on its text with its subsections', so editing 1.1 blanked every ruling in 1: now its own text, plus any entry whose quote is no longer word for word in the section's text (a quote may come from a subsection); (d) of two equally good links, finding order chose, so a reordered re-review could flag: now the first by kind and id; (e) bad YAML names its line and says text with a colon or `#` goes in quotes; the file's header says comments elsewhere aren't kept and a `notFound` entry stays until deleted. Kavita's saved review: 51 findings, 42 entries (20 undocumented after grouping by link), flow 11, screen 27, node 4, file 0 (fallback 9.5%), refreshes carry all 42, rebuilds byte for byte the same. Left, listed: the saved runs have no matches, so confidence, `new` and section confirmation are tested with fakes only; a statement typed unquoted with a `#` loses what follows (YAML's comment), unwarned; the PM's block-scalar layout of a statement becomes one quoted string on a refresh (words kept); the truth-map files are generated, so whether a project commits them is its choice (rebuild after committing rulings, or who and when stay empty). Cost: one build round and one fix round; the docx rebuilt from the saved outline hashes differently from the one the saved runs read, so `fsd rulings` on Kavita warns that the review is of an older version.
- D368 · **Layer 1 of the truth map (links and the App frame, D362) built and fresh-checked** (2026-11-16; Sonnet, ~108K tokens; `tm-layer1-check.md` in the session notes). **App frame** (`inventory` v1 → v2): the root is a part no other part renders, no screen starts at and no code opens as a dialog, mounted from a file that calls the framework's mount function (`bootstrapApplication`, `bootstrapModule`, `createRoot`, `hydrateRoot`, `ReactDOM.render`), named in `bootstrap: [..]`, or `_Layout.cshtml`; a part in the root's own file that nothing renders joins it (a router's layout element: Sonarr's `PageLayout`); what they reach, unplaced so far, moves in. My call: "used from a file's top level" alone found 50 roots on Sonarr (React files use components at top level everywhere), so the mount call is required. Kavita: `AppComponent`, 4 groups, 59 lines (the checker: everything in it is drawn from the root, the main and side nav included); Sonarr: `App`, 2 groups, 53 lines; nopCommerce: no root. **Links** (`stangent fsd links <doc>`, `TruthLinks.cs`): flow, else screen, else node, else file, per evidence line; an exact step or control beats a walked method or a nearby control; a file holding several groups goes to the nearest inventory line (a tie to the one before); an undocumented finding's route names its screen; each link carries a fingerprint of what it points at. Kavita's 51 saved findings: flow 12, screen 35, node 4, file 0 (fallback 7.8%; D361's hand count 12/34/5/0). Fixed from the check: the nearest group instead of the largest in the file (F-022, F-027, F-031 had landed on a 23-line menu), exact before walked (F-010 now its route's own step, not a shared helper's flow), no crash on a line number past `int`, `path:10-` and `path:10,12` read as line 10, the route override for undocumented findings only, `_Layout.cshtml` exactly (not `_LayoutEmail`). The checker's 10 sampled links: 6 right, 4 wrong, all 4 right after the fixes; the 3 node links right. Left, listed: a server app with several layouts (nopCommerce) has no single frame; a data menu read only from a template property (Kavita's nav items) stays Not placed; a line in a screen's own component with no controls of its own (F-003, a library screen with 0 lines) falls to a node link; a screen's fingerprint is its whole JSON, so any change on that screen flags every ruling on it; mount detection reads raw text (a mount call in a comment counts).
- D367 · **Layer 0 of the truth map (matches in the FSD review, D366) built and fresh-checked** (2026-11-16; Sonnet, ~90K tokens; `tm-layer0-check.md` in the session notes): 12 of 12 review tests and the whole suite pass. Fixed from the check: (1) a sentence reported in one run as a match and as a gap or conflict keeps the difference and sends only the match to the retry (it had sent both, so a sentence holding two rules, "20 orders per page, newest first" with the paging right and the order wrong, could lose its real conflict), and the instructions say to report such a sentence as the difference only; (2) the output's cap went from 300 to 600 findings, and a match is reported only for a statement whose code the agent read, one per statement (a schema failure fails the whole output, so a long list of matches could cost the run its differences); (3) `review-merge --help` said v1. Left, listed: code doesn't check that a matched line does what its sentence says (a wide range passes; `review.md` says so, and the PM confirms matches, D366 (4)); the same sentence quoted under a section in one run and its subsection in another, or quoted shorter, isn't seen as the same (no join, no `disagreesWith`); counts of findings now include matches, and `benchmark/results/2026-11-15-fsd-review-free/review_to_trace.py` is a v1-only script.
- D366 · **The truth map covers every FSD sentence the code follows, not only the differences** (owner, 2026-11-16: the PM should "review all the key linkages to make sure they are align", with an md for the PM and JSON "deterministically readable by agents", showing the whole feature: "metadata, confidence, actor, journey, edge cases"; then "Yes, add matches"). (1) **The FSD review also reports matches**: a fourth state, `match`, with the FSD quote (word for word, as gaps and conflicts) and the `file:line` the code does it at, checked by the same code checks; merge joins matches by section and quote; `fsd-review-output` and `fsd-review` v1 → v2. (2) **The PM reviews everything, by section:** one confirm per section for its matches (example: "§4.2's six matches look right"), a ruling per difference; a match the PM rejects is marked `not-a-match` and counts as unclear. (3) **Two files made by code from the same data, no AI writing them:** `specs/fsd/<doc>/truth-map.json` for agents and `truth-map.md` for the PM: per section, the FSD text, each sentence's state and code link, the linked flow's actor, steps, conditions and scenarios (the edge cases), its 🟡/❓ count, the ruling and who made it (git), and how sure (found by every run or one; flow or fallback link). The code side is what layer 3's flows already hold; this joins it to the FSD. Why: a sentence the code follows had no entry, so a ticket it backs ("deleting a library does nothing", the FSD says admins can delete one) would stop as unclear and ask the PM what the FSD already says; this answers D365's Q1. (4) **How sure a match is, set by code** (owner, same day, after "rationally speaking, adding matches would help is it?" and "if its optional, didnt we go back to the same old issue": "i kinda like it if the confidence on the linkage is medium or low", then "we will ping the pm in the ticket comment to verify first, and of course during the setup it should already flag to the PM"): two signals, whether every review run found the match and whether its line is on a flow step or a screen's control (else a node or file, D362); **high** both, **medium** one, **low** neither. High routes a ticket at once, shown as not yet confirmed, and a backed verdict says so. Medium or low: a ticket resting on it stops as unclear, writes no test, and the questions draft asks the PM in the ticket's Jira comment to verify (example: "§4.2 says deleting a library removes its series; does the code at `LibraryService.cs:212` do that?"); the PM's confirmation lets it go on. **At setup** `rulings-check` and `truth-map.md` list the medium and low matches first, so the PM verifies them before tickets reach them. The PM's review is done only when every section's matches are confirmed; tickets don't wait on high ones. Why the PM still confirms: matches are where the AI is least checked (code checks the quote and that the line exists, not that the line does what the sentence says), and their false-match rate is unmeasured. Rejected: the reproducer reading the whole FSD (no PM check on what it relies on); PM confirmation optional (back to AI links nobody checked, D342). Not the old route back: links come from the review (26–29 of 30, 0–8% false alarms), not D342's cut linker (24 of 30, 31–44%). Cost: each review run outputs more (my guess +30–50%); matches flicker run to run like findings; the saved Kavita runs have no matches, so testing them needs a live review run (paid, asked first; headless `claude -p` limited until 2026-11-18); planted keys hold only differences, so false matches are counted by a fresh check on a sample (~100–150K tokens); the PM's review grows with the FSD. → the PM's view becomes an index, area summaries and module files (D415)
- D365 · **The truth map's build plan (`phases/tm-truth-map.md`) and the calls it needed** (my calls on the owner's standing word, 2026-11-14, each with an example): (1) **Links before rulings:** layer 1 is the linker and the App frame group, layer 2 `rulings.yaml`, because an undocumented entry's key is its link (D363); then email templates (`code-graph` v7), the bundle and verdict, `Ruling:` replies. (2) **Undocumented findings sharing a link and section are one entry**, ruled once: runs cite different lines for the same thing (D341: no two runs cited the same line), so nothing finer survives a new run. Example: three left-out options on Kavita's preferences screen are one entry; for a mixed answer the PM writes `restated`: "keep all but the debug toggle". (3) **A flag is a stored hash:** each entry keeps its link's hash; when it changes on a ruled entry `fsd rulings` writes `flagged: "link changed since ruled"`, and the PM deletes that line once they have looked; git shows who. (4) **An entry applies to a ticket** when one of its evidence files is a ticket file (starting point or found file) or its screen is a ticket's page; the link is for grouping and flagging, not matching, since a flow touching a shared service would pull in half the app. (5) **The verdict is in the reproducer's same call**, before any test: `backed`, `contradicted` or `unclear` with the entry and a word-for-word quote; code forces `unclear` when the cited entry is blank, flagged or not found again, or nothing is cited; only `backed` goes on to a test. A separate call would cost a second AI round per ticket for the same reading. (6) **`ticket pull` writes the drafted entry into `rulings.yaml` uncommitted**, and says so; the git diff is the review, the commit the confirmation (D364). (7) **Contracts:** `rulings` v1 (new), `inventory` v1 → v2 (App frame; no built consumer), `code-graph` v6 → v7 (consumers `needs review`, D20), `ticket-bundle` v2 → v3 (Phase 4 `needs review`), `bug-proof` and `bug-reproducer-output` v1 → v2. Cost: five narrow fresh checks, about 0.5–0.75M tokens; the PM rules coarser on undocumented code than the review reports it.
- D364 · **When the truth map doesn't settle a ticket, `ticket prove` stops and stangent helps the developer ask in Jira** (owner, 2026-11-16: "yes stop and ask and help the dev add a comment in the jira ticket which is applicable for things that are unclear"). **Unclear** means any of: no ruling and no FSD sentence covers the ticket ("not covered"); the ruling it lands on is marked "not found by the last review" or flagged because its link changed and nobody has looked again (D362, D363); the ticket matches a ruling in part only. Then no test is written, `bug-proof.json` records why it stopped, and the existing questions draft (`ticket draft <KEY> --kind questions`, D98: each sentence backed by a checked claim) asks the open point, quoting what the truth map does say and the ticket's own words. Example: "a deleted series still shows on the dashboard", nothing covers it: "Should a deleted series disappear from the dashboard? Nothing in the FSD or the agreed rulings says." The developer posts it under their own login (`ticket post`, D74). The answer becomes a ruling in `rulings.yaml` (D363), and `ticket prove` runs again against it. **Contradicted** (D361) uses the same draft, quoting the ruling. Why: a test is never written for a rule nobody agreed to, and the question goes where the reporter and PM already talk. **The answer comes back through Jira and is kept twice** (owner, same day: "pm confirms it, but devs can confirm if they want, preferably put in jira comment so we can pull it, but also kept as an extra record as well"): the answer is a Jira comment, so `ticket pull` reads it; stangent drafts the `rulings.yaml` entry from it (ruling, statement, a link to the comment, its Jira author and date); a person confirms it by committing, the PM by preference, a developer allowed; the file is the record that survives Jira. Git gives who confirmed, Jira who answered, and the entry shows both when they differ. **My call on the owner's standing word:** the question ends with how to answer ("Reply with a line starting `Ruling:`"), and only such a reply is read as one, so a chatty comment never becomes a rule; a reply that doesn't parse is listed, not guessed. Cost: the ticket waits for an answer; a ruling confirmed by a developer is weaker than the PM's, shown, not refused.
- D363 · **Rulings live in a committed file the PM fills in** (owner, 2026-11-16: "Yes, committed file"). `specs/fsd/<doc>/rulings.yaml`, one per FSD: stangent writes an entry per FSD-review finding (section, quote, links as D362) with the ruling left blank; the PM fills `ruling` (code-right, fsd-right; keep, remove for undocumented code) and an optional `note`. Code checks every finding has a ruling and every value is one of those; who ruled and when come from git, as the old route's `decided_by` did, so a ruling can't be signed in someone else's name and every change has a history. A form (Phase 8, the dashboard) later writes the same file. Why: a file in the repo is reviewed, versioned and diffable with the code it rules on, and needs nothing built for people to use it today. Cost: a PM has to edit a file in git until the form exists. **The PM can state the rule** (owner, 2026-11-16: the PM "should be able to rectify it with a statement other than just marking if its true or not"): a third ruling, `restated`, with a required `statement` in the PM's words (example: §2.3 says one device, the code allows any number, the PM writes "two devices; admins any number"); any ruling may carry one, `restated` must. The statement is then the judge a ticket is checked against and quoted, the FSD's sentence goes on the list of FSD changes, and the code is listed to be checked against it (it may differ from both). **A ruling's key** (owner: "ok sure"): the review's ids (`F-001` …) change run to run (`docs/spec.md`), so a gap or conflict is keyed by its FSD section id and quote (section ids survive FSD versions through the import's ledger; quotes are checked word for word), undocumented code by its D362 link. On a new review run: the same key carries the ruling; a new finding gets a blank entry; an old ruling no run finds again is kept, marked "not found by the last review", and listed for the PM (the review flickers, D324: 26–28 of 30 per run, so a miss isn't proof the code changed); a section whose text changed drops its rulings back to blank, unless the new text holds the PM's statement, which closes a `restated` ruling as "in the FSD now".
- D362 · **How a truth-map entry is linked (D361), and email templates** (owner, 2026-11-16: the inventory's first and third costs "fine", asked how the second is dealt with; "ok sure" to the email templates). (1) **Each entry links to the most specific thing that fits:** a flow (the statement is behaviour), else a screen or shared part from the inventory (what a screen shows or offers; read-only, not D337's checklist or steps 4–7), else the graph node its lines sit in, else the file itself. On Kavita's 51 findings: 12, 34, 5, 0 (D361's check). (2) **A ruling is flagged for the PM again** when what it links to changes: the flow, a line on that screen, the node, the file. (3) **App frame:** what the app's root component draws or opens is placed under one group, "App frame (on every screen)", by a general rule (Angular's bootstrap component, React's root render, the Razor layout), never an app's names; on Kavita it covers the inventory's 6 groups, 86 lines, not placed today (`AppComponent`, the side-nav menu and item, the download drawer, a nav dialog, the theme). (4) **The truth map prints how many entries used a fallback** (a node or file link), so a growing share is seen, never silent. (5) **Email templates:** level 1, linked by file through (1), part of the truth map; level 2, the graph knows which code sends which template (Kavita: `EmailConfirmTemplate = "EmailConfirm"` with `TemplatePath = "{0}.html"`, a string constant matched to a template file name in the project, 🟡 when the name is built at runtime), built with the truth map, not held (owner, 2026-11-16: "i like 2 also"); it bumps `code-graph` v6 → v7 (D20). Why: 97% of Kavita's source files (2,292 of 2,372, tests and migrations aside) have graph nodes; the 80 without are mostly email templates (15), event models (24), pipes (8), config templates (8), so the file link means no finding is ever unlinked. Cost: the inventory is rebuilt on every code change and its faults become the truth map's (a wrong screen name puts a ruling under the wrong screen); the truth map is only as complete as the review that fills it (28–29 of 30 planted on Kavita), and a missed difference is ruled when a ticket reaches it ("not covered").
- D361 · **The judge of a ticket is the truth map, not the FSD alone** (owner, 2026-11-16: the FSD is the original source, then the code, then the difference between them, which the PM checks and marks "code right" or "FSD right"; "truth map is okay"). **Truth map:** for each FSD statement, the code it ties to (`file:line`) and whether they match; where they differ, the PM's **ruling**: code right (the FSD gets updated), FSD right (the code has a bug, a ticket can follow), and for code the FSD never mentions, keep or remove. **Up front** (owner, 2026-11-16: "up front"): the PM rules on every finding once after the FSD review, in batches by FSD section (the review already groups findings that way), then one batch of code no section covers (undocumented), so every ticket has a judge when it lands; rejected: ruling only when a ticket reaches a section (less PM work, but the first ticket in an area waits). A ticket is then backed, contradicted or not covered by the truth map, which answers D96's "a reproduced test shows a difference, not a bug" without a person each time. Example: the FSD says a refund sends an email, the code sends none, the PM ruled "code right"; a ticket "refund sends no email" is contradicted, so no test demands the email. Found on the way (D358 corrected): `TicketBundle.Markdown` prints an FSD section's id, title and link states, never its text, so the reproducer has never read an FSD rule. Where it stands: the old route's `specs/trace.yaml` was this map in shape (links, states, a person's decisions), frozen by D342 (no new links, `trace approve`/`confirm`/`send-back` cut) and only on Sonarr; the FSD review (D341) finds the differences but has no ruling step (`docs/spec.md`: "no findings-to-decisions flow exists yet"). D358's measurement waits on the truth map's design (`benchmark/tickets/kavita-fsd-rules/plan.md`, frozen before this, to be revised). Why: the FSD can be out of date; only the PM's ruling says which side is right. Cost: a new store and a PM step before tickets can use it; the measurement moves later. **Free check: do the review's lines land in flows? (owner: "ok run the free check"; `benchmark/tickets/kavita-fsd-rules/flow-anchor/`, the 51 merged Kavita findings, no AI):** only 12 of 51 findings have a line on a flow step or in a method a flow walks (conflict 4 of 12, gap 0 of 10, undocumented 8 of 29); 33 more are in a file a flow touches but not that line, 6 in files no flow touches. Why: flows hold actions and requests, while the evidence is mostly what a screen shows (template lines, 68 of 107), repository bodies past a flow's database end, startup configuration, enum values and menu data. By folder, 50 of 51 reach an area, 21 of them more than one. 106 of 107 lines sit inside a graph node (template 68, method 17, property 11, field 5, page 5). **So (my call on the owner's standing word, reversing my own suggestion):** a truth-map entry is anchored on the graph nodes its evidence lines sit in, and a ruling is flagged for the PM again when any of those nodes changes; the area (by folder) groups entries for the PM; flows are listed only where a line is in one. Flows as the anchor would have missed a change to three entries in four. **Part 3, with the parked inventory (owner: flows should be fixed, improved or rethought, since the truth map must be linked):** per finding, a flow 12, an inventory line itself 3, a file the inventory places on a screen or shared part 31, a graph node only 5 (startup configuration, an entity, a DTO, a controller's top, an enum), none 0. Read: the misses are not a flow fault; FSD statements are of three kinds, behaviour (flows), what a screen shows or offers (the inventory's screens), and rules with neither (graph nodes). Settled in D362.
- D360 · **A fixed run's report names the same code left unchanged elsewhere** (owner, 2026-11-16: "Yes, build it", on D359's recommendation). After a `fixed` stop, code takes each line the fixer replaced (at least 15 characters, not a comment or `using`, not re-added) and searches the repo's tracked files for it word for word (`git grep -F`, the proof's test and `.stangent/` left out). Every other place is listed in `report.md` under "Same code elsewhere, not changed" and in `agent-run.json` as `sameCodeElsewhere` (an optional field; `agent-run` stays v1 because nothing reads it yet). It's a note for the developer, never a failed check: a twin may be right as it is. On D345 it would have named SONB-15's `SourceSpecification`; it can't see SONB-23 or SONB-24, whose second place is different code. Also fixed: `agent-run` v1's `context` didn't allow `files`, so D357's default would have made every `fix report` refuse (the test rig now prepares with `files`). Why: no AI, cheap, and it catches the commonest partial fix (a copied flaw). Cost: a word-for-word search misses a twin written differently; at most 20 lines and 10 places each.
- D359 · **D345 judged: 9 of 12 clean on both sides; a green fix missed part of the bug on 3** (2026-11-16, a fresh Sonnet agent, ~107K tokens, blind: the 24 fixes shuffled with their side removed; marks in `benchmark/tickets/sonarr/fix-test/judgments.json`, reasons in `judgments-why.json`). The bar reads `failed` (B 9 vs A 9, 127% of A's tokens), as D357 said it would. **Both sides missed the same part on the same 3 bugs:** SONB-15 (the same `NotEmpty()` flaw in a second validator, `SourceSpecification`; checked by hand), SONB-23 (the cookie jar still reusing stored credentials), SONB-24 (the Discord and Slack title builders still crash on an empty list). So **green means the test's path is fixed, not the bug**: the proof test checks one path, and nothing makes the fixer look for the same flaw elsewhere. The bundle didn't help with that either. Open in `questions.md`: what the loop does about it. Why: recorded as measured. Cost: until something checks it, about 1 in 4 green fixes on bugs like these is partial.
- D358 · **D357 keeps the FSD where the test is written, not in the fixer's default** (2026-11-16; the owner: the bundle is still useful later, because with code as the only truth the agent does what it thinks best, not the business rule, e.g. the FSD says a refund must send an email and the code never does, so the agent never knows). Agreed that D345 couldn't test that: in all 12 bugs the ticket and test were enough. My answer, kept: in the fix loop **the failing test is what carries a business rule to the fixer**, and it's checked by code (D18); an FSD rule the test doesn't check is one nothing checks, read or not. The bug reproducer, which writes that test, gets the bundle (`ticket prove`, `forProof`), but **corrected 2026-11-16 (the fsd-rule-bugs session, checked in `Bundle.cs`): only each FSD section's id, title and link states, never its text**, so today no step can carry an FSD-only rule into the test; how the reproducer gets the section text is open (that session's plan, `benchmark/tickets/kavita-fsd-rules/plan.md`). So the refund-email rule belongs in the proof test ("after a refund, one email is sent"), and then even side A has to send it. The gap that's left: a rule the FSD states, the ticket doesn't mention and the test doesn't check. Open in `questions.md`: measure it on bugs where the rule lives only in the FSD. Why: put the rule where code can check it. Cost: until measured, a rule missing from the test is missed by the fixer either way.
- D357 · **D345's result: the bundle fails its bar; the fixer gets the ticket, the failing test and the finder's files** (2026-11-16, run by the recheck session on the 12 unit-test bugs, D356, stangent `89e0b16`, Sonnet 5.5 medium, $2.20 recorded in all). **Side A** (ticket) fixed 12 of 12 for 674K tokens ($0.70); **side B** (bundle) fixed 12 of 12 for 854K (+27%, $0.92); B used more on 10 of 12. The frozen bar needs B ≥ A + 2 clean with tokens within 10%, or the same count for ≥ 10% fewer, so it fails whatever the clean-or-symptom judging finds. As D345 decided for a fail: **`fix prepare --context files` is the new default** (the ticket, the code, the failing test and the finder's starting files as a list, no reasons, pages, FSD or impact); `ticket` and `bundle` stay for measuring. My calls, and doubts: (1) **ceiling:** A fixed every bug, so these 12 bugs needed nothing beyond the ticket and the test; D345 shows the bundle costs tokens on easy bugs, not that it can't help on hard ones. (2) **`files` itself is unmeasured** (between A and B); it adds a few lines, so its token cost is small, but no run shows it helps. (3) **Both sides edited only 1 of the developer's 2–3 files on all 6 bugs where the developer changed more than one** (SONB-15, 21, 23, 24, 25, 26): the test passes, but whether the fix is whole is the fresh judge's question; that judging no longer decides D345, but it decides whether the loop's green means fixed. Also: the harness's exit now puts the clone back as `at` does (SDK pin, no leftover test). Why: D345's bar was frozen before the run, and its fail branch was decided then. Cost: a harder bug set is needed before the bundle (or `files`) can be shown to help a fix.
- D356 · **`fix.localFiles` takes folders; D345 is scored on its 12 unit-test bugs** (2026-11-16, the recheck session's fifth fault: SONB-3's check 4 failed both rounds with `./.stangent-app/build.sh: No such file or directory`, since the benchmark's untracked app scripts live only in the clone). (1) A `localFiles` entry may be a folder: copied whole into the worktree, kept out of the dirty-base, files and commit checks, and not the fixer's to edit (`Edit(<folder>/**)` denied). The Sonarr clone needs `fix: { localFiles: [global.json, .stangent-app/] }`. (2) **The 5 Playwright bugs (SONB-3/4/5/7/8) stay out of D345 for now**: even with their scripts copied, check 4 can't pass them, since D352 requires the other tests to be checked and a Playwright target has no full run. So D345's sides compare 12 bugs; the frozen bar (B ≥ A + 2 clean and green, tokens within 10%, or the same count for ≥ 10% fewer) is unchanged. Why: (1) is any repo's uncommitted build scripts, not just the benchmark's; (2) a run that can only end `check-failed` costs money and measures nothing. Cost: a smaller sample (12, not 17) makes a +2 edge easier to reach by chance; the Playwright bugs wait for a full-run equivalent for e2e targets (decided, not built).
- D355 · **A fix's commit skips excludes git already ignores** (2026-11-16, found on the D345 run by the recheck session): `CommitEdits` ran `git add -A -- . :(exclude)stangent.yaml :(exclude).stangent`, and git refuses an ignored path named in a pathspec, so in a repo that git-ignores `stangent.yaml` (or all of `.stangent/`) every stop failed to commit. Now each exclude is passed only when `git check-ignore` says it isn't ignored; `add -A` skips ignored files anyway. Why: committing the edits is how the branch holds the fix (D351 (4)). Cost: one `check-ignore` per excluded path per stop.
- D354 · **Three wiring faults from D345's first real fixer call** (2026-11-16, the recheck session's run stopped after SONB-12, about $0.20; fakes can't see any of them): (1) **The fixer isn't started with `--agent`**: the agent file's `tools:` list dropped the StructuredOutput tool `--json-schema` needs ("No such tool available: StructuredOutput", so check 1 failed twice with no plan), while the file finder, called without `--agent` and with `--tools`, has returned structured output in every real run; `fix-run.sh` now calls the fixer the same way (no `--plugin-dir`, the instructions are in its input). (2) **The read-only hook exempts the fixer** as it does the reproducer (it refused the fixer's Edit as "read-only"); moot once (1) holds, kept for a person's own `--agent` use. (3) **The proof test is committed with `git add -f`**, so a test under an ignored folder (Sonarr's `e2e/`, SONB-3/4/5/7/8) can start a fix. Why: each made every fix fail whatever the bug. Cost: none known; (1) is inferred from the finder's working calls, not yet shown on a fixer call: the next paid call is the test.
- D353 · **Untracked files never make a fix's base dirty** (2026-11-16, found by the D345 recheck session's first real `fix start` on Sonarr; fakes hid it because the test rig git-ignored `stangent.yaml`): `fix start` refused `dirty_base` for any repo that doesn't commit `stangent.yaml`, though it copies exactly that file into the worktree, and for untracked page output (`specs/`). Now only a changed tracked file is dirt; untracked files (other than the proof's test, `.stangent/`, `stangent.yaml` and `fix.localFiles`) are named in a start warning, since they never reach the worktree. Why: an untracked file can't change the commit the fix starts from. Cost: an untracked file the build needs is left out silently but for the warning; list it in `fix.localFiles`. The running D345 benchmark is unaffected (its build predates this; its clone ignores both paths in `.git/info/exclude`).
- D352 · Fixes from the fresh check (D158) of D351's build (2026-11-16, a fresh Sonnet agent, ~144K tokens, read-only; 3 medium and 5 low faults, none high), all taken: (1) **check 4 fails when the other tests weren't checked** (`--verify`'s `othersChecked` false: the full run timed out or gave no results), so D343's "the other tests still pass" is never assumed; a Playwright target, which has no full run, can't pass the loop yet. (2) **A test is never in reach, and an existing test file can't be planned:** a test reaching the change could otherwise be weakened by the fixer and pass checks 2–4. (3) **The scorer compares only bugs both sides ran**, so a partial or resumed run can't tilt the bar. (4) **Green means test reach passed:** a "fixed, test reach not checked" run is scored as `fixed-reach-not-checked`, not green (D346 step 3). (5) Test names match exactly (a full method name, or the same last part), not by substring, in check 1's `test` reason and check 5. (6) The harness's recheck qualifies a bug only when its test failed at the parent (`same-failure` or `different-failure`), not on `not-compiled` or `not-run`. (7) The harness's commands read no stdin from its loop, and any driver exit but 0 or 1 stops the run; a side B bundle cut short is put back by the exit trap. (8) `fix-run.sh` never `rm`s `/dev/null` when stangent gives no envelope. Also: `stangent.yaml` in the worktree is never the fixer's to edit, tracked or not. Left as is: a proof test that exists at `--base` and differs in the checkout is taken from the checkout (moot for D345, where it's new at the parent); "same count" in D345's bar is read literally (B = A + 1 with fewer tokens fails). Why: each let a check pass, or a score read, wrong. Cost: Playwright bugs wait for a full-run equivalent before the loop can fix them.
- D351 · Building D350's minimal loop, my calls (2026-11-16, steps 1–8 of `phases/4-agents.md`, all with fakes, nothing paid): (1) **A plan's point may be a name** (`OrderService.Place`) that code resolves to the graph id declared in that file at the base commit; the fixer can't run `stangent graph`. A new file carries no point and no reach. (2) **The fixer reads nothing of stangent's:** its input goes in on stdin and its plan comes back as structured output, and it may read and edit only inside the worktree, never `.stangent/`, `.git/`, the proof's test or a copied `stangent.yaml` (deny rules). So side A of D345 can't open the bundle the worktree holds no copy of. (3) **Checks stop at the first failure;** the later ones aren't run, so a retry is told about one failure, not a pile. (4) **Whatever the fixer edited is committed on the branch whenever the ticket stops** (`fix: <KEY>` or `wip: <KEY> (stopped: …)`), so the branch is what the report names; `fix-run.sh` checks the developer's checkout is unchanged (T.5). (5) **`fix-run.sh` passes D348's Sonnet 5.5 at medium effort as the run's flags** unless `--model`/`--effort` are given: flags win over `stangent.yaml`, as on every driver, so D161's "never a default" holds (the setting is named, recorded, and printed in the usage). (6) **`fix.localFiles`:** files a build needs that the repo doesn't commit (the benchmark's SDK pin over Sonarr's `global.json`) don't make the base dirty, are copied into the worktree, are left out of every check and can't be edited; without it the D345 harness couldn't start a single ticket. (7) **Telemetry (D21):** each `fix check` event carries its call's cost (so `guard_estimate "fix check"` has a per-call figure); the `fix report` event carries the model, the fixer's tokens and the checks count, not the cost again. (8) **The run's stop is a lifecycle, `fix-run`, in the state module** (D114): in-progress → fixed, gave-up, cap-hit, check-failed; no-failing-test; `fix start --replace` starts any of them again. (9) **Contracts:** `fixer-output` v1 is the agent's answer (changes with file, point, one `why`; or `gaveUp`); `task-plan` v1 adds code's reach, the base commit and the test; `agent-run` v1 has a proof step with `turns: null` (the proof's record keeps no turns). (10) **The D345 harness** re-derives its 17 bugs with a free recheck (the saved test must fail at the parent and pass at the fix) rather than trusting the 2026-11-02 list; side B's bundle is rebuilt on the parent's graph so it names no code from after the fix; the clean-or-symptom marks are read from `fix-test/judgments.json`, and the bar reads `pending` until every green fix has one. (11) `ticket-run.sh`'s round counting moved to `ai-run.sh` (`result_with_rounds`), which `fix-run.sh` shares. Why: each follows from D343–D350 once the code met them; (2), (5) and (6) were needed for the measurement to mean anything. Cost: reach is file-level (D339) and only from the graph at the base, so a fix in a file the graph doesn't link (config, SQL) fails check 2 until it's planned; a plan of only new files can't have its test reach checked.
- D350 · How D343–D349 fit the codebase, my calls on the owner's "go ahead" (2026-11-16; plan `phases/4-agents.md`): (1) **"`stangent fix <KEY>`" is `plugin/scripts/fix-run.sh <KEY>`** driving `stangent fix start | prepare | check | report`, the prepare → headless Claude Code → check shape every AI step already has (D60, D162); `/stangent:fix` runs the script. (2) **Bugs only, first:** D346's first-step test is the bug proof's (`ticket prove`, outcome `reproduced`), committed first on `stangent/fix-<KEY>`; a ticket without one stops with "no failing test". A test writer for features is built after D345's result, since D345 measures bugs only. (3) **The after-fix test check is the proof's `--verify` (D105)** run in the worktree: only `passed` passes. (4) **Test reach (D346 step 3)** is checked by rebuilding the graph in the worktree and finding the proof test in `graph impact`'s `tests` group of a planned point; a graph that can't be rebuilt marks it "not checked", never passed. (5) **Reach is filled by code** from `graph impact --file` on each planned file, not by the agent. (6) **The worktree** sits at `.stangent/fix/<KEY>/worktree` (git-ignored), base = the checkout's HEAD or `--base`; the proof's cost counts toward the ticket's $3. (7) **D345's sides** are `fix prepare --context ticket|bundle`. Why: reuse what Phase 3 built and measured instead of a second test writer and runner. Cost: features can't go through the loop until the test writer exists; the minimal loop inherits the proof's limits (D76's targets, one ticket at a time when an app runs).
- D349 · Phase 4's last open details, my calls on the owner's standing word (2026-11-16, closing the Phase 4 grill): (1) **A stopped run's report** (no test possible, D346; cap hit, D348; a check failed after the retry, D343) says why it stopped, the plan (where, why, reach), the last failed check's output, and the branch if anything was edited; example: "stopped: no test can show this (a colour change). Plan: `SeriesDetails.css` · why: ticket 'the button is the wrong blue'. No edits." (2) **Contracts, v1:** `task-plan` holds D344's changes (file, graph point id, one reason of kind ticket quote, FSD quote or failing test, reach list) and D346's test (file, name, failed-before output); `agent-run` is one record per loop run: ticket key, branch, base commit, model and effort, each step's turns and cost, each check's result with its output file, and the stop reason; D21's telemetry events are written from it. (3) **Inputs:** phases 0, 1a (graph and impact), 2 (FSD sections the bundle names) and 3 (`ticket-bundle`, unchanged, D69); not 2c. Why: these follow from D343–D348 and change nothing a person sees beyond the report. Cost: the contracts are fixed before the minimal loop is built, so building may bump them to v2 before anything reads them.
- D348 · The fix loop runs every AI step on Sonnet 5.5 at medium effort, each step capped at 30 turns and $1, each ticket at $3, each run by `--max-cost` (owner, 2026-11-16: "Yes, Sonnet, $3", on the recommendation; Phase 4 grill Q6). Same settings on both D345 sides, so a difference is the bundle's; the step caps are the bug proof's (D86's 30 turns, $1), passed to headless Claude Code as `ticket-run.sh` already does. A ticket that hits $3 (test + fix + one retry) stops, keeps what it has on its branch, and its report says "cap hit". Example: D345's 34 runs at an estimated $0.25–1 each run under `--max-cost 35`. Opus 5.5 only as an extra side after D345's result, if Sonnet's clean-and-green count is low, decided on the number. Why: the model of the proof and of D321/D339's agents, so results compare; several times cheaper than Opus. Turned down: Opus from the start. Cost: Sonnet may fail hard multi-file bugs Opus would fix; the fix test shows that as a low count.
- D347 · The loop runs as `stangent fix <KEY>` (with `/stangent:fix` only calling it), on its own branch in its own git worktree; it never pushes and hands back the branch and a report (owner, 2026-11-16: "stangent fix, own branch", on the recommendation; Phase 4 grill Q5). Each AI step runs through headless Claude Code with model and effort pinned and recorded (D162, D161); code runs every D343/D346 check itself. Branch `stangent/fix-<KEY>`, so the developer's checkout is never touched; pushing and the PR stay a person's (D18). The report: the plan, each check passed or failed, and every edited file outside the plan's reach. Example: `stangent fix SONB-14` → branch `stangent/fix-SONB-14`, "test written · fails before ✓; fix · test passes ✓ · other tests ✓; 1 file outside reach: flagged". Why: in the developer's own session the agent decides whether to call the checks, and a check the AI may skip isn't one. Turned down: the developer's session runs the agent and calls `stangent fix check` per check. Cost: depends on headless Claude Code, which hit its weekly limit twice in the week of 2026-11-09; no steering mid-run, only a re-run with a note.
- D346 · Every ticket in the D343 loop starts test first: the agent writes a test before the fix, code checks it fails on the unchanged code and reaches at least one planned change point in the graph, and a ticket with no such test stops after the plan and goes to the developer (owner, 2026-11-16: "Yes, test first", on the recommendation; Phase 4 grill Q4; makes D18's "a test must fail without the change and pass with it" the loop's first step). **Steps:** (1) the agent writes the test; (2) code runs it on the unchanged code: it must fail; (3) code checks through the graph that the test reaches a D344 "where" point, so a test failing for an unrelated reason doesn't count; (4) the fix, then D343's checks, including that test now passing; (5) no such test possible (a colour, a doc) → the plan goes to the developer, no edits. A bug the proof already reproduced uses the proof's test as step 1's. Example (feature): "show a 'missing since' date on Wanted › Missing": the agent first writes an API test that `/api/v3/wanted/missing` returns the field; it fails today and reaches the planned change in the missing-episodes service; after the fix it passes; the column's look stays a person's review. Why: features and the 8 of 30 bugs the proof didn't reproduce have no failing test, and without one the loop checks only that the code builds and old tests pass. Turned down: fixing without the agent's own test, relying on old tests and a person's review. Cost: one more step per ticket; some tickets come back with only a plan; a test can reach the planned point and still check the wrong thing, which only the senior's PR review catches; D345's sides use the saved test, so this step itself is measured only on tickets without one.
- D345 · Phase 4's first measurement is a two-sided fix test through a minimal D343 loop: does the ticket bundle help an agent fix a bug? (owner, 2026-11-16: "Yes, that test", on the recommendation; Phase 4 grill Q3; answers the 2026-11-02 question "Does the bundle help a fix, or skew it?", now in `decisions/answered-questions.md`). **Bugs:** Sonarr's 17 whose saved failing test fails before the developer's fix and passes after it (`phases/3-tickets.md`, the D105 re-score). **Side A:** the ticket, the code at the fix's parent and the failing test. **Side B:** the same plus the whole bundle (starting files, FSD sections, impact, attachments). **Scored by code:** the saved test green, the other tests still green, files changed against the developer's fix, tokens. **Scored by a fresh agent:** each green fix read beside the developer's and marked clean or a patch over the symptom (SONB-18: a null check on the screen turns the test green and leaves the bad data). **Bar, frozen now:** B passes if it gets at least 2 more of the 17 clean and green with tokens within 10% of A, or the same count for at least 10% fewer tokens (one bug is about 6 points; D339's rerun alone moved 4); if B fails, the fix agent gets only the finder's files and the failing test. **Order:** the minimal loop is built first and tested with fakes (free); the paid run (about $8–34 for 34 runs, the 2026-11-02 estimate) comes after 2026-11-18, asked for first. Dropped from the 2026-11-02 proposals: a ticket-alone side (the loop always gives the failing test when one exists); planting wrong leads (proposal c) waits and runs only if B passes. Why: D320's shape (source alone vs source plus the layer's output) on the job Phase 4 is for, with a key from history and real tests, not an AI. Cost: says nothing about tickets with no failing test (features, and the 8 of 30 bugs the proof didn't reproduce); one repo, public, so the model may know it; the saved tests predate the finder and may need rechecking first.
- D344 · Each change in the fix agent's plan (`task-plan`) names where, why and reach, all checked by code; no requirement or flow step ids (owner, 2026-11-16: "File, reason, reach", on the recommendation; Phase 4 grill Q2; replaces the plan's 2c requirement ids, cut by D342). **Where:** a file and its graph point; code checks the point exists at the ticket's commit. **Why:** exactly one cited reason: the ticket's own words quoted, an FSD section with a quote, or a failing test's name; code checks each quote word for word, as `fsd review` does (D341). **Reach:** what `graph impact` says the change touches; the D343 loop flags an edited file that is in neither the plan's changes nor their reach. Example (a Sonarr bug, monitoring resets after an edit): change `src/NzbDrone.Core/Tv/SeriesService.cs · SeriesService.UpdateSeries`; why: ticket "monitoring resets after I edit the series"; reaches `SeriesController.Update` (API v3), `SeriesControllerV5.Update`. Why: the plan is worth having only if code can check it, and the reach list is what makes the loop's changed-files check possible; flows are accurate but the bundle doesn't read them, and layer 4, the one layer built on them, was parked after helping an agent no more than the code (D318, D319, D323). Turned down: also citing flow step ids (e.g. `add-series · step 133`), which ties Phase 4 to the `flows` contract with no measured gain; and keeping flows current after a fix doesn't need them, since flows are rebuilt from the changed code by code alone, no AI (D176; owner, 2026-11-16: "it helps update the flow, but we also have a rebuild flow feature"). Cost: a reason the agent can't quote (a cause it inferred from code) has no slot, so it goes in as a failing test's name or not at all; reach is file-level as in D339.
- D343 · Phase 4 starts as one fix agent per ticket inside a loop run by code; no splitter or parallel subagents until a measured ticket needs them (owner, 2026-11-16: "ok", on the recommendation; first answer of the Phase 4 grill; amends D45's and D49's framing and spec.md item 9's order, not its goal). **The loop:** the ticket bundle goes to one agent; it plans and edits; code checks the result: it builds, the bug's failing test now passes, the other tests still pass, and every changed file is one the plan named or one `graph impact` reaches; a failed check gives one retry with the failure, then the ticket goes back to a person (D18, D138's one attempt). **Parallel subagents** are added only when a measured ticket needs them (it overflows one agent's context or takes too long), tried first on a large multi-part feature ticket, and gated by D320 against the single agent on the same tickets. Why: the graph's split failed its safety bar (D49: wrong about 1 time in 5, so parallel parts must be merged and built together anyway); D320's side-by-sides kept finding that one agent reading the code ties or beats prepared material; and no one has measured whether one agent can fix a ticket from the bundle at all (the owed "does the bundle help a fix?" test). Example: Sonarr SONB-14 is a two-file fix (an API resource and its newer-version copy); one agent does both in one pass, while two subagents would each need the other's change plus the merge-and-build step, for no saving. Turned down: building the splitter now with D49's merge-and-build rule. Cost: the brief's parallel subagents wait behind a test; the first paid run can't happen before 2026-11-18 (headless limit).
- D342 · Layer 7 cuts the old linking route and D156 P8's four switches; reference pages and `specs/trace.yaml` stay, frozen, read only by tickets (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, that cut", on the recommendation; the list below is my call on the owner's standing word). **Cut:** the link route behind `/stangent:fsd-link` (`fsd candidates`, `fsd prepare`, `fsd check`; the candidate finder, link judge and agree checker; `requirements-links`, `shared-through`, `attachments-in-checks`), claims and claim links (`fsd claims`), workflow files and test journeys (`workflows`, `workflows tests`), the disprover with `disprove-small`, `disprove-callers`, `page-gap-strict` (`fsd disprove …`), `fsd undocumented`, the requirement writer, checker and judge (`req …`) and with them the parked ticket matcher (`ticket match`, `ticket-run.sh --match`; P5), and P8's `spec-ask`, `judge-finer-verdicts`, `group-sections`, `security-flag`; the trace commands that only rule on new links and findings (`trace approve`, `confirm`, `send-back`, `security`, `attribute`) and `fsd not-in-code`; their agents, schemas, script modes, tests and docs. **Kept:** the reference-page writer and verifier and the `spec` commands around them, `trace check`, `trace links` and `trace findings` (readers), `fsd import` and versions, `fsd review` (D341), table facts (D124), and tickets as layer 6 left them. Why: D321–D324 measured the source-reading route better on every number (26–29 of 30 against 24; 0–8% false alarms against 31.4%), so nothing new is linked; ticket points (`Points.cs`, page titles and FSD sections through trace.yaml) and the bundle still read the old pages and links, so cutting those would move layer 6's measured 36 of 40 with no way to re-run it before 2026-11-18. Turned down: cutting the pages and trace too (layer 6's numbers would rest on inputs gone); only the four switches (the dead route would still have to build, pass tests and be documented, D285's reason). Cost: two page sets side by side until a ticket run without the old pages shows points hold; old findings in trace.yaml can't be ruled on any more; git history keeps the code. **Built 2026-11-15** (my calls on the owner's standing word): `trace approve` and `trace attribute` kept as well, since they act on the frozen file and table facts; trace.yaml's writer moved to `Trace/TraceStore.cs`, the candidate finder's name words to `Fsd/NameWords.cs` (ticket points read them); `spec needed` keeps only `--ticket`; `fsd promote` carries trace entries only; `fsd status`'s next step for an unreviewed document is `fsd-review-run.sh`; old findings read `unverified` (the disprover's send-back labels went with it) and a person settles them by `decision`; a cut component or `fsd:`/`spec:` key in stangent.yaml is refused by name. About 15,500 lines removed; `dotnet build` 0 warnings, `dotnet test` 1861 of 1861. Fresh check (D158, Sonnet, ~146K tokens): no runtime break; fixed its stale messages naming cut commands, two benchmark configs with cut keys, a frozen claim link quoting an attachment now checked against the attachment, and three tests added (a decision kept on a re-written entry, a vanished decided link dropping on the module, cut config keys named). Left: `TraceStore.Entry`'s attachment-row stamping has no caller that fills attachments now, and `NameWords` is tested only through ticket points.
- D341 · Layer 5 becomes a command before layer 7 cuts anything: `stangent fsd review`, the measured source-reading agent with code's checks (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, build it first", after learning that only the old `/stangent:fsd-link` runs and layer 7 would cut its route; the design is my call on the owner's standing word). **(1) `stangent fsd review <doc> [--run N]`** (prepare, as D60): writes `.stangent/fsd/<doc>/review/run-N/input.md`: D321's prompt made generic (no app name, three kinds: gap, conflict, undocumented, the "undocumented" rule as D321 words it), the current version's sections as Markdown with `[section id: X]` headings, `fsd.scope`'s route prefixes, and the output schema `fsd-review-output.v1.json` (findings: state, section, fsdQuote, screen, note, evidence[`path:line`]); `next` names the agent `stangent-fsd-reviewer` with read-only limits as the file finder's (Read, Grep, Glob in a throwaway worktree of HEAD; never `specs/`, `.stangent/`, `stangent.yaml`), capped by `fsd.reviewBudget` (default 60 turns, $3; D321 used 105K tokens, Kavita 205–242K). **(2) `stangent fsd review-check <doc> --run N`**: the checks of `benchmark/fsd-agent/check_output.py` without its checklist, in C#: a known state; the section exists in the reviewed version (null only for an undocumented screen); a gap's or conflict's quote word for word inside its section (whitespace and Markdown marks ignored); every evidence path exists at the reviewed commit with its line inside the file; an undocumented finding names a screen or a section. One retry with the errors (D60); after that a finding that still fails is dropped and listed with its reason, never shown as a finding. **(3) `stangent fsd review-merge <doc>`**: the checked runs (`fsd.reviewRuns`, default 2, D324: two merged gave 28–29 of 30) unioned; exact duplicates joined (same state, section and quote, or for undocumented the same screen, or the same section with an evidence file in common), each finding saying which runs found it; writes `specs/fsd/<doc>/review.json` (contract `fsd-review` v1) and `review.md` by section (gaps, conflicts, left out), headed by the commit, FSD version, model, effort, runs and tokens. **(4)** The plugin: agent `stangent-fsd-reviewer.md`, driver `plugin/scripts/fsd-review-run.sh` (both runs side by side, as `ticket-run.sh`'s finder), command `/stangent:fsd-review`. **(5) Checked first for free:** the saved Kavita runs (`benchmark/results/2026-11-15-kavita-fsd/alone*/findings.json`) through `review-check` and `review-merge`, the merge scored by `--score-fsd` on the frozen key (expected 28–29 of 30, as D324); then one live run. `/stangent:fsd-link` stays until layer 7 cuts it. Why: D321–D324 measured this route better than the old linking on every number (26–29 of 30 against 24; 0–8% false alarms against 31%; 100–250K tokens against about $19), but it ran only as benchmark scripts. Cost: findings rest on an AI's reading of the code, kept honest only by the quote and line checks (they don't show a conflict is real; D322's checker found 0 of 20 false after the quote rule); no findings-to-decisions flow yet (D62's `trace.yaml` decisions stay with the old route); two runs cost about twice one **Built 2026-11-15** (`src/Stangent.Spec/Fsd/FsdReview.cs`, `docs/spec.md` "The FSD review"; my calls on the owner's standing word): as above, with these changes. (a) **Merge narrowed:** "the same section with an evidence file in common" joined different differences on Kavita's saved runs (3 of its 10 file joins: a layout choice with other reader settings, two preference switches crossed), and no two runs ever cited the same line, so an undocumented finding in a section is joined only when the pair is unambiguous (same section, routes equal or one blank, an evidence file in common, each the other's only such finding), at most one finding per run; anything else stays apart, marked `maybeSameAs` for the reader (a near-duplicate costs a glance, a wrong join loses a finding). Gap and conflict joins on the same quote: 20 of 20 right. The looser rule scored the same 29, so this fixes the record, not the score. (b) **Quote check:** D53's rule (4 words or a whole line), from a word's start to its end, with emphasis marks deleted, curly quotes and long dashes folded, and table bars and list markers ignored where that makes it match. (c) The run's stage moves through the state module (`fsd-review-run`: prepared → retry → checked | failed, D114). A failed run records nothing and is prepared again; a second check while the retry waits with no new answer is refused (`no_output`). (d) `fsd review-status` (read only) gives the driver the run count before its cost guard; the driver's worktree is made at the commit the run was prepared on. Schema errors name the field (`SchemaRegistry.ValidateDeep`). **Free check (5) done:** the saved `alone/` and `alone2/` findings through `review-check` and `review-merge`, scored on the frozen key: **29 of 30** (gap 10, conflict 10, undocumented 9, missed U9; 29 as their kind), 51 findings, 22 unplanted (not hand-checked), 0 dropped, no retry (`benchmark/results/2026-11-15-fsd-review-free/`). The checks never fired on real output, because these runs passed them all; the unit tests show they reject. **Fresh check (D158) done** (Sonnet, ~158K tokens, no AI calls): no lost finding, wrong join or path trick; 8 general defects (false rejects on emphasis before punctuation, curly quotes and quotes across list items, a quote starting mid-word accepted, schema errors that didn't name the field, a failed run read as current, a second check killing a pending retry, the driver preparing run 1 before its cost guard) all fixed as (b)–(d), free check re-run: unchanged. Open: whether Claude Code's Read deny rules also stop Grep and Glob in `specs/` (the file finder's design too); the live run, paid, on the owner's word
- D340 · Layer 6 (tickets) closes on what Phase 3 built, changed only where a measurement said so (my call on the owner's standing word, 2026-11-15: "iterate until we finish the layer … ur discretion"). **Kept:** the agent file finder for starting points (D338); the bundle's impact answer, and now `/stangent:ticket` step 5 telling the developer's session to run `stangent graph impact` on each file it means to change, with that command allowed (D339's pass); the bug proof as advice only (D79's preset after its bars failed); drafts as `draft-output` v2, where an AI words parts and code checks every claim, link and quote (grounded 50/50 and 30/30 after D92). **Already done:** P5, the requirement matcher runs only with `ticket-run.sh --match`. **Not built:** P7's comment templates, since code already checks that no fact in a draft moved and the drafts pass, so templates would be refinement for its own sake; the bundle's cut order (impact lists go first over `tickets.bundleChars`), since after D101 no Sonarr bundle reached the cap. **Left owed:** the proof re-run (D109), which needs headless Claude Code (weekly limit until 2026-11-18) and the app builds; the 2026-11-02 question "does the bundle help a fix or skew it?", which needs a fix agent and tests run; bitwarden's held-out 24 for D339. The bundle's reference pages and FSD sections come only from the old 2a/2c route (set aside by D322) and are absent where a repo has none. Cost: layer 6's one measured gain is small and on one public repo; the proof keeps its failed bars (7 of 10 not-bugs "reproduced")
- D339 · Layer 6 opens with D320's side-by-side on one job, "name every file the fix changes", before anything of it is built (my call on the owner's standing word, 2026-11-15; first step of layer 6, D337). **Tasks:** Sonarr's 40 bugs (`benchmark/tickets/sonarr`), and bitwarden's 24 held out (D100) if its graph builds at their commits; **the key is the developer's own fix commit**, no AI-written key: the files it changes that exist at the fix's parent (files it creates reported apart). **Side A:** `benchmark/tickets/agent_baseline.py` as run on 2026-11-02 (Sonnet 5.5, the ticket's text, the code at the fix's parent by `git archive`, Read/Grep/Glob, 30 turns, $1, up to 10 files), run again fresh. **Side B:** the same, plus one tool: `stangent graph impact` on a graph built at that same parent (so no fix leaks in), with one line saying it lists who else runs the code it names. **Scored by code:** share of the fix's files named (all bugs, and the multi-file fixes apart: the agent named 52% of Sonarr's 21), wrong files per ticket, tokens. **Reading, frozen now:** B passes if, on the multi-file fixes, it names at least 5 points more of the fix with at most 1 more wrong file per ticket, or the same share for 10% fewer tokens; otherwise `graph impact` earns no place in the ticket bundle for this job, and the 2026-11-02 question "does the bundle help a fix?" (`questions.md`) is not run on its account. Why this job: it is the one place the agent visibly falls short (half of a multi-file fix), it is what `graph impact` is for (D319: it named the callers a page missed), and the key comes from history, not from an AI. Cost: about $5–10 of agent runs (the 2026-11-02 rate was $0.05 a ticket; side B takes more turns); graph builds at up to 64 commits, free but hours of machine time; the paid run is asked for first. Limits: both repos public, so the model may know them; one model; impact at file level only. **Set up 2026-11-15 (free):** graphs at all of Sonarr's fix parents (`benchmark/tickets/parent_graphs.sh`, about 55 s each), side B as an option of `agent_baseline.py`, scorer `score_d339.py`; the saved 2026-11-02 baseline scores 58.2% of the multi-file fixes' existing files, so B needs about 63%; headless Claude Code (both the Homebrew 2.1.292 and the desktop app's bundled 2.1.325) answers "weekly limit" on every model. **Amended before any run (2026-11-15; owner: "just go ahead and run … its ur discretion"):** both sides run as Sonnet subagents of the session (Explore type), the same prompt with the ticket in a file beside the export, one fresh `git archive` export per ticket and side (`benchmark/tickets/d339_prepare.py`, side B's with the graph, a 3-line `stangent.yaml` and a `.bin/stangent` wrapper); "Read, Grep and Glob only, inside the export" (and for B "Bash for that one command only") is an instruction now, not enforced as the runner did, and is checked afterwards from the transcripts where they're kept; no 30-turn or $1 cap. Only the 21 multi-file fixes run, since the reading is decided on them; the token test is on the same 21. Cost: about 1.5–2.5M Sonnet tokens of the session's own usage (`benchmark/results/2026-11-15-d339-impact/`). **Result (2026-11-15): pass, fragile.** On the 20 scored multi-file fixes, side A named 54.2% of each fix (whole fix 3), side B 62.3% (whole fix 8): +8.2 points; wrong files 2.05 → 2.45 a bug (0.40 more; 2.14 → 2.57 counting SONB-32, all its files created by the fix); tokens +16% (5.68M → 6.61M, cache reads included); any fix file 19 of 21 on both. B did better on 5 bugs (SONB-1, 14, 17, 31, 39: all two-file fixes where impact pointed at the second file, e.g. the V5 copy of a V3 API resource, a service's other caller) and worse on 4 (SONB-2, 3, 6, 38: fixes of 3–9 files, where it named fewer); one run each, and side A's rerun moved 4 points against its 2026-11-02 run, so the margin is about twice the noise. No reads outside an export; side B used Bash for plain grep/ls/find inside its export 27 times (no information side A lacked). Fresh check (D61, Sonnet): every answer matches its transcript, scores recomputed, isolation and 3 seeded key entries confirmed; its one note, wrong files averaged over 21 instead of 20, is shown above. What follows: `/stangent:ticket` tells the developer's session to run `graph impact` on each file it means to change (D340)

- D336 · Inventory step 3's fresh check (D158) and its one fix round (2026-11-15; my calls on the owner's standing word). - D335 · D334's skip list narrowed after the first run on the three clones (2026-11-15; my call on the owner's standing word). (2) **Bare next, previous, prev, first, last are dropped** from the skip words; the "… page" forms stay.
