- D334 · Calls made before building D328's inventory (inventory step 3, 2026-11-15; my calls on the owner's standing word, written before anything runs, against tuning as D330 asks). (1) **A part** is a frontend component with its template, or an MVC view, partial or view component, that holds at least one line; parts with no lines are walked through, never listed. **A screen reaches a part** through `renders` lines from its routed component (or its action's view), and through **dialogs opened from code**: a method of a reached component, or a frontend method it reaches through `calls` lines within 4 hops, holding a `uses` line to another component that it does not render (Kavita: a card menu's click → `ActionService.editReadingList` → `uses EditReadingListModalComponent`). A dialog's own `renders` belong to the dialog. (2) **Data menus** (`menu-item` facts with `via: data`, one menu per `menu` key) belong to the method that builds them; a screen reaches the menu when one of its components' methods reaches that method by the same walk; the menu is titled by that method's name said readable (`getSeriesActions` → "Series Actions", marked `code`, since code holds no title for it). Menu entries with an address (navigation, D221) are lines of the part they're written in. (3) **Placement (D328):** a part rendered by exactly one other part is folded into it, recursively; dialogs and data menus are never folded (they keep their own title, D327: the part's top heading or titled element from its `screen-title` facts, else its class name said readable, marked `code`). A part or dialog reached from one screen is listed under it (the screen's own section, or inside the dialog it sits in); from two or more screens under "Shared parts" with "appears on" and a pointer line on each screen; from none under "Not placed". (4) **Server-defined fields:** one carrying a display-name attribute (`DisplayName`, `Display`, any `…DisplayName`) labels a model property for a view's own field, so it shows only through a view field bound to it (merged into that line); one no field binds is set aside with reason `unbound-model-label` (a label, not a control; counted and printed, nopCommerce's step 1 open item). Any other (a `Label =` argument, Sonarr's `FieldDefinition`) is drawn by a generic form from the server's list: listed per class under "Not placed" ("Newznab Settings, 12 fields"), as D329 expected. (5) **D326's skip list, fixed now for every repo**, on buttons and menu entries: visible words (trimmed, ignoring case and a trailing `…`/`:`) equal to close, cancel, back, go back, dismiss, ×, expand, collapse, expand all, collapse all, show more, show less, read more, read less, see more, see less, next, previous, prev, first, last, next page, previous page, first page, last page, select all, deselect all, unselect all; or the handler's short name, after taking off a leading `on`/`handle` and a trailing `Click`/`Press`/`Clicked`/`Modal`/`Dialog`, equal (ignoring case) to close, cancel, dismiss, back, goBack, expand, collapse, toggleExpand, toggleCollapse, sort, sortBy, changeSort, updateSort, pageChange, changePage, nextPage, previousPage, prevPage, firstPage, lastPage, setPageSize, pageSizeChange, selectAll, toggleAll, deselectAll, showMore, showLess. On fields: label words equal to page size, items per page, per page, or a select-all box (label select all, or bound to `selectAll`). Each skipped line keeps its reason (`close`, `table`, `expand`) in `inventory.json`'s `skipped`, never in the `.md`. Filters, search and view modes stay in. (6) **Lines** hold kind (`button`, `menu-entry`, `field`, `server-field`), the D327 name or ❓ with why, help, choices (names, or why not listed), `file:line`, code name, handler point and part; `.md` per screen as D328, counts at the top (screens, lines by kind, share named by visible text, ❓, skipped by reason, set aside, Not placed). (7) **`inventory check`:** the files byte for byte what the graph builds now; every ❓ says why; every skip reason on the fixed list; every control fact (`ui-event` button, `form-field`, `menu-item`) outside test code is exactly one line, skipped line or set-aside line (complete by construction). Exit 1 `inventory_check_failed`. Rejected: dialogs placed by name (`*Modal*`, per-app); an unlimited call walk (a service method called from everywhere would place one dialog on every screen); listing unbound model labels as controls. Cost: the 4-hop walk may still miss a dialog opened through an event bus or a router outlet (it lands in "Not placed", counted); a menu titled by its method name is a code name the PM may not recognise; the skip words are English only.
- D333 · Calls made building D327's label lookup (inventory step 2, 2026-11-15; my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) **One lookup, `Labels` in `src/Stangent.Flows`**, over D176/D228's translation reader with .NET resource XML always on (before, flows read JSON only, so nopCommerce's `@T` buttons were named by code): English JSON first (flat or nested, nested keys joined by dots, which is how Kavita's prefixed transloco keys are found as written), then resource XML ignoring case, in path order, first file wins; `|`-joined keys are alternatives. Layer 3's click names and screen names use it, so they and the inventory never disagree (D327 item 4). (2) **Screen titles are layer 2 facts, `screen-title`, added to code-graph v6 without a new version**: v6 was made today on this unpushed branch, the addition only adds a fact kind and a field, and nothing outside layer 3 reads v6 yet; a bump would mark every consumer twice for one day's work. Rules, general: a route's `title` (or `title` under `data`/`handle`) when constant; a screen component's outermost element that wraps other elements and carries a `title`, within four levels, never a button, link, control, label, span or icon (their title is a tooltip); its first highest-level heading `h1`–`h6` with words; in a Razor view, a `ViewBag`/`ViewData` slot whose name holds "Title" given a localizer key (ASP.NET's own `ViewData["Title"]`), then its top heading. Layer 3 picks route, then titled element or title slot, then headings by level. (3) **Razor `asp-for` fields join their label by the compiler's property** (a new `property` field on the Razor `form-field`, the documentation id of the property the lambda ends at, at its declaring type), not by the model type plus the path's text: 666 of nopCommerce's 2,795 `asp-for` paths go through another property (`Locales[item].Name`), and the graph doesn't hold property types to walk them. (4) **Help beside a label key** by the usual suffixes `.Hint`, `Hint`, `HelpText`, `.HelpText`, only when the field gives no help of its own and a file holds the key: nopCommerce's label tag helper shows `<key>.Hint`, Sonarr's provider fields' `…HelpText` keys sit beside their labels. (5) **"Reads as a key"** (one token joined by dots, dashes or underscores, or capitalised words run together) is tested only where a value may be either words or a key and no file holds it: a route's `title` and a constant list's item; such a value is ❓, not shown as words. (6) **What the shares count:** buttons are click and submit events (`click`, `submit`, `onClick`, `onSubmit`, `onPress`, double-click), not change events or a modal's callbacks; fields are client and Razor fields outside a wrapper's own template; server-defined fields apart; choices count each field with an enum or a constant list once, named only when every choice is; screens are pages plus MVC GET actions returning a view. Words written in markup count only when they hold a letter (a lone "/" or "×" doesn't). Reported by `stangent flows labels`. (7) **A flow keeps its code name when no words are found** (a flow needs a name; the inventory shows "❓ name: no visible text found"); screen-opens flows are named "Title (route)", ids stay the route's, so no flow file moves. (8) **TypeScript enums' members have no label fact**: their choices are ❓ "the members of X (no label on them)" (Kavita shows them through pipes at runtime); listing member names was rejected, since they are code names. (9) **A key no file holds that reads as words is shown as written** (`Language Profiles`, `URL` on three Sonarr provider fields): translation libraries show a missing key as written, so the user sees it; one that reads as a key stays ❓ with the key named. (10) **A screen component that wraps a body** (Sonarr's `SeriesIndex` renders providers around `SeriesIndexBody`, which holds `<PageContent title>`): a titled wrapping element in what it renders, up to two levels down, ranks after its own; headings there are a part's, not the screen's, and don't count. (11) **A handler handed to a component's prop** (`<LogFiles onRefreshPress={handleRefreshPress}>`) takes the words of that component's own button run by the prop (`onPress={onRefreshPress}`, labelled there), the component found among what the owner renders by name, two levels at most. (12) **A Razor button with no words of its own takes its `aria-label` or `title`** (layer 2, as the TypeScript side already did): nopCommerce's dialog close buttons. **After the fresh check (Sonnet, ~93K tokens; of ~27 named lines 21 right, 2 weak, 4 wrong from 4 causes; of ~21 ❓ lines 12 right, 9 missed), its general faults fixed:** (13) a translation value that is only a reference to another key (`"cancel": "{{common.cancel}}"`, 657 such values in Kavita's file) is that key's words, three hops at most, and a reference to a key no file holds is no words (Kavita's `{{common.copy}}`: the user sees the braces); (14) a placeholder option with no letter or digit (`---`) is no choice, and a list of only placeholders is "made at runtime"; (15) options drawn through the field's own `<ng-template>` (`{{ rd | writingStyle }}`) are made at runtime, not the raw values (layer 2); (16) a heading written in branches gives several keys, and the words without a placeholder (`{0}`) are taken (nopCommerce's blog list: "Blog", not "Blog posts of '{0}'"); (17) a handler list gives the first handler's short name, not the whole id. Left, with reasons: a label written as a plain `<div>` above a field isn't joined (D332's tag rule, a known cost); words that pipes or code make at runtime stay ❓; an icon button named by its `aria-label` shows words a screen reader says, not printed ones (kept: the nearest words a user meets); nopCommerce's enum labels by the `Enums.<Type>.<Member>` key convention, like its plugins' install-code strings, are one app's framework pattern and not read. Rejected: reading nopCommerce plugins' strings from their C# install code (`["Plugins.…"] = "…"` given to a resource-adding call; most of what nopCommerce leaves ❓), a pattern of this one app. **Why:** each rule is the general form of what the three clones show and names no app. **Cost:** a title set at runtime (detail screens, readers) stays ❓; a page whose outermost titled element is a tooltip-bearing `<div>` would take the tooltip as its title; the help-suffix rule could pick a key that exists for another purpose; screens' ❓ count depends on the route list, which counts each parameterised route separately.
- D332 · Calls made building D329's layer 2 facts (inventory step 1, 2026-11-15; my calls on the owner's standing word). (1) **Which controls are `form-field` facts:** an element with Angular's form bindings or `asp-for` (any tag helper), a JSX element with `name=` and `onChange=`, a wrapper's use (below), and also **every native `<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>` that isn't hidden or a button, bound or not**, the same set D330's scanner counts; one inside a wrapper's own template is marked `inside: wrapper`, so step 3 lists the wrapper's uses and not its insides twice. (2) **D329's wrapper rule made exact:** a component whose every control takes one of its own inputs or props as given (`value`, `props.value`, `value()`) into `value`/`checked`/`ngModel`/`formControl`/`formField`, or that passes its props on by name or spread to another wrapper (found by repeating until nothing changes), or an Angular value accessor (`writeValue` + `registerOnChange`). "Every control" and "as given" keep out a page that passes one prop into one field among others (the first test fixture caught exactly that). **Tightened after the fresh check** (Sonarr's `TablePager`, list rows such as `FilterBuilderRow`, Kavita's cards with a selection box had passed): the value must be two-way by the framework's own convention: Angular a `model()` or an input X beside an output `XChange`; React a prop of the control's own name (`value={value}`) or a spread of props. Also from that check: a conditional's condition and a comparison's literals aren't label or help words; an action entry's `handler` lists every handler-like property (Kavita's 88 all say `callback=this.dummyCallback`, their `action=Action.…` is what tells them apart); nopCommerce's display-name properties are server fields whether or not a view edits them (2,660, read-only grid models among them), so step 3 lists only those an `asp-for` field or a generic form reaches. (3) **Labels and help by a fixed order** (own attribute; a label element pointing at the id, or Razor's with the same `asp-for`; a label element just before it in the same parent, up to three levels up while that level holds only this field; an element around it holding no other field with a label attribute), with label and help elements known by a tag ending in `label` / `help`, `helptext`, `hint` (HTML's own `<label>`, Sonarr's `<FormLabel>`, nop's `<nop-label>`), the same tag rule the scanner uses. (4) **Choices:** `enum:Name` and `list:a|b` when code shows them; anything else is `runtime:<expression>`, because code can't tell a list from the server from one computed in the browser; C#'s `server:<value>` only where an attribute's options argument names a server-side provider (`SelectOptionsProviderAction`). (5) **Server-defined fields are a new detector, `form-fields`** (on by default, its own component switch): a `Label` argument or a `label` constructor parameter; a display-name attribute, known by deriving from `System.ComponentModel.DisplayNameAttribute` **or by a class name ending `DisplayNameAttribute`** (nopCommerce's `NopResourceDisplayNameAttribute` derives from plain `Attribute`); DataAnnotations' `[Display(Name = …)]`. Enum members with such an attribute (or an unset `Label`/`label` with a `Hint`) are `field-choice` facts. (6) **Transloco prefixes apply only to keys passed to `t()`**, not to the `transloco` pipe (which ignores the structural prefix); D221's menu reader prefixed both and now doesn't. (7) **Data action entries** (`menu-item` with a handler and no address): handler-like names `callback`, `onClick`, `command` (PrimeNG's menu item), `handler`, `onSelect`, `action`, the first present recorded with its line; help from `description`/`tooltip`/`hint`/`subtitle`/`helpText`; a list of three or more as D221 says, **or two when both are action entries** (Kavita's person and side-nav menus hold two; a name plus a handler is specific enough that two don't catch random arrays; one still doesn't count). (8) **Markup handed to a helper as a Razor template** (`@<div>…</div>`, compiled to `new HelperResult(…)`) is read as the view's own, at the helper call: nopCommerce's localized editors hold 175 of its fields there; Razor's `to` drops a leading `@Model.`. (9) **Razor buttons' `@T` keys were already facts** (D163's N1: keys from anything typed `…Localizer`; nopCommerce 870 of 989 `ui-event` facts carry one), so item 1's Razor half needed no change. **Why:** each is the general form of what the three clones show, and the scanner's own patterns decide what counts, so the D330 match measures one set. **Cost:** a page with one bare control and nothing else could still pass as a wrapper (a one-field component given its value is one, by the rule); tag-suffix label rules miss labels written as plain `<span>`/`<div>` text; `runtime` choices say nothing about where the list comes from.
- D331 · D325's gate runs on a new planted nopCommerce FSD (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, nopCommerce"). One admin area, the catalog (products, categories, manufacturers); written and frozen by a fresh Opus 5.5 agent told only that it writes a benchmark and nothing of D325–D330, as Kavita's was (`phases/2c-spec.md`, Kavita section: 30 planted items, 10 gaps, 10 conflicts, 10 left out; sha256 recorded); it is written only when the build reaches the gate, so no rule can be shaped after it exists, and nobody reads it before both sides have run. Both sides run on it: two fresh source-only Sonnet 5.5 runs with the Kavita "alone" prompt, merged, against the inventory plus its AI link step and the source agent's gaps and conflicts; scored by code on the frozen key; false ⚠ checked by a fresh Opus agent on a seeded sample. The pass bar stays as D325 froze it. Why: it is the third framework (Razor), the hardest case for ⚠ volume (2,627 admin lines in D330's scan), and nobody has seen an FSD for it; Kavita and Sonarr are ruled out because their FSDs and D324's four items are known here. Rejected: a new key on Kavita or Sonarr (tuned); a fourth repo (most honest, but a new clone and graph first). Cost, when run, each paid step asked for first: writer about 250K tokens, two source runs about 250–300K each, the link step no more than two agent runs, the false-⚠ check about 80K; about 1.0–1.3M in all. One area of one repo is still a small test.
- D330 · How the inventory's completeness is checked (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, as recommended"). (1) **A crude independent scanner**, `benchmark/inventory/scan.py`, outside stangent on purpose, finds candidate controls by plain text patterns that apply to every app (elements with a form binding or `asp-for`, JSX elements with `name=` and `onChange=`, event attributes and submit buttons, C# properties with a `Label =` argument, data objects with a name and a handler property); run before the build on all three clones for expected sizes. (2) **After the build every scanner hit is accounted for** by `file:line`, as an inventory line or one of D326's skip reasons: bar 95% or more per clone, unmatched hits printed for a person. (3) Printed beside it: the share named by visible text (D327) and the "Not placed" count (D328). (4) **On the owner's word, after the build:** a fresh Sonnet agent lists every control on three screens per clone picked by a fixed seed, from source (about 150–250K tokens), because the scanner and the extractor are written by the same hand and can share blind spots. Against tuning: patterns and D326's skip list are written before anything runs; planted keys and FSDs are not read; D324's four items are not used as test cases. Rejected: the agent sample alone (tokens every time, and three screens can't show a whole kind going missing, such as Sonarr's 485 server-defined fields). Cost: the scanner shares the author's blind spots, answered only by the sample; 95% is a floor picked before any run, not derived. **Pre-build scan (2026-11-15, free; `benchmark/results/2026-11-15-inventory-scan/`):** candidate lines (fields, actions, server-defined fields, data-written menu actions; a rough guess at D326's skip list taken off): Sonarr 1,228 (366 fields, 393 actions, 469 server fields), Kavita 879 (333 fields, 451 actions, 95 menu entries as data), nopCommerce 3,634 (admin 2,627, plugins 623, public 384). Already in the graph as facts, by `file:line`: actions 381 of 393 (Sonarr), 447 of 451 (Kavita), 703 of 735 (nopCommerce); fields 1, 48 and 12; server fields and data menu entries none. So buttons are nearly all seen today; the build's work is fields, server-defined fields, data menus and labels. Size against D325's guess: line by line the ⚠ count would be in the hundreds on any FSD of a few thousand words; D328's grouping and the FSD's scope are what bring it down, untested so far.
- D329 · Where the inventory is built (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, as recommended"). (1) **Layer 2 records what's on the page** (contract `code-graph` v5 → v6): `ui-event` keys get the template's transloco `prefix`/`read` as `menu-item` already does (`tools/ts-extractor/src/menu.ts`, D221), and Razor buttons their `@T("…")` key; a new `form-field` fact holds what the field is bound to (`formControlName`, `[formField]`, `[(ngModel)]`, React `name=`, Razor `asp-for`), its label key, help key and choices (an enum, a constant list, or "from the server"); wrapper components count as fields by a general rule (a component whose own template binds an input to a value it receives), never by name; C# properties carrying a `Label =` argument or a display-name attribute are server-defined fields (Sonarr `[FieldDefinition(…, Label = …)]`, nopCommerce `[NopResourceDisplayName(…)]`); `menu-item` also takes data entries with a name and a handler-like property (`action`, `callback`, `onClick`) and no address (Kavita's action menus). (2) **Layer 3 puts the list together** in `src/Stangent.Flows`, which already walks screens and looks up translation keys: `specs/inventory.json` and a readable `.md`, commands `stangent inventory` and `stangent inventory check`, its own contract `inventory` v1, so later inventory changes don't ripple through flows; flows v5 → v6 for D327's click names. Consumers marked `needs review` when built: layer 4 (parked). Why: Kavita's graph already holds 1,120 `ui-event` facts with label keys (missing only the prefix: `keys: "save"`), and the menu reader already applies the prefix and reads menus as data, so most of the work is widening what exists. Rejected: a separate pass reading source again (Angular templates, JSX and Razor parsed twice, the lookup and the screen-to-part walk duplicated, every framework change fixed in two places); building it in layer 5 (the inventory is code-only and useful with no FSD, e.g. the per-release diff). Cost: two contract bumps; graphs rebuilt on the three clones (free); Sonarr's server-defined fields reach the screen through a generic form that asks the server for its field list, so code may not tie "Newznab's fields" to the Add Indexer dialog at first, and they would show in "Not placed".
- D328 · How inventory lines are grouped (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, as recommended"). (1) **One group per screen** (a route's screen; nopCommerce: a view a GET action returns), titled as D327: the screen's own fields and buttons first, then each dialog and menu it opens under its own title. (2) **A part reached from two or more screens is listed once** under "Shared parts" with "appears on: …" and the count; each of those screens gets one pointer line ("uses Series menu (14 actions)"). (3) **Parts no screen reaches** go in a "Not placed" group, never dropped, their count printed; that count is part of the completeness check (falling means extraction improved). (4) **⚠ is reported as lines and as groups**; a whole missing screen, dialog, menu or shared part stays one ⚠ with its contents folded (D325). Measured by code from the graphs (`renders` plus modal opens; `inventory-reach.py` in the session notes): Kavita 303 components, 171 on one screen, 90 on two or more, 42 on none (`CardActionablesComponent`, the card "…" menu, on 21 screens); Sonarr 648: 300, 186, 162. Why: repeating a shared part under every screen multiplies one missing menu entry into up to 21 ⚠, while an FSD usually describes such a menu once; dropping unplaced parts would make "complete by construction" false without anyone noticing. Rejected: repeating shared parts under each screen. Cost: the plain "two or more screens" rule moves some dialogs away from where a PM looks (an Edit dialog opened from a page and from a card menu lands in Shared parts), answered only by the pointer line; part of Sonarr's 162 unplaced components are building blocks with no controls, part are dialogs the graph doesn't place yet.
- D327 · How each inventory line is named (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, as recommended"). (1) **Lines** carry the words the user sees, looked up in the app's own translation files in each format: Angular transloco keys with the template's `prefix:` applied (Kavita: `t('auto-close-menu-label')` under `prefix:'manage-reading-profiles'` → "Auto Close Menu"), `translate('Key')` through the app's `en.json` (Sonarr `src/NzbDrone.Core/Localization/Core/en.json`, which also holds server-defined field labels such as `IndexerSettingsApiPath`), Razor `@T("…")` and `[NopResourceDisplayName("…")]` through the resource file (nopCommerce `defaultResources.nopres.xml`, 7,055 strings); help text rides along as a second column; a dropdown's choices by their visible names. (2) **Screens** by their title with the route beside ("All Series (`/all-series`)"), from route `title:` data (Kavita), `<PageContent title>` (Sonarr), the view's `<h1>@T(…)` (nopCommerce). (3) **No label found:** the line shows ❓ with the code name and why ("❓ saveName: no visible text found"); the build reports the share named by visible text per clone, a floor check of extraction, never measured against a planted key. (4) **Layer 3's click names use the same lookup**, so the two never disagree: flows contract v5 → v6, layer 4 (parked) `needs review` (D20). Why: today layer 3 names clicks by code name when it misses the label: Kavita 217 of 223 (`saveName`, `loadPage`; the transloco prefix isn't applied, 243 templates use one), Sonarr 56 of 116, nopCommerce many "cshtml form N" (`@T` ignored), and screens only by route; a PM, and the AI link step, can't match "loadPage" to an FSD sentence. Rejected: labels in the inventory only, which leaves two layers naming one button differently. Cost: the label lookup is the largest new extraction work in this design (Kavita's prefixes and nopCommerce's resource file each a few days with tests); a flows contract bump.
- D326 · What D325's inventory holds: every control a user can touch, whether or not it calls the server (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, as recommended"). (1) **Settings options:** every form field with its choices (switch, dropdown, text, number, tag and pattern fields), including fields the server defines and a generic form draws (Sonarr's 485 `[FieldDefinition(…)]` provider fields, e.g. Newznab's `Label = "IndexerSettingsApiPath"`) and fields inside the app's own wrapper components (Kavita's `app-setting-switch`/`app-setting-select` bound with `[formField]`, Sonarr's `FormInputGroup type={inputTypes.…}`, nopCommerce's `<nop-editor>`/`<nop-select>`). (2) **Actions:** every button and menu entry, including menus written as data rather than markup (Kavita's 94 entries in `_services/action-factory.service.ts`, e.g. "Mark as read"), and the clicks layer 3 drops today as "screen behaviour only" (Kavita 612, Sonarr 1,039). (3) **Left out, by one fixed list that applies to every repo and is never tuned per app:** dialog close, cancel and back; table mechanics (column sort, column chooser, page size, paging, select-all boxes); expand, collapse and "show more". Filters, search options and view modes stay in (Kavita's FSD has a section "Filtering series"). Why: D324's misses were settings options and a menu entry with no server call of its own, so "only controls that save or call the server" brings the gap back; an AI deciding line by line what a PM cares about is unchecked judgement (D18) of the kind that flickered. Cost: raw counts before the list, by grep with duplicates, about 1,000 lines on Kavita, 1,300 on Sonarr, 2,900 on nopCommerce, against FSDs of a few thousand words (Kavita's planted one about 2,000), so D325's guess of 10–40 ⚠ per FSD won't hold line by line; how ⚠ lines are grouped and counted is decided separately. Each app's own shapes (wrapper components, menus as data, server-defined fields) need extraction rules that are general, not named per repo; how is decided separately.
- D325 · (→ build stopped at step 3 and parked, D337, 2026-11-15) The PM's document is a code-made inventory of what the app offers the user, linked to the FSD, and layer 5 splits its work by what was stable (owner, 2026-11-15: "yeah i agree"; answers Q-L8; replaces D322's checklist). (1) **Inventory, by code:** every screen, action (buttons and menu entries, with or without a server call of their own) and settings option with its choices, per screen, from the source; complete by construction, the same every run, diffable between releases. (2) **Links, by AI, checked by code:** each line is linked to the FSD sentence that covers it, quoted word for word inside its section (as D322's check), or marked "⚠ not in the FSD". (3) **Wrong statements, by the source-reading agent:** gaps and conflicts, which it found 10 of 10 in every run (D324). (4) **The PM reads** the ⚠ lines and the agent's gap and conflict findings; "covered" lines stay for checking and for the release diff. The FSD's own scope is applied first, so a screen it leaves out on purpose is one line, and a whole missing screen is one ⚠ with its contents folded under it. **Gate (D320), on a fresh key nobody has looked at:** more "left out" items found than two merged agent runs (D324: 28–29 of 30 overall, 8 of 10 left out at best), false ⚠ counted, the ⚠ count reported (guess: 10–40 per FSD), cost no more than two agent runs. **Needs first:** layer 3 lists only screens and clicks that call the server; of D324's four unstable or missed items it holds one (the PDF reader screen), not settings options (Exclude Patterns, Blur Unread Summaries) or a menu entry with no server call (Remove from On Deck). Why: D321–D324: the agent reading source wins on accuracy, and is unstable only on "left out", which a complete list made by code answers; a PM reading the ⚠ lines answers the owner's pivot (graph, flows and pages linked to the FSD, read by a PM). Cost: new extraction work in layer 3 (options, menus) before anything can be tested; the link step is AI judgement where Sonarr's run 2 went wrong, kept honest only by the quote check; a false ⚠ costs the PM a look; layer 4's page stays parked (D323), since the inventory is a different, shorter document.
- D324 · Before the owner's pivot is decided, the source-only agent's stability is measured (owner, 2026-11-15: "yes we will make PM read the documentation, the one after we link and md we create later … sure lets run it"). The pivot: keep the graph, flows and pages, link them to the FSD, write business documentation from them, and report discrepancies, for a PM to read. Its discrepancy half is the route D321 already measured as worse than an agent reading the source (24 of 30 and 31.4% against 26 and 7.8%; Kavita 28 and 2.7%), so the pivot can only earn its place on what code gives and an agent may not: the same, complete answer every run, and a document that changes only when the code does (diffable per release). Test: the Kavita "alone" prompt word for word, two more fresh Sonnet 5.5 runs on the frozen key; planted items per run and "flicker" (found by some runs, not all), by code. Reading, frozen (`fsd-kavita/stability-method.md` in the session notes): every run at least 27 and flicker at most 2, stable, the business document has no job on these grounds; any run 26 or less or flicker 3 or more, unstable, a code-made base document has a job; otherwise talked through. Recorded now: the reader of any business document is the PM (owner); what it holds and whether it is built wait for this result. Cost: about 400K tokens, no false-alarm checks; three runs on one AI-written key. **Result (2026-11-15): unstable.** Runs found 28, 28 and 26 (gaps and conflicts 10 of 10 every time); flicker 3 (U3 the PDF reader screen, U5 and U10 settings options), U9 (a menu action) never found; merging two runs gives 28 or 29; tokens 205K, 242K, 205K. All the instability is in "left out", and layer 3 lists only one of those four (the screen). Detail: `phases/2c-spec.md`, last section.
- D323 · Layer 4 is parked, not cut (owner, 2026-11-15: "idw cut rule, park is better, but i wanna know if this is useful in the future"; answers Q-L6; D138's cut rule is set aside for layer 4 by the owner). Parked means: the code (`AreaPages`, `PageChart`, `PageElsewhere`, `PageGate`, `PageSummary`), the `pages` commands and their tests stay and keep passing; `components.pages` stays off by default; no gate runs, fixes or new rules; the `page.md` files in the bench clones stay; D280's gate 3 is recorded as not passed (task test 17 and 21 of 27, D318, D319). It comes back only when one of these shows it helps, each cheap and each run only on the owner's word: (1) after D322's checklist test, a variant whose checklist is `pages summary`'s items per click (saves, checks, exits with their conditions) instead of layer 3's bare click list, on the same planted key (several of D321's conflicts are checks, e.g. the tag label rule); (2) when a much larger repo is in the benchmark, D318's task test there with source alone against source plus page, where reading the source should get expensive; (3) the owner opening a page before the code on a real ticket and saying it helped; (4) layer 6 (PR review) designed to show what a change does to a click, by diffing a page between two commits. - D322 · Layer 5 (checking an FSD against the code) is built on an agent that reads the source and does the comparing; Stangent gives it tools it calls and code checks on what it returns, not pre-written reading material (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, that premise"; answers Q-L7). Tools: `graph impact`, and each screen's routes and clicks from layer 3's flows, given as a checklist the agent must answer item by item ("does the FSD cover this click?"). Checks, by code (D18): every FSD quote word for word, every `file:line` real, every checklist item answered. Reference pages, `page.md` and `pages summary` are no longer layer 5's input. First step, before anything else of layer 5 is built: D320's side-by-side on the planted Sonarr key, the agent alone (D321: 26 of 30, 7.8% false alarms) against the same agent with the checklist and the checks; the checklist stays only if it finds more (D321's four misses were all clicks on screens the FSD covers: Add New's cutoff-search option, manual import from the queue, unmonitor selected missing, mark as failed) without raising false alarms past the tolerance D320 sets. Why: D321, where an agent with only the source beat the old page-based linking on every number at a fraction of the cost; and the owner's own instinct of 2026-11-05 ("lets directly compared to the parse fsd and the code"). The agent marked three listed clicks "covered" that the FSD never names; "covered" was the one answer code didn't check. **One fix attempt (owner: "Yes, one rerun"):** "covered" must quote, word for word, the FSD sentence inside its section that describes the action, checked by code: 29 of 30 (undocumented 9 of 10, only a form option missed), 0% false alarms (20 of 20 sampled real, Opus checker), 101K tokens. By the frozen reading the checklist stays, with quoted "covered". Partly tuned to this key (the cause was read from run 2's answers on its items), so it counts as shown only on a key nobody has looked at. **Unseen key, Kavita (owner: "Yes, confirm it"):** a fresh Opus agent wrote and froze a planted Kavita FSD and key; the reviewer alone found 28 of 30 with 2.7% false alarms (205K tokens), the reviewer with the checklist 26 (201K): **not confirmed**, Sonarr's 29 was tuning; the checklist goes back to the owner. Detail: `phases/2c-spec.md`, last three sections.
- D321 · Before layer 5 is designed, one source-only agent runs on the planted Sonarr FSD and is scored on its frozen 30-item key beside the old linking pipeline (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, test first"). Layers 1–3 were only ever checked for accuracy, the October plan to compare agents with and without the graph (1d part 2) never ran, and layer 4's task test, the one usefulness check, tied the source. Layer 5's job (checking an FSD against code) is where the lower layers should matter most, and a ready test exists. Method frozen before the run (`fsd-source-only/method.md` in the session notes): the agent gets the FSD text with its section ids, the scope's route prefixes and the clone at the pinned commit, nothing of `specs/`; its findings are scored by the existing `--score-fsd` and the key's frozen matching rule; up to 20 unplanted findings (seed 42) go to a fresh Opus 5.5 checker for false alarms. The old pipeline: 24 of 30 (undocumented 4 of 10), false alarms 31.4%. Reading: fewer than 24 found or more than 31.4% false alarms, the old layers earn their place on this job; 27 or more at or below 31.4%, the pipeline's design is rethought before layer 5; in between, a tie or better for the source agent, reported as such. Cost: about 260–470K tokens (agent 200–350K, checker 60–120K); one run; the FSD and key are AI-written and Sonarr is public. My calls (owner delegated by standing word): the agent runs on Sonnet 5.5, the model of the old pipeline's judges, so a difference is the layers' and not the model's; the checker on Opus 5.5. **Result (2026-11-15):** the source agent found 26 of 30 (gap 10, conflict 10, undocumented 6; 25 as the planted kind) with 7.8% false alarms (3 false of 20 sampled), for 105K tokens plus an 81K check; the old pipeline 24 (20 as kind) with 31.4%, about $19 a run plus its 718 AI-written pages. By the frozen reading: in between, a tie or better for the source agent, which did better on every number but is not at 27. Detail and limits: `phases/2c-spec.md`, last section; files `benchmark/results/2026-11-15-source-only-fsd/`.
- D320 · From layer 5 on, every rebuild layer's gate includes a side-by-side on that layer's own job against an agent that has only the source (owner, 2026-11-15: "Yes, make it a rule"). The same tasks, frozen before the run, go to two fresh agents: one with the source alone, one with the source plus the layer's output (and the outputs of the layers it is built on). A third fresh agent, not told which answer came from which, marks both against a key written from source. Right answers and tokens are recorded side by side. The layer passes only if it beats the source-only agent on right answers or on tokens and is no worse on the other (tokens within 10%). Accuracy gates, like D280's gates 1 and 2, stay as a floor, not as proof of use. Layers 2 and 3 are not re-tested now: layer 5's side-by-side tests them through what layer 5 reads. Why: every rebuild gate so far checked that a layer is accurate. The first one that asked whether it helps (layer 4's task test, D318, D319) found the page plus `graph impact` (21 of 27, 166K tokens) no better than the agent that wrote the key from source (189K), so accuracy gates alone can pass a layer nobody needs, and layers 5–7 would be tuned on top of it. Cost: about one more agent per gate (150–200K tokens on Opus 5.5) plus a grader marking both; a layer can fail a bar it would have passed on accuracy; the three clones are mid-sized, which favours the source-only agent, so a result says little about large repos unless one is in the test. My calls (owner delegated by standing word): the source-only agent is a separate fresh agent, never the key writer, which in D319 was not marked blind; "no worse" on tokens means within 10%; layer 1 (foundation) has no job of its own to compare and is exempt. Answers the gate part of Q-L6; what happens to layer 4's page is still open there.

- D316 · After gate 3 run 7 (D315, three readers: Add New 3 yes, pass; Kavita Reading Lists 3 partly, fail; Sonarr Series pair 3 partly, two leaning yes, fail; `layer4-build/gate3-run7-{a,b,c}.md`), the causes all three readers name: (1) layer 3: a screen-open walk follows a request declared by a mutation hook (React Query `useMutation`/`useApiMutation`, the step's condition case `useMutation`) as if sent on load; it is sent only when a click calls `mutate`, and the click flows already reach it that way. Sonarr's `/series/:titleSlug` screen flow has 12 such of its 31 requests (PUT and DELETE series, DELETE episodeFile, POST manualimport, POST seasonPass, POST history/failed …), which is why it is 1,231 steps and its changes list holds other clicks' work. The on-load walk stops at a mutation declaration (one line: "declares PUT /api/v5/series, sent by clicks"); a flows contract bump (D20); (2) pages: an area's page gets a short "Elsewhere" list: the other endpoints of the backend controllers its flows land on, each with the areas and start flows that call them (Kavita Reading Lists: create, delete and add-to-list live on other areas' pages, which all three readers missed); (3) check Kavita removeItem's K rows for `UpdateReadingListPosition` (named wrong by runs 4, 6 and 7): fix if they're another endpoint's checks, otherwise leave. Not taken: lowering D312's small-folder threshold to give Series its own Search part (tuning to one repo). Owner delegated (2026-11-14, "keep going until layer 4 is done"): my call. Cost: one Opus build (~250K), gate 2 (~300K) and gate 3 with three readers (~330K). Built 2026-11-14 (`docs/flows.md` v5, `docs/pages.md`, the assistant's calls while building): (a) the rule is any walk that reaches the hook with no start on the way (a call of a value from a function's result such as `mutate(…)`, a hook's handed-back function calling `mutate`), not only on-load walks: a click rendering a modal whose hooks declare saves doesn't send them either; on-load code that does call `mutate` (in a `useEffect`) is followed; (b) a mutation's own function (`mutationFn`, `onSuccess`) and a runtime-URL request on the hook's line are cut the same way; (c) shared code entered with no start, whose walk followed a mutation only because its entry started one, is walked apart as `<id>--declared`; (d) "Elsewhere" sits after the parts list, lists controllers the area's own requests (or MVC screen actions) land on, not those reached only through shared code (Kavita Reading Lists would otherwise list `LibraryController`'s 22 endpoints), groups endpoints with the same callers on one line and the uncalled on one line, 15 per list then `+N more`. Found: Kavita's create and add-to-list are called by no start flow (menu callbacks left for later, D306); removeItem's K rows are right (`DeleteListItem` binds `UpdateReadingListPosition`), left; 16 Sonarr provider-settings endpoints were reached only through the wrong screen walks (their Save buttons are no `ui-event`), now "no start flow calls".

- D312 · Oversized areas split by deeper frontend folder (owner, 2026-11-14: "Yes, split them", relayed by the parent session after gate 3 run 3's Series no, D311). An area over 40 start flows (D221) that has no menu children to split by is split by the subfolders of its frontend folders (Sonarr `Series` → `Series › Details`, `› Index`, `› Edit`, `› Delete`, `› Search` … from `frontend/src/Series`'s eight subfolders); `areas.yaml` is rewritten in all three clones; existing tie answers are kept (D234); any new tie goes back to the owner. Two conditions (parent session, recorded with the owner's yes): (1) a start flow with no frontend folder (jobs such as RefreshSeries and DiskScan, event handlers) never lands in whichever sub-area sorts first: it goes with the click that sets it off where the links show one, otherwise in one `<Area> › Background` sub-area; (2) gate 3 run 4 must not get easier by reading the smallest sub-area: in place of Sonarr Series the reader takes the Series parent/index page plus the sub-area holding the clicks run 3 named (Edit or Delete, Save, Monitor); Add New and Kavita Reading Lists stay. Cost: one build (~200–300K), gates 2 and 3 again (~410K). Built 2026-11-14 (`docs/areas.md`, the assistant's calls while building): a part gaining fewer than 3 start flows stays with the parent, so Series' Edit, Delete and MonitoringOptions clicks stay on Series (not the `› Edit`/`› Delete` parts named above); a subfolder whose menu screens sit on an existing part joins it (`AddSeries/ImportSeries` → Series › Library Import); one part taking all is no split (Kavita Customize stays large); a job goes to Background when the split area holds its majority but its own flows don't. Sonarr Series 78 → Series 9, › Details 7, › Index 11, › Background 47 (large); no new ties.

- D307 · The build of D306 (2026-11-14) made these calls D306 left open. (1) The cut: the TypeScript extractor treats a function assigned to a property (`action.callback = (a) => handler(a)`) as kept for a later call, and a parameter called only inside such a stored function as not called now, so Kavita's `wireBulkCallback` keeps the bulk handler and the selection clicks stop there; code in a stored function is kept even when a stream or listener inside it would mark it (the outermost keeping wins); a function stored on an event property (`reader.onload`, `request.onsuccess`) is a listener, walked with its "when", not kept (Kavita's profile image upload runs in `reader.onload`). The walk shows each keeper once per method, at the first kept line, as a step ending `waits` ("leaves code for later (wireBulkCallback): …"), a new end kind, so `flows` v2 → v3 and r4 `needs review` (D20); a click left with no server call is skipped as "screen behaviour only" and its reason names the keepers. Kavita's three selection clicks are now skipped (12 such skips in Kavita, none in Sonarr or nopCommerce); their three stale tie answers were removed from Kavita's `areas.yaml` so `areas check` passes. Graphs rebuilt with `--full` (extractor change). (2) A shared flow gets a part only when it brings a reaching flow something the flow showed before (D260's saves, pushes, files and plugin calls, D304(6)'s messages): one that only stops moves nothing, since no click showed shared code's stops. (3) The box reads "runs X (see its part): N changes, M stops", N every change it leads to (its own and in shared code it runs, each line once), M its own stops; with 3 or fewer changes it names them ("1 change (saves AppUser)"), because a 1-save repository update reached by three clicks was otherwise a part that hid what each click saves; one box per part per chart, with every step that runs it; a part whose changes are all framework changes (D281) is listed under "Changes made in shared code", not drawn. (4) Titles: rounds of handler/component method, then the start's code line, then a C# method's parameter types (Sonarr's three `EpisodeSearchService.Execute` jobs share their line), then the flow id; shared parts with the same name add the line, then the parameters. (5) D306(4)'s "identical" lines are the same words without "(in shared code X)" at the same line. Owner delegated (2026-11-14, "keep going until layer 4 is done"): my call. Cost: the parts show what was never on a page (shared code's own stops and outside calls), so Sonarr's pages grow 9.4M → 10.4M characters and charts over the cap rise 54 → 70 (39 flows, 31 shared parts) while Series falls 2.47M → 2.3M; nopCommerce gets 1,528 shared parts; a reader of a click with a big part must jump to it.
